It was removed from `NOTICE` only. `CONTRIBUTING.md` kept it verbatim — "(AGPL-3.0-or-later), the same license as upstream herdr" — a pre-base sentence (the warden's canonical blame places it at `f3259ddf`) that predates the relicense and directly contradicts `NOTICE`, which records upstream's move to Apache-2.0 at upstream `cd5ea1be`. The M2 row is left exactly as written (this ledger is append-only); this row is the correction. **`CONTRIBUTING.md`** — the *Licensing* section is rewritten: zynk's own AGPL-3.0-or-later combined-work terms and the contributor agreement stay, a new paragraph separates upstream provenance (AGPL-3.0-or-later through upstream tag v0.7.1; Apache-2.0 from upstream `cd5ea1be` onward, redistributed inside zynk's AGPL combined work as Apache-2.0 §4 permits, zynk's own license unchanged), and the preservation paragraph now names `LICENSE-APACHE-2.0.upstream` beside `NOTICE`/`LICENSE` and tells a contributor who touches a post-`cd5ea1be` upstream file to add it to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list. Per ADR 0010 §6/§7 the section says "upstream", not the upstream product name; the §6(b) provenance attribution at `CONTRIBUTING.md:6-8` (the fork-of link) is untouched, and the section no longer names the upstream product at all. **`README.md`** — *License & provenance* said "Upstream copyright notices and the AGPL license are preserved", the same single-license story; it now names both provenance licenses, `LICENSE-APACHE-2.0.upstream`, and the §4(b) modified-file list. **`docs/zynk/SPEC.md` §2** — its licensing bullets predate the relicense (and the ADR 0010 rebrand sweep left them naming both projects `zynk`); one bullet is appended recording the relicense and pointing at `NOTICE` as authoritative, without rewriting the older bullets. **Co-author docs** — the three release/boundary checklists that named only the AGPL attribution (`.agents/skills/shipping-and-launch/SKILL.md`, `.agents/skills/zynk-pre-release-audit/references/pre-release-audit.md`, `.agents/skills/context-engineering/SKILL.md`) now name `LICENSE-APACHE-2.0.upstream` and Apache-2.0 §4 too, so a pre-release audit cannot pass while that text is missing from the build. **Guard** — new `scripts/test_license_docs.py`, an assertion-only maintenance unittest in the `scripts/test_skills_catalog.py` shape, asserts that `CONTRIBUTING.md`/`README.md`/`NOTICE` carry no "same license as upstream" claim, that `NOTICE` still records AGPL-3.0-or-later, Apache, `cd5ea1be` and `LICENSE-APACHE-2.0.upstream`, that `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `README.md` each name the relicense commit and Apache-2.0, and that the Apache text file exists and stays in the `Cargo.toml` `include` list. It is wired into BOTH `justfile` unittest lists (`test` and `check`) and the CLAUDE.md maintenance-unittest line. Verified RED by re-inserting the retired sentence into `CONTRIBUTING.md` (the failure names the offending file), then GREEN again after restore. **`NOTICE` is unchanged** — the warden independently re-checked its 14 §4(b) *Modified files* entries against the upstream commit file lists and found no omission, so only the docs that contradicted `NOTICE` changed. |

---

# LINUX-ONLY + v0.8.2 PORT LEDGER — M3 detection + identity

The upstream `v0.7.1..v0.8.2` detection and agent-identity port (22 commits), on branch
`port/linux-only-v0.8.2`. The licensing split of the M2 section above still applies: an upstream commit
dated before `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22) arrives under AGPL-3.0-or-later, a later one under Apache-2.0, and
only the latter appends its touched files to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list.

| zynk | upstream | what / where / why / adaptations |
|---|---|---|
| *(this commit)* | `80d2958a` | **Foreground discovery already scopes to the pane's process group here, so only the upstream test is needed.** Upstream's regression came from `ef67a970 perf(linux): cache foreground process group scans` (2026-07-03), which is **not in the M3 list and was never ported**: it snapshotted the whole `/proc` group map into a 250 ms `FOREGROUND_MEMBERS_CACHE`, and on a cache hit re-validated only the pids already in that snapshot (`ForegroundMembersCache::members`, `80d2958a^:src/platform/linux.rs:247-271`), so a child that appeared after the snapshot — the agent a shell wrapper had just `exec`-ed — stayed invisible for up to 250 ms while the wrapper itself kept the members list non-empty and suppressed the refresh. `80d2958a` fixes that by deleting the cache and replacing the global scan with a walk of the pane's own process tree (`process_tree_pids` over `/proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/children`) filtered by pgid. **The fork never had the cache:** `foreground_job` (`src/platform/linux.rs:16-62`) is still the pre-`ef67a970` shape and rebuilds the pgid-filtered `/proc` walk on every call, so it already reports a just-spawned group member; `FOREGROUND_MEMBERS_CACHE`, `foreground_process_group_members` and `process_tree_pids` appear nowhere under `src/`. **TAKE — the three `src/detect/mod.rs` test hunks only:** extract `open_test_pty() -> portable_pty::PtyPair`, rewrite `foreground_job_detects_sleep` and `foreground_job_detects_shell_running_command` onto it, and add upstream's new `foreground_job_detects_agent_behind_shell_wrapper`, which spawns `bash -c "bash -c 'exec -a codex sleep 999' & wait"` and asserts the job carries both the wrapper `bash` and the `argv0 == "codex"` `sleep`, and that `identify_agent_in_job` returns `(Agent::Codex, "codex")`. That test is **green against the fork's existing scan, 20/20 consecutive runs** — the evidence the port sheet's C10 prerequisite check asked for, and the reason this commit is `test` and not `fix`. **EXCLUDED — the entire `src/platform/linux.rs` net-state port** (`foreground_process_group_members{,_with}`, `process_tree_pids`, `process_task_ids`, `process_task_children`, `numeric_file_name`, `ProcGroupMember`): it would be a pure reimplementation of a path that already passes upstream's own regression test, and it would put the fork-local tolerance tweak at `src/platform/linux.rs:21-23` (skip a `/proc` entry that vanished mid-scan instead of `?`-ing the whole scan to `None`) at risk for no behavioral gain. **EXCLUDED — both `tests/api_ping.rs` hunks** (`sleep 30'` -> `sleep 30; :'` and the extra `pane.process_info` assertion): they exist to make upstream's tree walk observable and to pin the rewritten implementation, so `tests/api_ping.rs` is untouched and its 23 tests stay green. **Adaptations:** the `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]` guards upstream puts on the helper and all three tests are dropped, matching the fork's already-unguarded PTY tests after the M1 predicate sweep (`fc383f6`); the ported bodies are otherwise byte-identical to upstream. **Pre-relicense** (2026-07-14 < `cd5ea1be`): AGPL provenance, so no `NOTICE` modified-files entry. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only enabling refactor)* | **`Agent::ALL` is split into an identity roster and a manifest-bearing subset.** `src/detect/mod.rs` keeps `Agent::ALL` as the roster of every agent identity this build knows and adds `Agent::SCREEN_MANIFEST_AGENTS`, the agents that ship a bundled screen manifest; both are 18 members and currently equal, so this commit changes no detection behavior. `build_manifest_cache` (`src/detect/manifest.rs:291`) now iterates `SCREEN_MANIFEST_AGENTS` instead of `ALL`, matching upstream `v0.8.2:src/detect/manifest.rs:293`, and `all_bundled_manifests_parse_and_validate` (`src/detect/manifest/tests.rs`) stops asserting over a hand-listed 18-element array — which silently covered only the agents someone remembered to add — and iterates the constant, matching upstream `v0.8.2:src/detect/manifest/tests.rs:293-301`. New fork test `screen_manifest_agents_is_a_subset_of_all_agents` pins the containment relation between the two. **Why now, ahead of the agent additions:** upstream `d0e33345` (mastracode) introduces the fork's first *identity-only* agent — enum variant, label, aliases and hook authority, but no screen manifest — and its own test asserts `!Agent::SCREEN_MANIFEST_AGENTS.contains(&Agent::Mastracode)`. *Verified, so the rationale is honest:* a manifest-less member of one undifferentiated `ALL` does **not** panic — `load_manifest_uncached` opens with `let bundled = bundled_manifest(agent)?;` (`src/detect/manifest.rs:566-567`) and `bundled_manifest` returns `None` for a label with no `BUNDLED_MANIFESTS` entry (`:703-714`), the panics there firing only on an *invalid* manifest — it becomes an invisible `(agent, None)` cache entry instead. The split converts that silent state into a named distinction plus a red test. **Verified negative:** temporarily deleting the `("qodercli", include_str!(...))` `BUNDLED_MANIFESTS` entry makes the tightened test fail with `missing bundled manifest for qodercli`, so it pins the guard and not just the current agent list; the entry was restored. **`#[allow(dead_code)]` on `Agent::ALL`, with the reason recorded in-source:** the manifest cache was its only production reader here, and upstream's two others have no counterpart in this fork — `v0.8.2:src/cli/spec.rs:457` builds a clap agent-kind value list (the fork's CLI is a hand-rolled positional dispatcher with no clap spec) and `v0.8.2:src/terminal/metadata.rs:69` `metadata_report_agent` is unported. Every fork production path resolves a single label through `parse_agent_label` rather than enumerating the roster, so `ALL` is read only by the identity tests until a fork surface needs it; M3-02's round-trip test is its first iterating caller and M3-04 (mastracode) is where its contents first diverge from `SCREEN_MANIFEST_AGENTS`. **No upstream code taken**, so no `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* entry. |
| *(this commit)* | `2c444eee` | **One label/alias table behind every agent-identity lookup.** `src/detect/mod.rs` had two byte-identical 18-arm matches over `normalized_agent_lookup_name` -- one in `parse_agent_label`, one in `identify_agent` -- so every alias edit had to land twice or the two surfaces silently disagreed. Both collapse into one private `lookup_agent(name: &str)`; `parse_agent_label` normalizes and then calls `parse_canonical_agent_label(&name).or_else(|| lookup_agent(&name))`, and `identify_agent` reduces to `parse_agent_label(process_name)` (its `// Match against known binary names` comment goes with the deleted block). New `pub(crate) fn parse_canonical_agent_label` resolves a label only when it equals `agent_label(agent)` exactly -- no aliases, no case folding, no trimming, no `.exe`/`.js` suffix stripping -- which is the strict form later identity work needs when an alias must not be accepted. **Behavior-preserving, and provably so:** the two deleted match bodies were byte-identical (the port patch asserted a single match on an anchor containing both), and `canonical.or_else(alias)` returns exactly what the single `lookup_agent` call returns, so `parse_agent_label` and `identify_agent` map every input to the same agent as before; the 46 `detect::tests::` and 92 `detect::` tests pass unchanged. **Adaptations:** upstream's `lookup_agent` carries 21 arms including `omp`, `mastracode`/`mastra-code`/`mastra code` and `maki`; the fork's existing 18 arms are moved in verbatim instead, and the three absent agents arrive with `d0e33345`/`3b8aeee1` in M3-04 -- as one arm in one place, which is why this commit lands first. Upstream's replacement test `every_agent_label_round_trips_through_canonical_and_alias_parsers` hand-lists its 21 agents in an array; the fork iterates `Agent::ALL` instead, which is strictly stronger (a new enum variant is covered the day it is added, not the day someone remembers the array) and makes M3-01's roster constant its first iterating reader. `canonical_agent_labels_are_strict` is taken verbatim (`"claude-code"`, `"Pi"`, `" pi "`, `"opencode.exe"` all `None`). The deleted `agent_labels_use_display_names` needs no replacement: the round-trip loop asserts `agent_label` -> `parse_canonical_agent_label` for every agent, which subsumes its nine spot checks. Two doc comments are added above the new functions -- upstream has none -- because the strictness contract is the entire point of `parse_canonical_agent_label` and is what the new negative test pins. **Port-sheet discrepancy (C7 "Dead-code risk"):** the sheet warns that `parse_canonical_agent_label` has no non-test caller at this commit and that `clippy -D warnings` will fail without an `#[allow(dead_code)]`. It does have one -- `parse_agent_label` calls it on the first line of the ported body -- so no allow was added and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` is clean. **Second discrepancy (acceptance filter):** the sheet's `cargo nextest run --bin zynk detect::mod` matches zero tests, because `src/detect/mod.rs` *is* the `detect` module and its tests are `detect::tests::*`, not `detect::mod::tests::*`; the working filter is `detect::tests::`. **EXCLUDED:** nothing -- upstream touched only `src/detect/mod.rs` here, with no website, `docs/next`, macOS, Windows or Nix hunk. **Pre-relicense** (2026-07-13 < `cd5ea1be`): AGPL provenance, so no `NOTICE` modified-files entry. |
| *(this commit)* | `6fb0d803` + `fc824b99` | **Two process-identity fixes: the `opencode2` binary name, and Python interpreters that carry a version in their name.** (1) OpenCode's v2 binary is `opencode2`, which no alias covered, so a live OpenCode pane identified as no agent at all. `src/detect/mod.rs` `lookup_agent` gains it: `"opencode" | "opencode2" | "open-code"`. Because M3-02 already collapsed the two duplicate label matches into that one table, this is a one-line, one-place edit -- the port sheet's C15 fallback ("otherwise apply to **both** `parse_agent_label` and `identify_agent`") does not apply. (2) `wrapped_agent_name_from_runtime_argv` and `is_generic_runtime_or_shell` both matched the literal strings `"python"` / `"python3"`, so a Nix, pyenv or Homebrew interpreter invoked as `python3.12` was neither unwrapped to the script it was running nor recognized as a generic runtime: `python3.12 …/hermes --resume <id>` reported `python3.12` as the agent process and identified as nothing. New private `fn is_python_runtime(name: &str) -> bool` accepts `python` plus any `python<dotted-digits>` suffix (each dot-separated part non-empty and ASCII-digit), and both call sites route through it -- the runtime match arm becomes the guard `name if is_python_runtime(name)` and `is_generic_runtime_or_shell` hoists `is_python_runtime(&name) ||` in front of a `matches!` that no longer lists the two literals. The old inputs stay accepted: `"python"` hits the equality arm and `"python3"` strips to `"3"`, so this is a strict superset. The guard arm keeps upstream's seat -- after `"node" | "bun"`, before `"sh" | "bash" | "zsh" | "fish"` -- because a guard arm is order-sensitive in a `match`; nothing it can match also appears in a later literal arm, so the seating is upstream fidelity rather than a behavior dependency. **Adaptations:** none -- every production hunk and all three test hunks are taken verbatim, with no rebrand needed (neither commit touches `full_lifecycle_hook_authority`, a manifest alias line or any `ZYNK_*` surface). **Tests:** `identify_known_agents` gains `identify_agent("opencode2")` and `("opencode2.exe")`; two new job tests, `identify_agent_in_job_detects_opencode2_as_opencode` (leader `opencode2 --standalone` -> `(Agent::OpenCode, "opencode2")`) and `identify_agent_in_job_detects_python_version_wrapped_hermes` (`python3.12` running `…/bin/hermes --resume session-id` -> `(Agent::Hermes, "hermes")`). The nearest existing fork test, `identify_agent_in_job_detects_python_script_named_codex`, covers a different case (unversioned `python` running a script) and is kept. **Red/green proof:** reverting only the two production hunks and keeping the tests turns all three red (`identify_known_agents`, both new job tests); restoring them turns all three green, so no assertion is vacuous. `detect::` goes 92 -> 94 tests and `detect::tests::` 46 -> 48, all passing; `identify_agent` filter 19/19; `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo build --locked` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean; `python3 -m unittest scripts.test_agent_detection_manifest_check` 5/5. **EXCLUDED:** `6fb0d803`'s `docs/next/CHANGELOG.md` hunk (upstream release artifact, path private per the fork's content gate). `fc824b99` touched `src/detect/mod.rs` only. Also deliberately **not** touched: `src/detect/manifests/opencode.toml` -- upstream left it alone and a manifest is keyed by agent label, not by process name, so the fork's `aliases = ["open-code", "zynk:opencode"]` stays as is. **Port-sheet drift (not an error):** C17's fork anchors (`is_generic_runtime_or_shell` at `:561`, the §1.4 identity-surface line numbers) were measured before M3-01/M3-02 landed; the function now sits at `:597` and the anchors are otherwise character-identical, so every hunk applied cleanly. **Post-relicense** -- `6fb0d803` is 2026-08-01 and `fc824b99` is 2026-08-06, both after `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22) -- so Apache-2.0 provenance: `src/detect/mod.rs` is appended to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list (first entry for this path; no duplicate). |
| *(this commit)* | `d0e33345` *(detect hunks only)*, `3b8aeee1` | **Two new agents, on opposite sides of the M3-01 split.** `d0e33345` adds **Mastracode** as the fork's first *identity-only* agent -- enum variant, canonical label, aliases and hook authority, but no screen manifest -- and `3b8aeee1` adds **Maki**, a normal screen-detected agent that gets all of that plus a bundled manifest. **Mastracode (`d0e33345`), six `src/detect/mod.rs` hunks, all TAKEN:** the `Mastracode` variant lands in `enum Agent` (upstream seats it after `Omp`, which this fork does not have, so it goes after `Cline` -- the same neighbour, one slot earlier); `agent_label` gains `Agent::Mastracode => "mastracode"`; the alias arm carrying `"mastracode"`, `"mastra-code"` and `"mastra code"` lands **once**, in `lookup_agent`, because M3-02 already collapsed upstream's two duplicated match bodies -- upstream's fifth hunk (the same arm re-added to `identify_agent`) is therefore **absorbed, not dropped**; and `full_lifecycle_hook_authority` gains `("zynk:mastracode", "mastracode")`, rebranded from upstream's `herdr:` source label per ADR 0010 §7. The variant goes into `Agent::ALL` (18 -> 20 with Maki) and **deliberately not** into `Agent::SCREEN_MANIFEST_AGENTS`, which is exactly the distinction M3-01 built the two constants for. **Maki (`3b8aeee1`):** `src/detect/manifests/maki.toml` is taken **verbatim** (byte-identical to `3b8aeee1:src/detect/manifests/maki.toml`, md5 `d2ba8e27944a4eae11e743afc884061f`; `min_engine_version = 1`, 5 rules -- `permission_prompt` blocked 980 and `plan_complete_form` blocked 970 over `whole_recent`, `status_bar_spinner_working` working 900 and `status_bar_idle` idle 850 over `bottom_non_empty_lines(1)`, `prompt_box_idle` idle 840 over `bottom_non_empty_lines(3)` with `not` gates for the queue placeholder and a spinner row). No `aliases` key: upstream ships none and Maki has no `zynk:` hook source yet, so there is nothing to rebrand. `("maki", include_str!("manifests/maki.toml"))` joins `BUNDLED_MANIFESTS` alphabetically between `kiro` and `opencode`, and `Maki` joins **both** rosters (`SCREEN_MANIFEST_AGENTS` 18 -> 19, `ALL` -> 20). **`src/config/sound.rs` is required in M3, not deferrable to M4:** `AgentSoundOverrides::for_agent` matches `Option<Agent>` exhaustively, so the enum variants do not compile without their arms -- `Some(Agent::Mastracode) => AgentSoundSetting::Default` (upstream's shape: identity-only, no configurable field) and `Some(Agent::Maki) => self.maki` plus the `pub maki` field and its `Default` entry. **Doc comments corrected, because the split stopped being cosmetic:** `SCREEN_MANIFEST_AGENTS` no longer says "currently equal to" `ALL` (it is now a strict subset, 19 of 20), and `ALL`'s `#[allow(dead_code)]` justification now names its three test readers instead of forward-referencing milestones that have landed. **TAKEN beyond the port sheet's M3-04 file list, with reason:** `src/main.rs`'s sample-config `# Accepted:` list gets `maki` alongside the `src/config/model.rs` doc list the sheet calls out. Both lists make the same claim about `cjk_ime_agents`, which resolves names through `parse_agent_label` (`src/app/mod.rs:235`), so updating one and not the other would ship a self-contradicting commit; upstream reaches the same text at `v0.8.2:src/main.rs:460-462` but only syncs it in `a4d52ab6` (qwen), which §0 of the port sheet defers out of M3. **Deliberately NOT added to either list:** `mastracode` -- upstream omits it from both at `v0.8.2` too, and both lists are already non-exhaustive (`agy` / `antigravity` appear in neither, `devin` is absent from `src/main.rs`), so making them exhaustive is a separate concern, not this port's. `README.md`'s supported-agents table is likewise untouched: it is a curated fork-owned table that already omits `devin` and lists `gemini` / `cline` only as "detected but not fully tested". **Tests -- upstream's, adapted:** `mastracode_is_hook_authority_without_screen_manifest` is taken with `"herdr:mastracode"` rebranded to `"zynk:mastracode"`, `SCREEN_MANIFEST_AGENTS` kept as the constant name it now genuinely is, and one fork assertion added (`Agent::ALL.contains(&Agent::Mastracode)`) so the test pins both halves of the split rather than only the negative. `identify_known_agents` gains `mastracode` / `mastra-code` / `maki` and `parse_known_agent_labels` gains `mastracode` / `mastra code` / `maki`. Upstream's third hunk target, `agent_labels_use_display_names`, **does not exist here** -- M3-02 replaced it with `every_agent_label_round_trips_through_canonical_and_alias_parsers`, which loops `Agent::ALL` and so covers both new labels with no edit at all; that is the payoff the sheet predicted for iterating the constant. **Tests -- fork-only, beyond upstream:** `3b8aeee1` ships `maki.toml` with **zero behavioural coverage**, so `maki_manifest_detects_idle_working_and_blocked_states` (`src/detect/manifest/tests.rs`) exercises all five rules and the two precedences that matter -- bare ` [BUILD]` status bar -> Idle via `status_bar_idle`; a leading braille cell -> Working via `status_bar_spinner_working`; the `Permission Required` panel -> Blocked via `permission_prompt` **over** a status bar that still reads idle (980 > 850); the `Plan complete` form -> Blocked via `plan_complete_form`; a narrow pane whose status bar has overwritten the mode label -> Idle via the `prompt_box_idle` chevron fallback; and the same narrow pane while streaming -> **no** matched rule and `!visible_idle`, which is what pins the two `not` gates. **Red/green proof, so no assertion is vacuous:** deleting the `BUNDLED_MANIFESTS` maki entry turns `maki_manifest_detects_idle_working_and_blocked_states` red and `all_bundled_manifests_parse_and_validate` red with `missing bundled manifest for maki`; deleting the `("zynk:mastracode", "mastracode")` arm turns `mastracode_is_hook_authority_without_screen_manifest` red; **adding** `Mastracode` to `SCREEN_MANIFEST_AGENTS` turns that same test red *and* `all_bundled_manifests_parse_and_validate` red with `missing bundled manifest for mastracode` -- i.e. M3-01's tightened guard is what stops an identity-only agent from being mis-seated, exactly as designed; deleting the two `lookup_agent` alias arms turns `identify_known_agents`, `parse_known_agent_labels` **and** the round-trip test red. All restored and green. **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` + isolated `ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_*`):** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo build --locked`, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` all clean; `detect::` 94 -> 96 tests passing (`detect::tests::` 48 -> 49 for mastracode, `detect::manifest::tests::` +1 for maki), `config::` 123 and `sound` 18 unchanged and green; `all_bundled_manifests_parse_and_validate`, `mastracode` and `maki` filters 1/1 each; `python3 -m unittest scripts.test_agent_detection_manifest_check` 5/5; `python3 scripts/agent_detection_manifest_check.py --website-dir /nonexistent/agent-detection` prints `agent detection manifests ok`, which is the bundled half of the checker validating `maki.toml` against engine 2. **Pre-existing, not caused here:** the same checker run *without* that flag fails on `website/agent-detection/claude.toml` being an older version than the bundled copy -- reproduced identically on clean `871ab2e`; `website/` is gitignored and intentionally unsynced in this fork, and the script is not wired into `just check`, CI or the hooks. **EXCLUDED:** `d0e33345`'s entire integration half -- `src/integration/**` (8 files, including the `mastracode` hook asset), `src/api/schema/integrations.rs`, `src/cli/integration.rs`, `src/agent_resume.rs`, `src/terminal/state.rs` -- which is M4, and its `docs/next/api/*.schema.json` hunk (private path, upstream release artifact); `3b8aeee1`'s 8 `docs/next/**` hunks and both `website/agent-detection/*` hunks (`index.toml`, `maki.toml`) per the port sheet's global rules. **No manifest-engine change:** `maki.toml` declares `min_engine_version = 1`, so `MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION` stays at 2 and engine 3 remains M3-07's to claim. **Pre-relicense** -- `d0e33345` is 2026-07-04 and `3b8aeee1` is 2026-07-11, both before `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22) -- so AGPL provenance and **no** `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* entry for any of the seven touched paths. |
| *(this commit)* | `58b9fecc` + `7a852496` + `fae0b236` + `5dd20c0a` | **Four bundled manifests re-anchored onto the current agent chrome; no engine or code change.** All four fork copies were byte-identical to their upstream parents, so each is taken as the whole upstream file state at its commit and each is md5-identical to it (`antigravity.toml` `da7f61aa359335d736c50ffeae3af8a5`, `github-copilot.toml` `675b0fb26c8a1aaab01614cf15b57069`, `cursor.toml` `4b1ef2a20b4510e6f91ab1789282872e`, `kiro.toml` `138de79f5a80fb9ad40291be3f2f7041`); the fork-side diff's hunk bodies are line-for-line the upstream `src/`-side diff. **(1) antigravity `58b9fecc`:** the `/tasks` slash-command hint left the background-task status row, so `background_tasks_working`'s `line_regex` anchored on that literal (`(?i).*[1-9][0-9]*\s*·?\s+tasks?.*/tasks`) stopped matching and a pane waiting on background work read as idle; re-anchored on the chip alone, `(?i)·\s*[1-9][0-9]*\s+task`. | *(this commit)* | `99760f77` *(engine half)* | **A new manifest region that reads the TOP of the buffer, and the engine bump that gates it.** Grok clears its OSC busy signals while a background task runs, so the only remaining evidence of work is an animated chip pinned in the *first* non-empty row of application chrome. Every region in the fork's vocabulary addresses the bottom of the buffer or a prompt-relative anchor (`src/detect/manifest.rs` `validate_region_name`), so that chip was unaddressable. `99760f77` adds `top_non_empty_lines(N)` -- the mirror image of `bottom_non_empty_lines(N)`, returning the buffer prefix through the N-th non-empty line from the top -- and moves the manifest engine to 3. **Four hunks in `src/detect/manifest.rs`, all upstream-verbatim** (the diff of the two Rust files is byte-identical to `99760f77`'s): the region name is accepted in `validate_region_name` via `top_region_count(trimmed).is_some()`; `region()` gains the matching dispatch arm; the new `top_non_empty_lines` slicer reuses the existing `line_start_offset` helper (no new imports, and `slice_from_line_index` is untouched); and `validate_manifest` gains a guard that rejects a `top_non_empty_lines` rule whose manifest declares a `min_engine_version` below `TOP_NON_EMPTY_LINES_ENGINE_VERSION`. **The region name is declared with its own validation const, not a bare literal:** `TOP_NON_EMPTY_LINES_ENGINE_VERSION: u32 = 3` and `MAX_TOP_REGION_LINE_COUNT: usize = u16::MAX as usize` (65,535) sit beside the new `top_region_count` parser, and both are mirrored on the Python side (`scripts/agent_detection_manifest_check.py`: `REGION_RE` gains a third alternative, the new `REGION_COUNT_RE` catches an in-syntax count that is numerically too large, `MAX_TOP_REGION_LINE_COUNT = 65_535`, plus the two rejections in `validate_manifest` and `validate_rule`). **`top_region_count` is deliberately stricter than the existing `region_count` and does not replace it:** it rejects a leading `0`, a `+` sign, any non-ASCII-digit byte and any count above 65,535, whereas `region_count` still accepts `bottom_lines(0)` and `bottom_lines(+1)`. Retrofitting the strictness onto `region_count` would change how every existing `bottom_lines(N)` / `bottom_non_empty_lines(N)` spec parses and would desync the Rust matcher from `REGION_RE`, which is unbounded on digit count for those two names -- upstream left the asymmetry in place and so does this commit. **Why the const bump is safe on its own here.** The `min_engine_version > MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION` rejection lives in `parse_remote_manifest_for_agent` (`src/detect/manifest.rs:884`) and therefore fires only on *remote* manifests; the new `top_non_empty_lines` guard is the opposite -- it sits inside `validate_manifest` and so fires on *bundled* files too. No bundled manifest declares 3 or uses the new region yet, and a bundled manifest only ever needs `min_engine_version` at or below the const, so raising 2 -> 3 cannot invalidate one: `all_bundled_manifests_parse_and_validate` stays green and the bundled half of the checker (`python3 scripts/agent_detection_manifest_check.py --website-dir /nonexistent/agent-detection`) prints `agent detection manifests ok`. The reverse split *would* be unsafe, which is why `grok.toml`'s 2 -> 3 bump ships together with its `background_work_chip_working` rule in M3-08 rather than here. **This is the only `MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION` bump in M3.** **Red/green proof, one mutation per hunk, each restored md5-identical afterwards.** Dropping the `top_region_count` arm from `validate_region_name` reds only `top_non_empty_lines_requires_a_canonical_positive_bounded_count`; dropping the `region()` dispatch arm reds only `top_non_empty_lines_uses_top_occurrence_for_repeated_text`; dropping the `validate_manifest` guard reds only `top_non_empty_lines_requires_engine_three_when_declared`. On the Python side, dropping the `validate_manifest` rejection reds `test_rejects_top_non_empty_lines_below_engine_three`, dropping the `REGION_COUNT_RE` bound reds the two over-bound subtests of `test_top_non_empty_lines_requires_canonical_positive_bounded_count`, and dropping the `REGION_RE` alternative reds both. **Two gaps recorded rather than papered over.** (1) The const bump itself is unpinned: reverting `MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION` to 2 leaves `detect::` at 112/112 green, because every new test goes through `parse_manifest`, which never consults the const. It first becomes load-bearing in M3-08, when `grok.toml` declares `min_engine_version = 3` and the remote path would otherwise reject it. (2) `top_non_empty_lines_requires_engine_three_when_declared` asserts only `parse_manifest(..).is_err()`, not the message, so it also passes under a broken region acceptance (it survived mutation A above) -- it pins the guard's existence, not its reason. Both shapes are upstream's. **EXCLUDED, with reasons.** From `scripts/agent_detection_manifest_check.py`: the `import hashlib`, the `STAGED_WEBSITE_MANIFESTS` allowlist and the `staged_manifest` / `website_digest` / `stages_new_engine_manifest` branch in `validate_catalog`. Upstream uses them to let one published website manifest lag the bundled one while engine-2 clients catch up, keyed on a sha256 of the website file. `/website/` is gitignored here and the fork publishes no remote catalog, so there is no engine-2 client population to protect and no website file to digest -- carrying the allowlist would pin a checksum of an untracked file, passing on this machine and failing in CI. `validate_catalog` is left unchanged. The three staged-website tests are excluded for the same reason (`staged_grok_dirs` reads `check.DEFAULT_WEBSITE_DIR/"grok.toml"`, which no CI checkout has); the two tests taken are synthetic and keep the fork's Python suite free of any `DEFAULT_WEBSITE_DIR` dependency. Also excluded: `src/detect/manifests/grok.toml` (the manifest half, which lands in M3-08 with `b04c6496` and `b42c6e66`) and the `website/agent-detection/grok.toml` mirror. **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` + isolated `ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_*`):** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo build --locked` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` all clean; `detect::` 109 -> 112 passing and `detect::manifest::tests::` 50 -> 53; the `top_non_empty_lines` filter 3/3 and `manifest_update` 13/13; `python3 -m unittest scripts.test_agent_detection_manifest_check` 5 -> 7 OK; the bundled checker run above ok; `python3 scripts/check_public_tree.py` clean (1671 files). *(Running the checker with no `--website-dir` override fails on this machine -- the local gitignored `website/agent-detection/amp.toml` still carries `2026.06.10.1` against the bundled `2026.07.09.1` from M3-06. That is a stale untracked mirror, not a regression: the HEAD copy of the script fails identically, and `just check` only ever runs the unittest, never the script's catalog path.)* **This row also corrects the M3-06 (a05ce3b) row: the `codex_` filter count was 23 -> 31, not 24 -> 31** (existing rows are never edited). **Licensing:** `99760f77` is dated 2026-07-17 and predates `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22), so it arrives under AGPL-3.0-or-later and **no path is appended to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list** (it stays at 6 entries). |

The `min_engine_version` 1 -> 2 bump in this commit is pre-emptive: `bottom_non_empty_lines(N)` is an engine-1 region, and the OSC rules that actually need engine 2 arrive in the next commit. **`b42c6e66` (2026-07-17) makes Grok OSC-first:** Grok Build 0.2.101 emits OSC 0 titles and OSC 9;4 progress, so five rules land -- `osc_title_blocked` (1300, `Action Required`), `osc_progress_working` (1150, `^4;1;-1$`), `osc_title_idle` (1100, the `grok` title anchor, gated `not` on any braille frame), `osc_title_working` (1000, a bare non-whitespace catch-all) and `osc_progress_idle` (950, `^4;0;0$`) -- and **every screen rule is re-seated into the 1180-1300 band** (320 -> 1200, 310 -> 1190, 305 -> 1185, 300 -> 1180). The re-seat is load-bearing, not cosmetic: once `osc_title_working` matches any title that is not a known idle one, a permission prompt under a custom title would outrank every screen blocker at its old priority and report *working*. The header comment is rewritten with the new evidence. **`99760f77` (2026-07-17) closes the background-work hole:** Grok clears both OSC signals while a background task runs, leaving only an animated task-count chip in the first non-empty row of pinned chrome, so `background_work_chip_working` (1170, `top_non_empty_lines(1)`) lands and `min_engine_version` goes 2 -> 3. That rule is the reason M3-07 had to precede this commit: the `top_non_empty_lines` guard sits inside `validate_manifest` and fires on bundled files, so a bundled `grok.toml` declaring 2 while carrying the rule would fail validation outright, and one declaring 3 against `MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION = 2` would be rejected on the remote path. **The single adaptation:** the `b42c6e66` header comment names the upstream product as the thing that retains the payload after `9;`; that sentence is rewritten to name zynk, because ADR 0010 §7's CI gate bans the upstream product name in active source including comments. Nothing else in the file differs from upstream. **Tests are fork-authored -- all three upstream commits ship zero.** Six tests in `src/detect/manifest/tests.rs`, after the Amp section: `grok_option_dialog_and_footer_hints_are_blocked` (the gutter list wins at 1200 over the permission footer at 1190; each footer rule answers alone when the list is not drawn; note `Esc:unselect` does not contain the `:select` needle, so the two footer rules cannot answer for each other; a footer stranded above two newer lines is stale scrollback and matches nothing); `grok_spinner_status_and_cancel_hints_are_working` (the `[stop]`-anchored status line; the cancel footer beating `prompt_hints_idle` through its `not` gate; and the U+2800 narrowing, where a blank-braille splash row must not read as a running tool while a real spinner frame does); `grok_prompt_hints_are_idle_only_without_a_cancel_hint`; `grok_osc_title_and_progress_carry_the_turn_state` (both idle title forms, a spinner title falling through the braille `not` gate to the catch-all, the `Action Required` title needing its 1300 seat because the blink-on title still ends in the idle anchor, both progress payloads, and an unrelated payload matching neither); `grok_screen_blockers_outrank_the_generic_non_idle_title` (all four re-seated blockers under a custom title); `grok_background_work_chip_is_working_from_the_pinned_top_row` (the chip, a zero count, and the same chip one row lower). **Red/green proof, three layers, the manifest restored md5-identical after each.** (1) Whole-file: the six new tests against the `v0.7.1` `grok.toml` fail 6/6. (2) Per-rule ablation: dropping each of the twelve rules in turn reds exactly the test that names it -- `osc_title_blocked`, `osc_progress_working`, `osc_title_idle`, `osc_title_working` and `osc_progress_idle` red the OSC test; `option_dialog_blocked` reds both the blocker test and the re-seat test; `permission_hints_blocked` and `question_dialog_hints_blocked` red the blocker test; `spinner_status_working` and `esc_cancel_hints_working` red the working test; `prompt_hints_idle` reds the idle test; `background_work_chip_working` reds the chip test. (3) Per-edit ablation: reverting the braille class to `[\x{2800}-\x{28FF}]` reds only the blank-braille assertion; reverting `option_dialog_blocked` to 320 reds the re-seat test with `left: Working, right: Blocked` -- the exact regression the band exists to prevent -- and also flips the blocker test's winner to `permission_hints_blocked`; reverting `permission_scope_selector` to 300, or the two footer rules to 310/305, reds the re-seat test on that entry alone. **EXCLUDED:** `website/agent-detection/grok.toml` (all three commits; `/website/` is gitignored and the fork ships only the bundled copy) and `docs/next/CHANGELOG.md` (`b04c6496`; private upstream release artifact). **Licensing:** `b04c6496` is dated 2026-07-06 and `b42c6e66` / `99760f77` 2026-07-17, all before `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22), so they arrive under AGPL-3.0-or-later and **no path is appended to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list** (it stays at 6 entries). **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` + isolated `ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_*`):** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo build --locked` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` all clean; `detect::` 112 -> 118 passing and `detect::manifest::tests::` 53 -> 59; the `grok` filter 6/6; `all_bundled_manifests_parse_and_validate` 1/1 with grok now at `min_engine_version = 3`; `manifest_update` 13/13 unchanged; `python3 -m unittest scripts.test_agent_detection_manifest_check` 7/7 OK; `python3 scripts/agent_detection_manifest_check.py --website-dir /nonexistent/agent-detection` prints `agent detection manifests ok`; `python3 scripts/check_public_tree.py` clean (1671 files) and `python3 scripts/scrub_check.py` clean (82 scoped files). *(As recorded in the M3-07 row, running the checker with no `--website-dir` override still fails on this machine -- the local gitignored `website/agent-detection/` mirror now lags on eight manifests including `grok.toml`. It is a stale untracked mirror, not a regression: `just check` only ever runs the unittest, never the script's catalog path.)* |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix)* | **Claude's current-approval detection becomes structural and wrap-aware, closing the OPEN Gate-3 finding `ARB-18C-BOTTOM-WINDOW-001`** (major; it de-duplicates the five helper reports `SENT-R11-DETECT-001/002`, `AUD-18C-FRESH-001/002`, `INSPECTOR-18C-001`, `ARCH-18C316-OSC-TAIL-001` and `WARDEN-18C316-FRESHNESS-001`; evidence carried in `probe-boundaries.log` + `probe-terminal-wrap-evaluator.log`). The checkpoint-0 rule `current_approval_dialog` (990) selected an unconditional two-row PHYSICAL suffix, which is neither the documented contract nor wrap-aware: (1) an answered unboxed footer with exactly ONE later work row stayed inside the window and kept reporting Blocked, and (2) below roughly 48 columns the terminal soft-wraps the footer, so `esc to cancel` split across rows or fell out of the window and the waiting approval read as Working. Both directions are reproduced first as failing tests on the production path. **Engine:** `DetectionInput` gains `unwrapped_tail: Option<&str>`, the soft-wrap-joined twin of the same bottom-of-buffer snapshot, threaded from the two pane detection loops and the `agent explain` handler via a new `detection_unwrapped_text()` that shares `ghostty_detection_rows()` with `detection_text()` — still a snapshot, never the parser or the viewport. New region `bottom_logical_non_empty_lines(N)` selects the last N non-empty LOGICAL lines from that tail and falls back to the physical `bottom_non_empty_lines(N)` when no tail is supplied (remote and shim callers). It rides the existing engine-3 floor: `MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION` stays 3 (bumped once in M3 by `99760f77`), and the per-region gate + strict canonical count parser are generalized over `ENGINE_3_REGIONS` in both `src/detect/manifest.rs` and `scripts/agent_detection_manifest_check.py`. **Manifest:** `claude.toml` 2026.09.09.1, `min_engine_version = 3`; `current_approval_dialog` splits into `current_approval_footer` (990, `bottom_logical_non_empty_lines(1)`, the existing footer grammar) and `current_approval_boxed` (990, `bottom_logical_non_empty_lines(2)`, the same grammar plus a closing-box-border regex anchored with `\z` to the end of the region, so a boxed dialog admits its earlier footer row only under PROVEN chrome). Every other rule and priority is unchanged. **Tests:** a production-path matrix rendered through the real Ghostty terminal at 80/40/30/20 columns × boxed/unboxed × 0/1/2 post-answer work rows × half-circle and Braille titles, asserting the winning rule id and the visibility flags through `detection_update_for_publish_with_osc` and `explain_with_input`; the `SENT-R11-DETECT-003` negative control (footer grammar quoted inside a log line is not a dialog); a static-title prompt-box control (Idle via `live_prompt_box`); the logical-region unit tests and their engine-3 gate in both languages. The six fork-only precedence tests stay green, with the boxed and unboxed fixtures now naming the rule that must claim each. **Docs:** the CHANGELOG entry states the exact supported contract in place of the checkpoint-0 overclaim. **NOT touched:** the live override at `~/.config/zynk/agent-detection/claude.toml` (still 2026.09.08.4 / engine 2) — rolling it out is a separate operator gate. |
| *(this commit)* | `a47bc3e0` + `4473e391` *(verified no-ops: `9ba32c25`, `2075de5d`)* | **Claude's `/btw` overlay and the newer confirmation grammar, merged into the fork's diverged manifest.** `src/detect/manifests/claude.toml` → `2026.09.09.2`; `min_engine_version` stays 3 and the two M3-09 `current_approval_*` rules are left untouched. **`a47bc3e0` — `btw_overlay_working`, rule body verbatim, priority re-seated 975 → 955.** Claude drops its spinner title while the `/btw` overlay is open, so the pane read the retained static title as idle even though the turn was still running; the rule takes the overlay itself as the evidence (`region = "bottom_non_empty_lines(5)"`, `line_regex = ['^\s*/btw(?:\s\|$)', '(?i)esc to close\s*$']`, `visible_working`). Upstream seats it at 975, between its `live_blocked_form` (980) and `live_prompt_box` (950); the fork compressed that band (busy title 970, blocked forms 960, prompt box 950), so a literal 975 would have jumped the fork's entire blocked band. **955 reproduces both upstream relations** — a genuinely blocked form (960) still wins, the idle prompt box (950) still loses — and cannot collide with the 990 current-dialog rules, whose mandatory token is `esc to cancel` while the overlay footer says `Esc to close`. **`4473e391` — `live_blocked_form`'s grammar restructured, priority kept at 960.** Claude's newer confirmation prints `Enter to confirm` with no option list and no navigate hint, so the mandatory `contains = ["enter to select", "esc to cancel"]` never matched and a blocked Claude read as idle. `esc to cancel` becomes the only mandatory token and the discriminating evidence moves into `any`: a bare `enter to confirm`, or `enter to select` **nested with** an `any` over the five navigate-hint variants. **Adaptations.** (1) *The 990 rules stay as M3-09 left them* — `current_approval_footer` and `current_approval_boxed` already list `enter to confirm` in their `any`, and they read a different region (`bottom_logical_non_empty_lines(1\|2)` vs `after_last_horizontal_rule`), so both halves are needed: the 990 rules claim a footer that is still the logical tail, `live_blocked_form` claims one that has scrolled above it. (2) *Their bare `{ contains = ["to navigate"] }` is deliberately NOT propagated* into `live_blocked_form`: `after_last_horizontal_rule` is a suffix selector, not proof of freshness, so upstream's explicit five-variant list is what keeps stale scrollback from matching. (3) Upstream's `version` / `updated_at` hunks are excluded (the fork is ahead of `2026.08.04.1`), and `website/agent-detection/claude.toml` is excluded throughout — that tree is not tracked here. **Recorded, not re-applied:** `9ba32c25` (half-circle busy frames `U+25D0..U+25D1`) and `2075de5d` (widened to `U+25D0..U+25D3`, plus a test) are **no-ops for this fork** — `osc_title_working` already carries the `\x{25D0}-\x{25D3}` superset with an equivalent explaining comment, and `claude_osc_title_half_circle_frames_are_working` already covers the same four frames with the same three assertions. Verified green, nothing changed. **Tests:** four new in `src/detect/manifest/tests.rs` — the `/btw` overlay reports Working under both an empty and a static `✳` title, with each `line_regex` half proved load-bearing on its own; a confirmation after a divider is Blocked through both grammar branches (option list plus navigate hint, and the bare `enter to confirm` this commit exists for); an `enter to select` remnant with no navigate hint is NOT claimed by `live_blocked_form`, with the hint restored as the positive control; and the 955 seat is proved in both directions from `evaluated_rules` (loses to `live_blocked_form` at 960 while itself matching, beats `live_prompt_box` at 950 while that itself matches). The six fork-only precedence tests and the five M3-09 production-path width-matrix tests in `src/pane/terminal.rs` stay green. **Licensing:** `4473e391` is dated 2026-08-04, after `cd5ea1be`, so `src/detect/manifests/claude.toml` is appended to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list; `a47bc3e0` (2026-07-13) is pre-relicense and adds nothing there. **NOT touched:** the live override at `~/.config/zynk/agent-detection/claude.toml` — rolling a manifest out to the live runtime stays a separate operator gate. |
| *(this commit)* | `5f1957c2` *(detect half; asset half deferred to M4)* | **Hermes is demoted from lifecycle hook authority to a session-identity-only integration, and its screen manifest is hardened to carry the lifecycle instead.** Hermes's hook integration reports session identity only, never lifecycle transitions, so leaving it in `full_lifecycle_hook_authority` let a stale identity report pin the pane to a state the screen contradicted. `src/detect/mod.rs` drops `("zynk:hermes", "hermes")` from `full_lifecycle_hook_authority` and adds `session_identity_only_integration(source, agent_label)` (the upstream `herdr:` source labels rebranded to `zynk:` per ADR 0010 §7). `src/detect/manifests/hermes.toml` is replaced wholesale with upstream's post-commit file -- the fork copy was byte-identical to upstream's parent (md5 `b28b6c24bf116182482f39ba179d49d4`) and carries no fork alias, so this is a verbatim take: `min_engine_version` 1 -> 2, version `2026.06.10.1` -> `2026.07.24.1`, and the rule table grows from two whole-pane rules to nine bounded ones -- OSC-title `osc_title_blocked` (1100), `osc_title_working` (1050) and `osc_title_idle` (100); `interrupt_status_working` moved off `whole_recent` priority 100 onto `bottom_non_empty_lines(5)` at 950 with `ctrl+c to interrupt` alongside `msg=interrupt`; the new `classic_cancel_working` (500) on the same window; and four blocked panels at 900 on `bottom_non_empty_lines(14)` -- `dangerous_command_approval` (moved off `whole_recent` priority 300, gates widened), plus the new `clarification_prompt`, `credential_prompt` and `confirmation_prompt`, each with explicit `all`/`any` gates so a bounded footer, not incidental scrollback, decides the state. **ADAPTED, `src/terminal/state.rs`** (taken here, not deferred, so the demotion ships with its consumer and no `#[allow(dead_code)]` is needed): the early return guards the fork's single hook-authority entry point `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status_at`, which is where the fork funnels the whole `set_hook_authority*` family, rather than upstream's `set_hook_authority_at`; the session-replacement arm `("zynk:hermes", "hermes", Some("startup" | "new" | "resume"))` is appended to `session_start_source_allows_session_replacement` (the fork's list has three arms where upstream has six -- the mastracode/pi/omp arms come from commits outside this port range); and the replacement guard is recomputed against the fork's simpler `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start`, which has neither upstream's `process_present`/`session_replacement_allowed` locals nor its `recent_agent_process_exit` field (a later upstream commit, out of range). The local is therefore named `replacing_identity_only_session` after the generic predicate rather than upstream's hermes-specific `replacing_hermes_session`, and `process_present` reduces to `parse_agent_label(agent_label)` matching `detected_agent` -- the fork clears a persisted session on process exit inside `set_detected_state_with_screen_signals_at`, so the dropped `recent_agent_process_exit` clause has no fork equivalent to lose. Net behavior matches upstream: an identity-only integration may replace its own anchored id-kind session only while its process is the detected agent, so a background or stale start report cannot silently repoint the pane's identity. **Tests:** new `detect::tests::hermes_session_integration_leaves_state_to_screen_detection` (rebranded) and `terminal::state::tests::hermes_session_claim_leaves_state_to_detection` (upstream's, adapted to the fork's `set_hook_authority_with_session_ref` custom-status parameter and its `TerminalStateMutation` without `agent_released`); `visible_working_does_not_override_full_lifecycle_hook_idle` and `detected_agent_disappearance_does_not_clear_full_lifecycle_hook_session_ref` re-anchor from Hermes to Kimi, exactly as upstream did, because they need an agent that still holds full-lifecycle authority. **EXCLUDED -- deferred to M4:** the integration-asset half (`src/integration/assets/hermes/__init__.py`, `scripts/test_hermes_integration_asset.py`, the `justfile` recipe, and upstream's removal of the eight `HERMES_PLUGIN_INIT_ASSET` assertions in `src/integration/tests.rs`) -- the fork's asset is unchanged here, so those assertions still describe it and stay green. **EXCLUDED:** `website/agent-detection/hermes.toml` (the `website/` tree is untracked in this fork). Upstream `5f1957c2` is dated 2026-07-25, after `cd5ea1be`, so `NOTICE` §4(b) gains `src/detect/manifests/hermes.toml` and `src/terminal/state.rs` (`src/detect/mod.rs` was already listed). |
| *(this commit)* | `a4d52ab6` *(detect half; resume/CLI/integration halves deferred to M4/M8)* + `9e6c2b4e` | **Qwen Code becomes a detected agent, with the localized-UI fix folded into the same manifest.** `9e6c2b4e` is a delta against a `qwen.toml` that only `a4d52ab6` creates, so the two ship together: `src/detect/manifests/qwen.toml` is taken **verbatim at `9e6c2b4e`** (byte-identical to upstream, md5 `04e31de294ec223f1ed3a0a9c41a6f00`, `version = "2026.08.14.1"`, `min_engine_version = 2` -- no engine bump, the M3 engine-3 budget stays spent on `top_non_empty_lines`), rather than applying `a4d52ab6`'s 89-line file and then patching it. Qwen's chrome is why the manifest is shaped the way it is: the composer stays on screen while the agent answers, so the prompt box is not idle evidence on its own, and every status phrase is localized. The two OSC title rules `osc_title_blocked` (1200, `^\x{2733}\x{FE0E}? `) and `osc_title_working` (1100, `^\x{25D0}\x{FE0E}? `) are therefore the primary locale-independent signals and outrank `composer_idle` (100); the screen rules below them are exact per-locale fallbacks for the default `ui.showStatusInTitle = false`. **TAKEN, `src/detect/mod.rs`:** the `Qwen` variant (`:63`), `Agent::ALL` 20 -> 21 (`:84`) **and** `Agent::SCREEN_MANIFEST_AGENTS` 19 -> 20 (`:117`) -- Qwen ships a manifest, so unlike Mastracode it joins both rosters and `all_bundled_manifests_parse_and_validate` now covers it; `agent_label` (`:162`); the alias row `"qwen" | "qwen-code" | "qwen code"` in the single `lookup_agent` table (`:203`), which reaches `parse_agent_label`/`parse_canonical_agent_label`/`identify_agent` at once because M3-02 already centralized them; the `@qwen-code/qwen-code/dist/index` window in `agent_name_from_known_package_path` (`:595`, alongside the existing pi window in the same `windows(5)` loop); and the node/bun `argv[0]` re-read in `normalized_process_name` (`:370-390`). That last one is the non-obvious hunk: Qwen renames its main thread, so `argv0`/`name` is `MainThread` -- neither an agent nor a generic runtime -- and the existing `is_generic_runtime_or_shell` branch never fires. Reading the runtime back out of `argv[0]` fixes it, and upstream's narrowing to `identify_agent(..) == Some(Agent::Qwen)` is kept verbatim: widening it would let any renamed process claim an identity the earlier branches deliberately refused. **TAKEN, `src/detect/manifest.rs`:** the `("qwen", include_str!("manifests/qwen.toml"))` entry (`:265`). **TAKEN, `src/config/sound.rs`:** the `qwen` field (`:46`), the `Some(Agent::Qwen)` arm of the exhaustive `for_agent` match (`:142`) and the `Default` arm (`:182`). **TAKEN, doc lists:** `cjk_ime_agents`' accepted names gain `qwen, qwen-code` in `src/config/model.rs:944` and in the shipped default config template `src/main.rs:351-352` (the fork's template line does not list `devin`, so it is reflowed on its own wording rather than upstream's). **Tests:** `detect::tests::identify_agent_in_job_detects_node_wrapped_qwen` (upstream's, verbatim -- the fnm shim path and the npm package entry point, both behind a `MainThread` process name) plus the qwen assertions appended to `identify_known_agents` and `parse_known_agent_labels`; four **fork-authored** manifest-behavior tests in `src/detect/manifest/tests.rs` that upstream has no counterpart for -- `qwen_osc_title_states_outrank_the_always_visible_composer`, `qwen_confirmation_wait_is_blocked_in_every_shipped_locale`, `qwen_option_list_with_a_navigation_hint_is_blocked` and `qwen_working_footer_requires_the_elapsed_time` -- because the whole point of `9e6c2b4e` is behavior (title priority over the always-visible composer, the localized `any` gate behind the spinner-row anchor, the two-`line_regex` option dialog, and the footer now requiring an elapsed time so a bare `(esc to cancel)` no longer reads as an active turn). **EXCLUDED -- deferred to M4:** the whole integration/resume surface of `a4d52ab6` -- `src/agent_resume.rs`, `src/api/schema/integrations.rs`, `src/cli/integration.rs`, `src/integration/{actions,env,mod,registry,targets,tests,types}.rs` and the two `src/integration/assets/qwen/` session scripts -- together with its two consumers of that hook: the `session_start_source_allows_session_replacement` arm in `src/terminal/state.rs` that would admit a qwen hook source with `Some("startup" | "clear" | "resume" | "compact" | "branch")` and the matching `session_identity_only_integration` arm in `src/detect/mod.rs`. Those two are a single hook policy and are held back as a pair: no `zynk:qwen` source can exist until the integration is ported, so landing half of the policy would declare a contract for a reporter that cannot report. Qwen loses nothing in the meantime -- it has a screen manifest, and screen detection is exactly what the deferred policy would route it to. **EXCLUDED -- no fork counterpart:** `src/config/sidebar.rs` (the fork has no sidebar module) and upstream's `interactive_agent_executable` arm (no such function here). **EXCLUDED -- untracked/private:** `website/agent-detection/qwen.toml`, `website/agent-detection/index.toml`, `docs/next/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/next/api/*.schema.json` and the fifteen `docs/next/website/**` pages. Upstream `a4d52ab6` (2026-08-13) and `9e6c2b4e` (2026-08-14) are both after `cd5ea1be`, so `NOTICE` §4(b) gains `src/config/model.rs`, `src/config/sound.rs`, `src/detect/manifest.rs`, `src/detect/manifests/qwen.toml` (recorded even though it is a new file rather than a modified one -- it is upstream Apache-2.0 content end to end) and `src/main.rs` (`src/detect/mod.rs` was already listed). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `5f1957c2` row above)* | **Hook IDENTITY is split from lifecycle AUTHORITY, so a session-identity-only integration keeps the identity it reports.** Codex Gate-2 M3 finding (`msg_34f2e9b655927aaf`, P2): the row above routed `session_identity_only_integration` reports to an early `return None` in the fork's single hook-report funnel `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status_at` (`src/terminal/state.rs`), which discarded the WHOLE report -- including the `agent_session_id` the still-bundled Hermes asset sends through `pane.report_agent` (`src/integration/assets/hermes/__init__.py`). `pane.get` then lost the session, and `authoritative_receiver_identity` (`src/app/creation.rs`), which required `terminal.hook_authority`, answered `receiver_identity_unverified` for a really-detected Hermes pane that had explicitly reported its session. **Fix.** `TerminalState` gains `hook_identity: Option<HookIdentity { source, agent_label }>` beside `hook_authority`. The identity-only branch of the funnel now calls the new `record_identity_only_hook_report`, which keeps the hook-derived identity (source, label and session) under the same seq, detected-agent-conflict, owner-coherence and same-owner session-replacement clamp the full-lifecycle path applies, and drops only the lifecycle half: `state`/`message`/`custom_status` are ignored and `hook_authority` is never taken, so no state arbitration runs and the screen stays the sole lifecycle authority. `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` records the same identity for these integrations, because for them the session report IS the identity report. The identity ends with its process: dropped on the same process-exit evidence that drops the session, when the detected agent contradicts the reported label, on that owner's `clear`/`release` (another owner's leaves it alone, and the release path now also matches on the identity alone), and on respawn. `authoritative_receiver_identity` resolves the label from `hook_authority` OR `hook_identity` with owner coherence unchanged; a detection-only pane has neither and stays `receiver_identity_unverified`. The third consumer in the chain, the App-side `HookStateReported` dispatch (`src/app/actions.rs`), now names the identity-only shape explicitly beside the existing `is_reserved_native_state_source` branch and calls `record_identity_only_hook_report` directly, so the routing is visible where the report arrives instead of hidden behind a method named for hook authority; `TerminalState` keeps the same guard inside the funnel, so a caller reaching it directly still cannot route around the split. Reserved-native sources (claude, codex, copilot, devin, droid, qodercli, cursor) are deliberately NOT given hook identity: they persist a session and stay non-receipt-capable exactly as before. **Deliberately unchanged** because they are lifecycle, not identity: `full_lifecycle_hook_authority_active` and its callers (`src/app/agents.rs`, `src/app/api/agents.rs`, `src/app/api.rs`, `src/app/actions.rs` screen-detection skip), `effective_agent_label`/`effective_known_agent` and the effective-state arbitration in `src/terminal/state.rs`, and the hook-authority `custom_status` + metadata `applies_to_source` guards in `src/terminal/metadata.rs`. `terminal_agent_session_info` (`src/app/creation.rs`) and the snapshot capture (`src/persist/snapshot.rs`) need no change: both already fall back to `persisted_agent_session`, which is where the identity-only session lands. `src/zynk/header.rs::is_agent_target` likewise needs none -- the restored `agent_session` satisfies it. **Tests.** Eight `terminal::state` unit tests (identity kept without authority, identity without a session, the replacement clamp, process exit, conflicting detected agent, owner-scoped clear/release, and pi still taking authority with no identity); one `app::actions` characterization test, the sibling of `reserved_native_state_report_does_not_override_screen_state`, pinning the new dispatch branch; and five `tests/zynk_receipt.rs` integration tests, two of them the reviewer's probes (`codex-m3-hermes-probes.patch`) adapted to the fork harness. The five cover the SHIPPED reporter path (one `pane.report_agent` carrying state + `agent_session_id`, the only call the asset makes) for both `pane.get` and a receipt, the session-only path (`pane.report_agent_session`) for a receipt, a same-label pane holding a DIFFERENT session refused with `receiver_identity_mismatch` while the addressed session still receipts, and a REALLY DETECTED agent that never reported through a hook still `receiver_identity_unverified`. The M3-11 guards stay green unchanged: `hermes_session_claim_leaves_state_to_detection` (state ignored, session replacement) and the receipt characterization suite. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `920e1e1` row above)* | **A retired hook identity stays retired: the identity-only paths now apply the same retirement, freshness, sequencing and owner-coherence rules as the full-lifecycle path.** Codex Gate-2 M3 extension verdict (`msg_fd6336c8e9038990`, REQUEST_CHANGES on `920e1e1`, four P2 findings on the new `hook_identity`). The row above created `hook_identity` on two paths -- `record_identity_only_hook_report` and the `session_identity_only_integration` branch of `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` (`src/terminal/state.rs`) -- that skipped every guard the full-lifecycle funnel applies, so (1) a released Hermes session was resurrected by its own late callback and the SAME receipt flipped from `receiver_identity_unverified` back to `received`; (2) a detection observation captured BEFORE a newer identity report erased that identity and its persisted session; (3) `pane.clear_agent_authority` with `source = None` resolved its sequence fence from `hook_authority` only, so a stale clear erased a newer identity; and (4) an identity-only identity and a full-lifecycle authority could name different owners at once, after which the obsolete identity vetoed the accepted owner's `release` and that owner's own clear suppressed the live owner's later reports. **Fix -- one shared implementation, reached from both paths.** `hook_report_retirement_applies` names the owners retirement covers (full-lifecycle OR session-identity-only) and replaces the `full_lifecycle_hook_authority` gate inside the suppression/stale-session predicates, which are renamed off `full_lifecycle_*` accordingly (`hook_report_is_suppressed`, `hook_report_matches_stale_session`, `hook_report_has_fresh_session_after_suppression`, `hook_report_has_fresh_session_after_stale_session`, `suppress_hook_report`, `remember_stale_hook_session`, `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent`, and the `SuppressedHookReport`/`StaleHookSession`/`HookSuppressionReason` records they use). `hook_report_survives_retirement` is the single gate all three report paths call before `accept_hook_report`; `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting` is the single consumption step that turns a superseded suppression into a stale session; `owned_session_ref` resolves the session an owner holds in EITHER representation, so a release or clear suppresses the identity-only owner WITH the session it anchored; `retire_hook_identity` retires an identity the way the authority path retires `hook_authority` (suppress the owner, and on a process exit drop its sequence anchor). `HookIdentity` gains `reported_at`, `record_identity_only_hook_report_at` carries the report's own instant through the funnel, and the new `hook_identity_not_newer_than` guards the detection-side identity clear the way `hook_authority_not_newer_than` guards the authority clear; the persisted-session clear-on-exit is scoped by `persisted_session_is_anchored_by_hook_identity` so identity and session are retired coherently. `hook_report_is_stale` is the non-mutating half of `accept_hook_report`, letting `clear_hook_authority_with_mutation` fence a source-less clear against EVERY owner it would drop (authority and identity) before committing to any, and that clear now suppresses only the owner it actually clears. `release_agent_with_mutation` scopes the identity guard instead of vetoing: another owner's identity is preserved rather than dropped, but it no longer blocks the owner of the live authority from releasing, and an accepted full-lifecycle report retires an identity-only identity belonging to a different owner. **Not weakened.** Nothing trusts a detected label; the stored target session triple and the owner-coherence filter in `authoritative_receiver_identity` (`src/app/creation.rs`) are untouched; restart is not banned -- a genuinely new session re-anchors after a release, and after a process exit a freshly observed process does, exactly as for a full-lifecycle owner. `src/app/actions.rs` and `src/app/creation.rs` needed no change: the dispatch still calls `record_identity_only_hook_report`, which now delegates to the `_at` variant. **Tests.** The reviewer's seven probes (`codex-m3-hook-identity-lifetime-920e1e1.patch`), renamed without the `review_` prefix: five `terminal::state` probes plus `tests/zynk_receipt.rs::retired_session_must_not_receipt_after_a_late_report` were RED on `920e1e1` and are green here, while the positive control `both_report_shapes_bind_the_addressed_session_only` (both reporter shapes, plus the same-label/wrong-session refusal) stays green in both states. Five more `terminal::state` tests pin the positive side: reacquiring after a release with a fresh session through each reporter shape, resuming after a process exit only on fresh process evidence, an identity-only clear leaving a different live owner's reports alone, and an obsolete identity not vetoing a coexisting owner's release. The named full-lifecycle counterparts stay green unchanged (`stale_process_exit_does_not_clear_newer_same_agent_hook_authority`, `stale_clear_all_sequence_is_checked_against_current_authority_source`) alongside the M3-11 Hermes guards. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `3b70f78` row above)* | **An explicit same-session resume is admitted again -- but only on process evidence gathered AFTER the retirement.** Codex Gate-2 M3 extension verdict (`msg_5fe4c9a3eff5f1a8`, REQUEST_CHANGES on `0af3637`, one remaining P2). The row above made `hook_report_survives_retirement` (`src/terminal/state.rs`) reject any report naming a session in `stale_hook_sessions`, and it ran BEFORE `session_start_source` was ever considered. After a release or an observed process exit, the next `None` -> agent detection transition moves the retired session out of `suppressed_hook_reports` into `stale_hook_sessions` (`clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent`), so a later `pane.report_agent_session` carrying `session_start_source = "resume"` and a higher sequence was refused **permanently** -- the identity and its session never came back, and with them the pane's receipt authority. That is the one shape a real resume must take: the Hermes resume command deliberately reuses `session_ref.value` (`src/agent_resume.rs`), so a NEW id would be a new session, not a resume. The existing positive `identity_resumes_after_process_exit_with_fresh_process_evidence` moves `hermes-1` -> `hermes-2`, so it proves RESTART, not resume, and left the gap uncovered. **Fix -- evidence, then shape, then commit.** `StaleHookSession` gains `fresh_process_evidence`. It is set true in exactly one place, `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent`, whose precondition IS the evidence (this owner was retired, and its agent is the detected process again) -- the same fresh-process notion the restart path already relies on; `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting` records false, because a superseded suppression proves nothing about a running process. Every new retirement clears the flag for that owner inside the single suppression funnel `suppress_hook_report_with_session_ref`, so only an observation made after the LATEST retirement counts. `hook_report_survives_retirement` now takes `session_start_source` and splits the two rejections it used to fuse: a suppression still rejects unconditionally (a resume with nothing observed since the retirement is exactly the late callback the gate exists to refuse), while a stale-session match consults the new `explicit_session_start_reclaims_stale_session`, which admits the report only when BOTH hold -- it carries a session-start reason this owner actually starts on (`session_start_source_allows_session_replacement`, the same set the anchor-replacement clamp trusts; for `zynk:hermes` that is `startup`/`new`/`resume`), and the session it names is a stale session carrying fresh process evidence. A reclaim drops the sequence anchor the way a brand-new session does, but the stale entry itself survives the gate: `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting` (now also taking the agent label and the accepted session) removes it through the new `forget_stale_hook_session` only once the report has passed every remaining check, so a report this gate admits but a later guard refuses leaves the session retired, while an accepted reclaim stops being stale and this owner's ordinary follow-up reports for it are no longer refused. **Scope.** The two report shapes that carry no reason pass `None` and are unchanged: `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status_at` (full lifecycle) and `record_identity_only_hook_report_at` (the shipped `pane.report_agent` shape) can never reclaim. Only `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` -- reached from `AppEvent::AgentSessionReported` (`src/app/actions.rs`) for `pane.report_agent_session` -- supplies one, so the rule lives in the shared gate and applies wherever a reason exists. **Not weakened.** The stale-session check is kept, not removed; nothing trusts a detected label (the reason comes from the hook report and the evidence from the retirement bookkeeping, never from `effective_agent_label`/`detected_agent`); the stored target session triple and the owner-coherence filter in `authoritative_receiver_identity` (`src/app/creation.rs`) are untouched, and no app-side file changed. **Tests.** The reviewer's two probes (`codex-m3-same-session-resume-0af3637.patch`), renamed without the `review_` prefix -- `identity_same_session_resume_after_release_with_fresh_process_evidence` and `identity_same_session_resume_after_process_exit_with_fresh_process_evidence` -- were RED on `0af3637` and are green here, each also asserting the reclaimed session is no longer stale. Three fork-authored negatives pin the other half: `identity_same_session_resume_after_release_without_fresh_process_evidence_stays_retired` and its `..._after_process_exit_...` sibling (the explicit reason alone reclaims nothing), and `identity_late_callback_after_fresh_process_evidence_stays_retired`, which replays three non-start shapes against a freshly observed process -- the shipped `pane.report_agent` state callback, a reason-less `pane.report_agent_session`, and a `compact` reason `zynk:hermes` never starts on -- and requires all three to stay retired. At the API level `tests/zynk_receipt.rs::a_bare_same_session_resume_must_not_receipt_for_a_retired_session` sends the released owner's own `session_start_source = "resume"` for the session it just retired and requires the receipt to stay `receiver_identity_unverified`; it is green in both states, guarding against the naive fix. Every retirement negative, restart positive and reporter-shape control from the two rows above stays green unchanged, as do `stale_process_exit_does_not_clear_newer_same_agent_hook_authority` and `stale_clear_all_sequence_is_checked_against_current_authority_source`. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `ad7adba` row above)* | **Pending resume evidence expires when a NEWER observation shows that process gone -- whether or not an identity is installed.** Codex Gate-2 M3 extension verdict (`msg_f23d4dbab48a4c35`, REQUEST_CHANGES on `ad7adba`, one P2). The row above recorded `fresh_process_evidence` as a bare bool, cleared in exactly one place -- `suppress_hook_report_with_session_ref` (`src/terminal/state.rs`), which only a RETIREMENT reaches. The detection path retires a hook identity only while one is installed, and between observing the replacement process and accepting its session report there is deliberately NONE: that is the whole window the reclaim exists to serve. So an exit observed inside it retired nothing and left the bit standing, and a delayed `pane.report_agent_session` carrying `session_start_source = "resume"` still reclaimed the retired session on evidence about a process that had since died -- restoring the identity and its session with `detected_agent` at `None`, which `authoritative_receiver_identity` (`src/app/creation.rs`) then treats as the pane's receipt authority. **Fix -- evidence with a lifetime, not a latch.** `StaleHookSession::fresh_process_evidence` becomes `Option<Instant>`: not merely THAT the owner's process was seen again after the retirement but WHEN, which is what lets a later observation reason about it. `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent` takes the observation's `now`, stamps the sessions it newly stales, and re-arms the ones ALREADY stale for that agent -- so expiry voids a reclaim without banning the session, and the next genuinely fresh process admits the resume exactly as the first one did. `record_fresh_process_evidence` keeps the NEWEST of the two, so a reordered older observation cannot roll evidence back over a newer one. The new `expire_stale_session_evidence_on_process_loss` runs on EVERY detection observation in `set_detected_state_with_screen_signals_at`, deliberately OUTSIDE the identity-retirement block whose `hook_identity` precondition is the bug, and clears evidence strictly older than the observation on the same two limbs that retire an identity: this owner's process seen exiting (`process_exited` and the detected agent is the stale owner's), or a different agent detected in its place (mirroring `hook_identity_conflicts_with_detected_agent`, which likewise decides nothing when no agent is detected at all). Ordering is preserved the way `hook_identity_not_newer_than` preserves it for the identity itself: an observation captured BEFORE the evidence it would erase decides nothing. **Not weakened.** `explicit_session_start_reclaims_stale_session` still demands both limbs -- a session-start reason this owner starts on AND fresh evidence -- so every retirement negative is untouched; nothing here reads `effective_agent_label`/`detected_agent` for identity (the expiry consults the stale entry's own recorded label against the observation, never a detected label as authority); no app-side file changed. **Tests.** The reviewer's two probes (`codex-m3-resume-evidence-ad7adba.patch`), renamed without the `review_` prefix: `identity_same_session_resume_cannot_use_process_evidence_invalidated_by_a_later_exit` was RED on `ad7adba` and is green here, and its companion `identity_same_session_resume_keeps_process_evidence_after_an_older_exit_observation` -- green in both states -- pins the ordering half, so the fix cannot degenerate into "any exit clears it". One fork-authored positive closes the loop: `identity_same_session_resume_after_an_invalidating_exit_needs_new_process_evidence` requires the resume to be refused in the window after the invalidating exit and admitted again once the agent is observed running, proving expiry voids the reclaim rather than the session. Every positive, negative and reporter-shape control from the three rows above stays green unchanged, including both full-lifecycle stale-observation tests (`different_session_after_process_exit_waits_for_fresh_process_evidence`, `missing_session_after_process_exit_waits_for_fresh_process_evidence`) and `tests/zynk_receipt.rs::a_bare_same_session_resume_must_not_receipt_for_a_retired_session`. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `5ffafed` row above)* | **The latest process-loss observation is retained as a boundary of its own, so a reordered running observation cannot re-arm a retired session.** Codex Gate-2 M3 extension verdict (`msg_d2c0941e71e39abe`, REQUEST_CHANGES on `5ffafed`, one P2). The row above gave pending reclaim evidence a lifetime, but the ONLY timestamp a stale session kept was that evidence: `expire_stale_session_evidence_on_process_loss` (`src/terminal/state.rs`) set `fresh_process_evidence` back to `None`, and from that moment the session held no record of WHEN its process was last seen gone. Two consequences, both reachable through the production state methods. Every further loss observation decided nothing, because the loop read the evidence FIRST and skipped a session that had none; and `record_fresh_process_evidence` read `None` as permission to accept ANY observation, so the re-arm loop in `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent` restored evidence from a delayed running observation CAPTURED BEFORE the exit that had just voided it. "Keep the newest" therefore held only while the evidence was `Some`, never across an expiry: a Hermes session retired at t1, seen running again at t3 and seen exiting at t4 accepted a resume backed by a running observation captured at t3.5, although no process had run since the t4 exit;
