The four v3-shaped hermes assertions in `bundled_integration_assets_report_session_refs` are restated for the v4 CLI shape (`report-agent-session`, `--agent-session-id`, `--session-start-source`, `ZYNK_BIN_PATH`, and `pane.report_agent",` now asserted ABSENT). **Verification** (isolated target and runtime): `integration::` 97 -> 98 run / 98 passed; `terminal::state::tests` + `app::` 731 / 731; `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` 44 -> 46 run / 46 passed; full `--bin zynk` 2659 -> 2660 run / 2660 passed, 1 skipped; `scripts.test_license_docs scripts.test_release_audit_refs scripts.test_check_public_tree` 25 passed; `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean; `just gate` clean. **NOTICE:** `src/integration/assets/hermes/__init__.py` is appended to the `apache-modified-files` index at the position the port branch uses, because upstream `50ddc06f` is post-`cd5ea1be`. `src/integration/env.rs` and `src/integration/mod.rs` take a hunk from the same upstream commit and ARE listed in the port branch's index; they are NOT added here, so the index change stays the single asset line — flagged for the operator, and the port-branch merge brings both entries. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-authored fix on top of the `6e8209f` row above; `src/kitty_graphics.rs` is upstream `4eceb75d` (pre-relicense) code — no `NOTICE` entry)* | **A host PLACEMENT id that collides with another source's now probes to a free slot, so two visible placements of one image can no longer collapse into one.** Gate-3 arbiter finding #9b (`msg_5eeb0e5706b01e27`), reproduced by the inspector at placement id 105494: two visible inputs, `placements.len() == 1`. `host_placement_id` truncated a 64-bit hash of (pane, source image id, source placement id) into a 900 000-wide namespace, and `(host_id, host_placement_id)` is the placement cache key, so two DIFFERENT sources of the SAME host image could claim one id — the second overwrote the first in `host_placements` / `current_placements` and the first source's placement was simply lost while both stayed visible. Fix #9 (`6e8209f`) closed the IMAGE-id namespace only and recorded this one as still open. **Design — the same shape as `resolve_host_image_id`, one namespace down.** `PlacementSignature` gains `source: PlacementSource` (a `(PaneId, u32, u32)` alias for the pane plus the client's own image and placement ids), so the cache records WHO holds each slot; ownership, not the hash, decides reuse. New `resolve_host_placement_id(source, host_id, host_placements)` (`src/kitty_graphics.rs`) starts at the hashed id and probes forward past every slot under this host image held by a DIFFERENT source, stopping at the first free slot or at the one already assigned to this source. Alternative (b), a parallel owner map, was rejected: it would duplicate a key that the signature already has to carry, and adding the field costs nothing on the `encode_graphics_update` test surface — the probe only ever returns a free slot or this source's own, so the `*existing == placement_signature` re-emit compare sees an owner component that is equal by construction. `host_placement_id` now takes the source triple directly (identical hash bytes: `HOST_PLACEMENT_ID_BASE` 1 + hash % `HOST_PLACEMENT_ID_SPAN` 900 000), which also makes the collision search cheap. **Determinism**, documented in the doc comment: the probe reads only the persisted cache, so a source keeps its resolved id for as long as the colliding owner stays visible, independent of arrival order within a frame; when that owner disappears its entry is swept at the END of the frame, so the survivor holds its probed id through that frame and may re-place at the freed base id on the next one — one delete plus one place, never a lost placement. Namespace exhaustion skips the placement with a `tracing::warn!`, never overwrites. **Tests, RED first** (`src/kitty_graphics.rs`): `host_placement_id_collision_preserves_both_visible_placements` searches at test time for two `placement_id`s of one pane and image that hash to one host placement id (`DefaultHasher` is not stable across Rust versions), renders one frame with both visible and requires two `a=p` commands at DIFFERENT `p=` ids, `placements.len() == 2`, no delete, and one shared host image — RED on BASE `left: 1, right: 2`, with the RED emission showing both placements written to the same `p=18671`. `placement_probe_is_stable_while_the_collider_persists` requires an unchanged second frame to emit NOTHING (RED on BASE: two re-placed `a=p` at one id). `placement_probe_releases_the_slot_when_the_collider_disappears` walks the churn: the base-id owner leaves and its placement is deleted while the survivor stays put, the next frame re-places the survivor once at the freed base id and releases its probed slot once, and the frame after that emits nothing. The collision search is now the generic `first_colliding_pair` helper, shared with `colliding_data_fingerprints`, and `displayed_placement_ids` reads the `p=` of every emitted `a=p`. **Verification** (isolated target and runtime): `kitty_graphics::` 20 -> 23 run / 23 passed; full `--bin zynk` 2660 -> 2663 run / 2663 passed, 1 skipped; `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean; `just gate` clean. **NOTICE unchanged:** `src/kitty_graphics.rs` carries pre-`cd5ea1be` upstream code, so it is not in the `apache-modified-files` index and nothing is added. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only ADR; `NOTICE` unchanged)* | **ADR 0014 — receipt principals are the target pane's same-UID process tree.** Operator decision 2026-09-09 on the Gate-3 B1 blocker `SENT-R12-RECEIPT-001` / `ARB-B70-RECEIPT-ORIGIN-001`: a passive process in a pane could report an agent identity for ANY pane and then receipt messages addressed to it. `docs/zynk/decisions/0014-receipt-principals-same-uid-pane-tree.md`, Status **Accepted**. The ADR states that no cryptographic or process-identity proof that "the integration ran" exists across same-UID processes (argv is forgeable, hook scripts are separate processes from the agent, the user's own processes are the adversary model), so the honest question is WHICH PANE the caller belongs to — which the kernel can answer. **Decision 1 (principals):** identity reports and receipts are accepted only from a socket peer whose PID ancestry reaches the target pane's PTY child PID, with the peer PID taken from `SO_PEERCRED` and never from a request field; cross-pane, out-of-pane, foreign-UID and reparented/daemonized callers fail closed with `caller_outside_pane`, and a refused receipt leaves the message `submitted` with no delivery event. **Decision 2 (semantics):** `received` means a process inside the receiver pane's tree, holding that pane's environment, reported ingestion of exactly these ids — the integration's report under the same-UID model, NOT a proof that the integration binary ran; the persisted provenance is renamed `integration` -> `pane_tree`, which also amends ADR 0003's `proof_source` enumeration (0003 itself is NOT rewritten). **Decision 3:** `pane.release_agent`, `pane.clear_agent_authority` and `pane.report_metadata` stay unbound — the first two only RETIRE an identity (refusing them would keep a dead session alive, the wrong failure direction) and operator tooling releases panes from outside them, while `set_agent_metadata` (`src/terminal/metadata.rs:81`) writes presentation only and never touches `hook_authority` / `hook_identity` / the persisted session. **Consequences** record that in-pane self-assertion stays possible BY DESIGN, that all 13 bundled assets already keep their reporter in the pane tree (4 in-process Node/Bun plugins, hermes' `subprocess.run` child, 8 shell hooks running `bash -> python3`; none backgrounds, `setsid`s, `nohup`s or double-forks), that `server.live_handoff` preserves pane child PIDs so the check survives it, that `SO_PEERCRED` and `/proc` are ADR-0013 Linux-only APIs behind `src/platform/`, and that the debug-only test seam must stay out of release builds. **Rejected:** detector corroboration as proof (an agent-shaped passive process defeats it, and it would break every hook that fires mid-turn), per-pane secret tokens (inherited by every process in the pane — no stronger than the tree, and a new secret to leak), cryptographic attestation (no key material on either side). Docs-only commit: no code, no tests, no version change. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only security fix; `NOTICE` unchanged)* | **Identity reports and receipts are now bound to the TARGET pane's same-UID process tree, so a process outside a pane can no longer speak for it (ADR 0014).** Gate-3 B1 blocker `SENT-R12-RECEIPT-001` / `ARB-B70-RECEIPT-ORIGIN-001`, operator decision 2026-09-09. **Caller identity comes from the kernel.** `ipc::stream_peer_credentials` (`src/ipc.rs`) reads the accepted connection's `SO_PEERCRED` through `interprocess`' own `getsockopt(SOL_SOCKET, SO_PEERCRED)` wrapper — the local-socket `Stream` exposes no raw fd, and this is the same syscall a hand-rolled call would make — and `ApiCaller::from_socket` (`src/api/mod.rs`) fixes it at accept for the whole connection. `ApiRequestMessage` carries the caller to the App; the wire JSON is unchanged and no request field can influence it. `ApiCaller::default()` is the FAIL-CLOSED value (no peer, no trust), so any path that is not the socket is refused by the bound methods. **The check** is one greppable list at the socket-to-state boundary: `caller::pane_bound_target` (`src/app/api/caller.rs`) names `pane.report_agent`, `pane.report_agent_session` and `zynk.message_received` with their pane, and `handle_api_request_after_internal_events_drained_from_socket` runs `App::caller_is_inside_pane` before the handler exists, so no bound handler can be reached without it. The check refuses an unknown peer, a foreign UID (`platform::current_uid`), a pane with no PTY child, and a peer whose ancestry does not reach that child; `platform::process_is_descendant_of` walks `/proc/<pid>/stat` PPid (`platform::parent_pid`) with the lookup INJECTED so the rule is unit-testable, stopping at PID 1, at an unreadable process, at a self-parent and after `MAX_ANCESTRY_HOPS` (64) — every stop a refusal. Refusal is the F4 code `caller_outside_pane` with a message naming the peer pid, the pane and the ADR; the receipt path writes NO delivery event, so a refused receipt leaves the message `submitted`. An unresolvable pane id falls through to the handler's own `pane_not_found` / `receiver_identity_unverified`, which is more accurate and grants nothing. **Unbound, and why:** `pane.release_agent` and `pane.clear_agent_authority` only RETIRE an identity (refusing them from outside would keep a dead session alive — the wrong failure direction) and operator tooling releases panes from outside them; `pane.report_metadata` (`set_agent_metadata`, `src/terminal/metadata.rs:81`) writes presentation only and never touches `hook_authority` / `hook_identity` / the persisted session. **Truthful provenance (ADR 0014 Decision 2):** the receipt's `delivery_events.proof_source` is renamed `integration` -> `pane_tree` (`receipt::RECEIPT_PROOF_SOURCE`), because the server proves a process inside the pane's tree reported ingestion, never that an integration binary ran. `proof_source` carries a CHECK constraint and SQLite cannot alter one in place, so the NEW `migrations/zynk/0004_receipt_proof_source_pane_tree.sql` rebuilds `delivery_events` — new table with the new value in the constraint, rows copied with the old value rewritten, old table dropped, renamed, index recreated; migration 0001 is NOT edited, so existing databases keep their checksums. No CLI or API string claimed a receipt was a verified integration, so none needed softening; the `received/integration` comments are restated. **Debug-only test seam:** `ZYNK_TEST_TRUST_PEER_PID=pane-child` (`src/api/mod.rs`), read at accept, makes a server treat each connection as the target pane's child. The constant, its reader and the `ApiCaller` field are compiled ONLY under `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`; a real `cargo build --release` binary contains ZERO occurrences of the name while the debug binary contains one and the shipped `ZYNK_BIN_PATH` appears seven times in the same release binary (so the search is sound). `the_debug_peer_trust_seam_cannot_exist_in_a_release_build` pins the gating in the source. **Tests, RED first.** `tests/zynk_receipt.rs` gains six, all against a server spawned WITHOUT the seam (`spawn_untrusted_fixture`): `an_identity_report_from_outside_the_pane_is_refused` (RED on BASE: the report answered `{"id":"sess","result":{"type":"ok"}}`), `an_identity_report_from_inside_the_pane_is_accepted`, `a_pane_process_may_claim_its_own_pane_but_not_another` — the arbiter's shape, stated as the rule: a plain passive process IS a trusted principal for its own pane, and only for it — `a_reparented_reporter_is_refused` (a double-forked reporter that waits until its PPID has actually changed before connecting, so the reparented state is asserted rather than raced), `a_receipt_from_outside_the_receiver_pane_is_refused` (RED on BASE: the receipt returned `"delivery_status":"received"`) and `a_receipt_from_inside_the_receiver_pane_is_received`. `tests/live_handoff.rs::live_handoff_keeps_the_pane_tree_binding` runs both directions across a real `server.live_handoff` on a no-seam server. Eight unit tests in `src/app/api/caller.rs` drive the walker against a synthetic process table (the pane chain, another pane, the server itself, reparented, unreadable, cyclic, over the hop bound, pid 0) plus the bound-method list; `src/zynk/db.rs::migration_0004_rewrites_the_retired_integration_proof_source` plants a real version-3 DB with an `integration` row, opens it, and requires the row rewritten with every other column intact and the retired value no longer writable. Every pre-existing suite that reports identity from the harness now sets the seam at its own server spawn, so the intent is visible where the server is started (`api_ping`, `cli_wrapper`, `client_mode`, `cross_area`, `live_handoff`, `zynk_header`, `zynk_message`, `zynk_native_cli`, `zynk_query`, `zynk_receipt`). **Verification** (isolated target and runtime): `--bin zynk` 2663 -> 2672 run / 2672 passed, 1 skipped; `api::` + `app::` 638 -> 646 / 646 passed; `--test zynk_receipt` 27 -> 33, `--test zynk_native_cli` 19 unchanged, `--test live_handoff` 29 -> 30 (including `live_server_holds_one_pty_master_fd_per_pane`, the known environment-limited case, which passed); FULL suite 2978 -> 2994 run / 2994 passed, 3 skipped; `just test-ts` 9/9; the 10 maintenance unittests 64 passed; `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean; `just gate` clean. `App::handle_api_request` is now `#[cfg(test)]` with a justifying comment — ADR 0014 gave production `handle_api_request_from_socket`, and ~70 unit tests still drive methods through the caller-less entry. **NOTICE unchanged:** none of `src/api/*`, `src/app/api*`, `src/ipc.rs`, `src/platform/*`, `src/server/headless.rs`, `src/zynk/*` is in the `apache-modified-files` index, and the one touched file that IS listed, `tests/client_mode.rs`, is already there. **Open, reported to the operator:** arbiter closure condition 3 also asks that `scripts/release_binary_audit.py` (fix #13) assert the seam name's absence; that script does not exist on this branch, so the extension is deferred to whoever lands fix #13, and the absence is proven here by a real release build plus the source-gating test. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only licensing fix; the `NOTICE` §4(b) index gains `src/integration/env.rs` and `src/integration/mod.rs`)* | **Every file in the `NOTICE` *Modified files (Apache-2.0 provenance)* index now carries the section 4(b) change notice in its own first lines, and a maintenance test enforces the index in both directions.** Gate-3 B1 finding `WARDEN-R14-APACHE-4B-001`: the central index tracked the duty, but no modified file said so on its face, which is what 4(b) asks of the file itself. The index is completed first — the `6a5783e` fix backported post-relicense upstream `50ddc06f` hunks into `src/integration/env.rs` and `src/integration/mod.rs` but listed only the Python asset, so both files are added at the port branch's positions. All 17 listed files then take the same standardized two-line notice as their first lines, in each file's native comment syntax (`//` for Rust, `#` for TOML and Python), ahead of any `//!` module doc, of `Cargo.toml`'s `[package]` table, and of the Python asset's docstring and its `ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=` marker — all of which keep parsing (`cargo metadata --offline`, `test_agent_detection_manifest_check`, the asset-version parity tests, `cargo fmt --check`). **Test:** `scripts/test_license_docs.py` gains `ApacheModifiedFileNoticeTests` — every listed path exists, is tracked and carries both notice lines within its first 5 lines; and no OTHER tracked text file carries the notice's first line, so a stale notice left behind by a delisting, or one copied into an unlisted file, fails the build. Both directions were proven RED first (17 unmarked files; a planted notice in `src/app/state.rs`). `CONTRIBUTING.md` and the `NOTICE` preamble now state the per-file rule next to the index rule. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only hardening; `NOTICE` unchanged)* | **The release-infra override becomes a compile-time seam, and a new maintenance script audits the BUILT release binary for what the source-level gating claims.** Gate-3 B1 finding `WARDEN-R14-SOURCE-ONLY-BYPASS-001`: `release_infra_test_override_active()` read `ZYNK_FAKE_UPDATE_VERSION` at runtime in *every* build, so a released, source-only binary still carried an environment switch that reopened the updater. `FAKE_UPDATE_VERSION_ENV`, its notes sibling, `fake_release_notes_body`, the `auto_update` fake-version branch and `release_notes::load_preview_from_local_changelog` are now all `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`; `release_infra_open()` has two bodies, and the release one is exactly `ZYNK_RELEASE_INFRA_AVAILABLE`. **Tests:** a `#[cfg(not(debug_assertions))]` twin asserts the gate stays closed with the env var set (`cargo nextest run --release --locked --bin zynk release_infra_override`) — RED against the old runtime override — and its debug twin asserts the seam still opens the gate for the release-machinery tests. **Artifact audit:** `scripts/release_binary_audit.py` (+ the hermetic fixture unittest `scripts/test_release_binary_audit.py`, wired into both `justfile` unittest lines and CLAUDE.md's maintenance list) reads the URL constants out of the sources and asserts a real release binary contains neither them nor `ZYNK_FAKE_UPDATE_VERSION`/`ZYNK_FAKE_UPDATE_NOTES_VERSION`/`ZYNK_TEST_TRUST_PEER_PID`, then runs it as `zynk update` with the environment sanitized of every inherited `ZYNK_*` — once plain, once with the override set — behind a PATH-local fake `curl`, requiring both runs to fail closed with the downloader never invoked. That closes the ADR 0014 condition fix #18 (`2696cb7`) could not satisfy from the source alone: the debug-only peer-trust seam is absent from the artifact. `just release-audit` builds `--release --locked` and runs it; `just check` stays hermetic on the fixture unittest, and B1 final verification runs the recipe on the candidate. Full removal of the self-update machinery remains the program's M9/M-FINAL item under ADR 0013. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only scope + custody fix; `NOTICE` unchanged)* | **Remote attach is Linux x86_64 only, seeded only from a reviewed local build, and the copy is now verified by source SHA and sha256 before anything else.** Gate-3 B1 finding `WARDEN-R14-M1-REMOTE-CUSTODY-001`. **This corrects the M1 section above:** that section says the macOS/Windows surface was removed rather than made optional, and its docs row claims every active surface was aligned — `src/remote/unix.rs` was not. `RemotePlatform::from_uname` still mapped `Darwin` to `macos` and `arm64`/`aarch64` to `aarch64`, `local()` still had a `cfg!(target_arch = "aarch64")` branch, and the remote bootstrap still carried a full release-manifest/asset download path for platforms zynk does not build. `from_uname` now returns `Some` only for `Linux` + `x86_64`/`amd64`; every other OS and architecture is refused with an error naming ADR 0013. The manifest types, `download_release_asset`, `remote_release_asset`, `fetch_remote_manifest`, `private_download_dir`, the two `zynk.dev` URL constants and `InstallSource`'s temporary-directory half are deleted, leaving exactly two install sources: the current executable and `ZYNK_REMOTE_BINARY`. **Custody (ADR 0013 Decision 3):** version and protocol are not substitutes for the reviewed source, so the binary now attests the commit it was built from — `build.rs` exports `ZYNK_BUILD_SHA` from `git rev-parse HEAD` (`-dirty` when tracked files differ, empty for a non-git build such as a crates.io `.crate` unpack) and `zynk --version` prints it as `zynk <version> (<sha>)`. Before the copy, `local_install_custody` records the file's sha256 and that attested commit, and REFUSES any source that cannot attest one, `ZYNK_REMOTE_BINARY` files included. After the copy, `verify_remote_custody` reads `sha256sum` and `--version` from the remote over the same ssh channel, logs both hashes with `tracing::info!`, and requires the remote hash and commit to match — before the existing version/protocol validation, so a substituted binary fails on its bytes rather than on what it says about itself. **Tests:** positives `(Linux, x86_64)` and `(Linux, amd64)`; negatives `(Darwin, arm64)`, `(Darwin, x86_64)`, `(Linux, aarch64)`, `(Linux, arm64)`, `(Linux, riscv64)`, `(FreeBSD, x86_64)` plus the ADR-0013-naming refusal; custody attesting and refusing cases over fake binaries; and the remote comparison for matching bytes, substituted bytes under a matching version line, a different commit, and a remote that cannot attest one. The Darwin asset-key assertion, the macOS path-discovery test, the four manifest-parsing tests and the temporary-directory cleanup test are removed with the code they covered. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only UX correction; `NOTICE` unchanged)* | **Every remaining install-guidance string points at an exact-source rebuild, and nowhere else.** Gate-3 B1 finding `WARDEN-R14-SOURCE-ONLY-UX-001`. **This corrects the M1 section above:** its docs row claims every active surface was aligned with ADR 0013, but four user-facing strings still offered channels zynk does not have — `ZYNK_UPDATE_UNAVAILABLE_MESSAGE` (`src/update.rs`) named Homebrew (`brew upgrade dzevs/tap/zynk`), a GitHub release binary and `nix run`; `channel set` (`src/cli.rs`) repeated all three; the `zynk update` and `zynk channel set` help lines (`src/main.rs`) said "update via Homebrew, a release binary, Nix, or source"; and `README.md` still carried the GitHub-release version badge next to a source-only install section. All four now say the same thing: zynk is built from source only, so rebuild the exact source you want — `cargo install zynk --locked` from crates.io, or `cargo install --path . --locked` from a reviewed checkout — then `zynk server stop` so the new binary takes effect. The release badge is removed; the crates.io and license badges stay. **Test:** `self_update_fails_closed_to_source_build_without_release_infra` now asserts the exact-source guidance AND that no `brew `, `github.com/dzevs/zynk/releases` or `nix run` channel is offered, so a retired channel cannot creep back into the fail-closed message. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only tooling hardening; `NOTICE` unchanged)* | **The vendoring script validates every archive member before extracting, instead of only the first path component of each name.** Gate-3 B1 findings `INSPECTOR-D03-003` / `INSPECTOR-B1-003`. `parse_archive_root` checks that all members share one root, but it reads only the first component, so `libghostty-vt-1.0.0/../../escaped.txt` passed that check and `tarfile.extractall(temp_dir_path)` then wrote it outside the `TemporaryDirectory`. Extraction moves into `extract_archive`, which runs `check_archive_member` over every member first: absolute names, any `..` component, symlinks and hard links whose target leaves the extraction root, empty link targets, device/fifo entries and any other non-file type are refused with a `refusing to extract …` error. That explicit validation is the primary guard because `tarfile`'s extraction filters only exist from Python 3.12 and only became the default in 3.14; where `tarfile.data_filter` is available, `extractall(…, filter="data")` runs as a second, independent check. **Tests (RED first, 5 of 6 failing before the fix):** a traversal member, an absolute member, an escaping symlink, an escaping hard link and a device entry are each refused with that error and write nothing outside the root; a clean archive — regular files plus an in-tree symlink — still extracts unchanged. The absolute-member case was the sharpest: on Python 3.14 it raised nothing at all, the filter simply relativized the path and extracted it. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only correction of a fork-owned migration; `NOTICE` unchanged)* | **Migration 0004 WIDENS the `proof_source` CHECK instead of relabelling history, so a pre-ADR-0014 receipt keeps saying `integration`.** Codex Gate-2 P2 1 on `e8f6e7e` (`msg_3e339000b75278a4`). The migration copied rows through `CASE proof_source WHEN 'integration' THEN 'pane_tree' ELSE proof_source END`, so EVERY historical row acquired the provenance `src/zynk/receipt.rs` documents as "the server proved a process inside the receiver pane's tree reported the ingestion" — a check the old server never ran. Codex reproduced it on a real upgrade of one isolated database (old release binary, then the new one, no new receipt): a receipt taken by a process OUTSIDE every pane read back as `received`/`pane_tree`. **Fix.** The rebuilt table's CHECK admits BOTH values (`pane.send_text`, `pane.send_input`, `pane.submit`, `integration`, `pane_tree`, `operator`, `system.recovery`) and the INSERT copies `proof_source` VERBATIM; the header comment now states that the migration widens the constraint and does not touch history. `receipt::LEGACY_RECEIPT_PROOF_SOURCE` names `integration` as the legacy, origin-unverified value — retained so history is not restated, and never written by this build: `persistence::append_delivery_event_in_transaction`, the one write path every delivery event passes through, fails closed on it with the code `legacy_proof_source`. ADR 0014 gains an `## Amendment 2026-09-09` section rather than a rewritten Decision 2: the rename applies to receipts THIS build records, the ADR 0003 enumeration amendment therefore ADDS `pane_tree` instead of replacing `integration`, and `docs/zynk/SPEC.md`'s `delivery_events` line now lists both. **Migration policy (disclosed).** Editing an existing migration re-checksums it, so any database that already ran the previous 0004 is WIPED at its next init (`CLAUDE.md`; accepted by ADR 0010 — the DB is disposable and has no external consumers). 0004 has never been merged, released or installed, so the only databases that ran it are this program's isolated test and reviewer databases. The alternative — a `0005` that un-rewrites — is impossible: once the old 0004 has run, a relabelled legacy row is indistinguishable from a genuine new `pane_tree` one. Migration `0001` is untouched, so existing databases keep their checksums. **Tests (RED first).** `migration_0004_preserves_legacy_integration_rows` replaces `migration_0004_rewrites_the_retired_integration_proof_source` (`src/zynk/db.rs`): a version-3 database carrying an `integration` receipt row is opened, the row must come back `integration` with every other column intact, a `pane_tree` row must then insert, and a value outside the enumeration must still be refused (RED on `e8f6e7e`: the row read `pane_tree`). `the_legacy_integration_proof_source_is_never_recorded` (`src/zynk/persistence.rs`) drives the write path on an ALREADY-`submitted` message, so the transition is valid and only the provenance can refuse it, and the same event with `pane_tree` is accepted. `no_write_path_records_the_legacy_proof_source` (`src/zynk/receipt.rs`) scans every `proof_source` initializer under `src/`, pins the set, and requires the legacy value to appear in exactly one place — the persistence test that proves it is refused. `valid_receipt_records_received_with_integration_proof` is renamed `…_with_pane_tree_proof` and now also asserts the two constants differ. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only identity-lifecycle fix; `NOTICE` unchanged — `src/terminal/state.rs` is already in the §4(b) index)* | **An unanswered hook exit is OWNER-SCOPED: it fences the label it was recorded for and no other.** Codex Gate-2 P2 2 on `e8f6e7e` (`msg_3e339000b75278a4`). `unanswered_exit_to_inherit` folded `self.unanswered_hook_exit` into the inherited minimum through `.map(|(_, at)| *at)`, DISCARDING the stored label, and every owner installed afterwards took it as its own `unconfirmed_since`; the terminal-wide field was cleared only when a running observation of the STORED label answered it. So after Hermes retired and Pi was detected, accepted and confirmed, Pi's next ordinary lifecycle report re-inherited the Hermes timestamp and `confirmed_hook_owner()` flipped `Some(("zynk:pi","pi"))` back to `None` — repeatedly, and with no way back, because only a Hermes observation could ever clear it. **Fix.** The pending fact is scoped end to end. `unanswered_exit_to_inherit(&self, agent_label: &str)` inherits the outgoing authority's or identity's `unconfirmed_since` only when that owner's label matches the incoming one, and the terminal-wide fence only when its STORED label matches; a different label starts unfenced, because the detector's own observation of THAT agent is its evidence. `hold_hook_owner_unconfirmed(&mut self, agent_label, observed_at)` stamps only the representations naming that owner and keeps-the-oldest only against a fence already stored under the SAME label — another owner's older instant is replaced, never relabelled. `hook_owner_has_unanswered_exit_older_than(agent_label, observed_at)` takes the label too, and both exit limbs in `set_detected_state_with_screen_signals_at` now resolve the exiting owner's label first (`exited_authority_owner` / `exited_identity_owner`) instead of re-testing the option inline. The same-owner guards from the fence's own fix are unchanged: a new session start from the SAME owner still inherits, the OLDEST pending exit still wins, and the fence still outlives its owner's retirement so a later same-owner report cannot escape it. **Accepted consequence:** a fence for an agent that never returns simply lingers, gating that one label, and it rides the handoff snapshot as it always did — `UnansweredExitSnapshot` has carried the label from the start. **Tests (RED first).** `an_old_owners_pending_exit_does_not_retaint_a_confirmed_new_owner` is Codex's reproducer with ordered detector captures and past instants: a Hermes report crosses an older exit, a later exit retires Hermes identity and session, Pi is detected, accepted and confirmed by a newer Pi observation, and an ordinary same-session Pi lifecycle report must leave `confirmed_hook_owner() == Some(("zynk:pi","pi"))` (RED on `e8f6e7e`: `None`). `a_fence_recorded_for_one_owner_never_gates_another_live_or_after_a_handoff` swaps the representations — the fence sits on a `pi` authority and the successor is the identity-only `hermes` — and asserts it live and again after an export/restore (RED on `e8f6e7e`: `None`). `a_pending_exit_still_fences_a_new_session_from_the_same_owner` is the same-owner control: a NEW Hermes session start inherits the OLDEST pending exit and only a Hermes running observation newer than it confirms the owner. `hook_retirement_round_trip_preserves_every_fence` gains a labelled-fence phase: the fence exports with its owner, re-exports byte-identically after the restore, still gates that owner, and is answered by a running observation newer than the restored boundary. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only build-identity fix; `NOTICE` unchanged)* | **The build attestation cannot survive an edit to the source it names: `build.rs` watches every TRACKED file, not just the git HEAD.** Codex Gate-2 P2 3 on `e8f6e7e` (`msg_3e339000b75278a4`). `export_build_sha` emitted `cargo:rerun-if-changed` only for the HEAD/ref paths, and an explicit list REPLACES cargo's default file watching, so the `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no` computation was simply skipped on an incremental build: after a clean build, editing a tracked Rust constant and running `cargo build --locked --bin zynk` again produced a CHANGED executable — Codex's marker string was in it — that still reported `zynk 3.1.0 (e8f6e7e…)` with no `-dirty`, and `src/remote/unix.rs:996`/`:1009` consumes exactly that line as ADR 0013 install custody (the reviewed-source requirement is stated at `:1004`). No `ZYNK_BUILD_SHA` override was set. **Fix.** `watch_tracked_sources` enumerates `git ls-files -z` from `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` and emits one `cargo:rerun-if-changed` per tracked path (1689 in this checkout), keeping the HEAD/ref watches; a path is checked for existence when emitted, and because cargo cannot stat a path that later disappears, a DELETED tracked file re-runs the script too. The dirty computation itself is unchanged — it can no longer be skipped. Git absent (a crates.io `.crate` unpack, a source tarball) still yields an empty SHA, and the remote-copy path already refuses to seed a remote from such a binary; the same now holds if the tracked set cannot be enumerated at all, because a SHA whose dirty flag cannot be kept fresh is worse than none. **Tests.** `build_sha_attestation_does_not_survive_a_source_edit` (`tests/build_script_targets.rs`, `#[ignore]`d like its ADR 0013 neighbour, so `just check` stays hermetic and fast) runs the REAL cycle in a scratch clone of the checkout at its own HEAD, one target directory throughout: warm clean build attests exactly `git rev-parse HEAD` with no `-dirty`; one tracked Rust constant is edited; the rebuild must carry the marker (proving the change compiled) AND attest `<head>-dirty`; reverting restores the clean line and drops the marker. Scratch root is `ZYNK_BUILD_SHA_CHECK_ROOT`, else `$CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, else `target/`. RED on `f210016`: the marker was in the binary and `--version` still read `zynk 3.1.0 (f210016a5f24387068f4c2bd05d2804945caae9e)`. `scripts/release_binary_audit.py` gains a third check, `check_build_attestation`: the audited binary's `--version` must attest `git rev-parse HEAD` of `--source-root`, with `-dirty` if and only if `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no` is non-empty; a binary that attests nothing in a checkout fails, and one that attests a commit while `--source-root` is not a checkout fails because nothing could verify the claim. Its unittest stays hermetic — no build — driving the check against a throwaway `git init` repository so the audit's own git calls are exercised: clean/own-commit and dirty/dirty pass; dirty-attested-as-clean (the regression), clean-attested-as-dirty, a foreign commit, a missing attestation and an unverifiable one all fail. Every existing fixture now answers `--version`. |

# LINUX-ONLY + v0.8.2 PORT LEDGER — M4 integrations + asset parity

The upstream `v0.7.1..v0.8.2` integration, hook-asset and asset-version-parity port, on branch
`port/linux-only-v0.8.2`. The licensing split of the M2 section above still applies: an upstream commit
dated before `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22) arrives under AGPL-3.0-or-later, a later one under Apache-2.0, and
only the latter appends its touched files to the `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* list.

| zynk | upstream | what / where / why / adaptations |
|---|---|---|
| *(this commit)* | `12cce7de` *(pre-relicense — no `NOTICE` entry)* | **The single 6206-line `src/integration/mod.rs` is split into upstream's ten-file layout as a pure move; no behaviour changes.** New `src/integration/{types,version,command,file_ops,env,registry,config_edit,targets,actions,tests}.rs`; `mod.rs` keeps only the 57 consts plus the 9 `mod` declarations, the 5 `pub(crate) use` re-export groups and `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;`. The fork's OWN bodies were moved into upstream's file layout — upstream's bodies were never imported — and each destination preserves the fork's relative item order, which reproduces upstream's order exactly once the twelve items the fork does not have are removed. Import style matches upstream per file so the 29 later `src/integration/**` commits keep their anchors: `registry.rs` uses `use super::env::*;` plus inline `super::CONST`/`super::Type`, everything else uses explicit `use super::{…}` lists. **Fork-only placements** (no upstream counterpart): `ZYNK_{PANE,TAB,WORKSPACE}_ID_ENV_VAR` → `env.rs` (re-exported from `mod.rs`; consumed by `src/pane.rs`), `INTEGRATION_ID_MARKER` stays in `mod.rs` beside its sibling `INTEGRATION_VERSION_MARKER` and is reached as `super::INTEGRATION_ID_MARKER`, and the three native-hook identity predicates `hook_has_herdr_residue` / `hook_is_native` / `expected_integration_id` → `registry.rs` (they are read only by `integration_status_at`). **Fork-only imports**: `env.rs` adds `use crate::config_dir::{config_dir_from_env_or_home, expand_tilde_path, home_dir};` (those three helpers left this module in the v0.7.0 port) and `tests.rs` adds `use portable_pty::CommandBuilder;` (upstream's `tests.rs` has no such test; the fork's `apply_pane_base_env_exports_zynk_socket_path` does). **NOT placed** — twelve upstream items the fork does not have, and no slot is reserved for them: the three `HERDR_*_ENV_VAR` consts (renamed), `config_dir_from_env_or_home` / `expand_tilde_path` / `home_dir` (now `crate::config_dir`), and the Linux-only-M1 casualties `integration_target_supported`, `legacy_bash_hook_path`, both `remove_legacy_bash_hook_file` arms, `windows_command_quote` and the Windows `qodercli_command_names`; consequently `config_edit.rs` drops upstream's `#[cfg(windows)] use super::file_ops::legacy_bash_hook_path;`, `targets.rs` drops `remove_legacy_bash_hook_file` from its `super::file_ops::{…}` list, and `actions.rs` drops `integration_target_supported` from its `super::registry::{…}` list. No `#[cfg(windows)]`, `#[cfg(unix)]`, `.ps1` or PowerShell branch entered the tree; the two `"powershell"` JSON **keys** in `config_edit.rs` are unchanged data-compat keys, not target branches. **Mechanical edits, three in total**: `AgentVersionRequirement`'s four fields gain `pub` (cross-module field access); `enforce_agent_version`'s two `{INSTALL_WARNING_PREFIX}` inline format captures become positional `{}` with a `super::INSTALL_WARNING_PREFIX` argument, because a path cannot be inline-captured; and rustfmt reflows a closure in `integration_specs` into a block once `pub(crate) ` widens the line. **One deviation from upstream**: `registry.rs::integration_specs` is `pub(crate)` here, where upstream deliberately leaves it private — the fork-only test `all_native_status_assets_pass_identity_gate` calls `integration_specs().len()` from `tests.rs`, which after the split is a sibling module; verified by rebuilding with it private (`error[E0425]: cannot find function integration_specs in this scope`). Every other item follows upstream's rule of `private → pub(crate)` except `actions.rs::install_target_inner`, which stays private. **Loss proof**: 321 items before and after with an empty symmetric difference; the 104 `tests` fn names and the 97 `#[test]` attributes are identical sets; the token multiset has an empty `only-in-old`; and a reflow-immune per-item body comparison reports exactly the three mechanical edits above. `cargo nextest run --locked --bin zynk integration::` reports **97 tests run, 97 passed, 2505 skipped** both before and after, and the full suite is 2908/2908. |
