13]` -> `[…; 14]`; `src/api/schema/integrations.rs` seats `Mastracode` after `Cursor`; `src/cli/integration.rs` grows the two usage strings, the parse arm, the `currently supported:` list and the two help lines; `src/agent_resume.rs` gains `("zynk:mastracode", "mastracode", Id) => ["mastracode", "--thread", <id>]` plus the `is_official_agent_source` arm. **NEW fork-authored wire characterization** (`src/api/schema/integrations.rs`, first tests in that file): `IntegrationTarget` reaches the socket through `#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]`, so every variant name is a published wire id, and the fork had **zero** coverage of it. `integration_target_wire_ids_are_stable` pins all fourteen ids in both directions; `integration_install_params_round_trip_for_mastracode` / `..._uninstall_...` pin the new variant inside its params envelope. **Tests adopted from upstream:** the five `install_mastracode_*` / `uninstall_mastracode_*` cases, with `uninstall_mastracode_removes_herdr_hooks_and_preserves_others` renamed `..._removes_zynk_hooks_...` to match the fork's six siblings and the in-test message `should have one Herdr hook` rebranded; the seven `bundled_integration_assets_report_session_refs` assertions with the two marker strings rebranded to `ZYNK_*`. **Tests the fork forces to grow:** the mastracode row in `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions` (adopted in M4-02 — this is the first integration added under it) and in the fork-only `all_native_status_assets_pass_identity_gate`, whose `assets.len() == integration_specs().len()` assertion makes the 13 -> 14 growth test-enforced as well as compile-enforced. **Red proofs, so no assertion is vacuous:** drifting the asset marker to `=2` with the const at 1 fails `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions` (`mastracode asset version must match its integration version constant`, left `Some(2)` right `Some(1)`); deleting the `# ZYNK_INTEGRATION_ID=mastracode` line fails `all_native_status_assets_pass_identity_gate` (`mastracode status asset is missing its ZYNK_INTEGRATION_ID marker`); adding `#[serde(rename = "mastra_code")]` to the variant fails both `integration_target_wire_ids_are_stable` and the install/uninstall round trips (left `String("mastra_code")` right `String("mastracode")`). All three reverted and green. **Runtime proof beyond the unit tests:** against an isolated `HOME`, `zynk integration install mastracode` writes the 0755 hook and twelve flat entries, `zynk integration status` reports `mastracode: current (v1)`, and `zynk integration uninstall mastracode` removes the hook file and empties `hooks.json` back to `{}`; driving the installed asset against a fake Unix socket with `ZYNK_ENV=1` produced exactly three requests — `pane.report_agent` with `agent_session_id` for a payload carrying `session_id`, `pane.release_agent` for `release`, and `pane.report_agent` without a session id for a payload without one — while an unknown action and `ZYNK_ENV=0` each produced none. **EXCLUDED:** upstream's two windows test hunks (`windows_supports_only_cli_hook_integrations`, `windows_does_not_offer_unsupported_integrations_even_when_commands_exist`) — `integration_target_supported` does not exist in this linux-only fork (M1 removed it; 0 hits in `src/`); `docs/next/api/herdr-api.schema.json` (private path, and the fork derives no `schemars::JsonSchema` on these types and tracks no generated schema artifact); the `IntegrationTarget::ALL` sizing hunk, which the fork does not have (upstream introduces that const in `3f809476`, our M8); and `src/detect/mod.rs` + `src/config/sound.rs`, both landed in M3-04 (`src/config/sound.rs:131` already carries `Some(Agent::Mastracode) => AgentSoundSetting::Default`). **DEFERRED, and a corrected port-sheet claim.** The M4 port sheet §2.03 marks `src/terminal/state.rs` **X, "already landed"**. That is wrong: M3-04's own ledger row above lists `src/terminal/state.rs` among the hunks it EXCLUDED and deferred to M4, and the fork has zero `mastracode` references in that file. The commit's state.rs half is a real behavioural change — `lifecycle_hook_report_replaces_persisted_session()` (`d0e33345:src/terminal/state.rs:866`) lets a fresh `zynk:mastracode` Id report REPLACE a restored `persisted_agent_session` id instead of being coerced back to it by `conflicting_same_owner_session_ref` (fork `src/terminal/state.rs:507`), plus three tests. It is held out of this commit deliberately: it narrows the fork's owner-coherence coercion on the identity path, and the fork's `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status_at` has diverged from upstream's anchor (the M3 identity work added retirement, sequencing and `hook_identity` handling around it), so it needs its own re-derivation and identity review rather than a mechanical apply. Recorded here because no other commit in the M4 plan covers `d0e33345`; without the integration half mastracode still installs, reports and resumes correctly — the gap is only that a restored thread id shadows a newly started one. Upstream `d0e33345` is 2026-07-04, before `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22), so this arrives under AGPL-3.0-or-later and **no** `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* entry is added for any touched path. **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=~/workspace/.zynk-dev-target` + isolated `HOME`/`ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_*`/`TMPDIR`):** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo build --locked` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` all clean; `cargo nextest run --bin zynk integration::` 116/116 (up from 111); `--bin zynk agent_resume::` 47/47; the full `--bin zynk` suite 2644/2644 passed, 1 skipped (up from 2636); `bun test` on both asset test files 13/13. **Isolation note for the next milestone:** four `api::server::tests::*` cases fail with `path must be shorter than SUN_LEN` when `TMPDIR` points at a long scratch path — the fork's server tests bind Unix sockets under `TMPDIR`, so an isolated temp dir must stay well inside the 108-byte `sun_path` budget. Not a regression; they pass at a short isolated path. |
| *(this commit)* | `be901fea` `60c04005` `f23e3779` *(all pre-relicense — no `NOTICE` entry)* | **opencode's `session.status` is read in its object form, a subagent's permission/question prompt projects `blocked` onto the pane without stealing the root session, and every lifecycle report is serialized behind one request chain; `OPENCODE_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 7 -> 9.** Three upstream commits land together because all three edit the same plugin asset and the third refactors the mapping the first two introduce. **`be901fea` (7 -> 8):** `stateFromSessionStatus` accepted only a bare string, so opencode's newer `{ type: "idle" | "busy" | "retry" }` payload fell through to `reportSession(sessionID)` and the pane never left its previous state; the reader now takes `typeof status === "string" ? status : status?.type` and `retry` joins the working set. Its second half fixes a real blind spot: child (task-tool) session events were dropped wholesale, so a subagent waiting on a permission prompt left the pane reading `working` forever. `permission.asked`/`question.asked` on a child now report `blocked` and `permission.replied`/`question.replied`/`question.rejected` report `working` — **state only, with no `agent_session_id`**, so the pane keeps the root session id it already holds. **`60c04005` (8 -> 9):** `request()` becomes a thin wrapper that chains onto `requestChain` and delegates to the extracted `requestOnce()`, so two reports can no longer race two sockets and land out of order; `reportState` records the session it reported under in `reportedRootSessionID`, and `session.updated` now re-reports only when the id actually changed, which stops the redundant `pane.report_agent_session` that followed every state report. **`f23e3779` (no bump):** pure refactor — the child-event switch becomes the `CHILD_EVENT_STATES` map and the status switch becomes `SESSION_STATE_BY_STATUS`; behaviour is unchanged and verified by the two tests it adds. **Adaptations:** standing renames only (`zynk-agent-state.js`, `ZynkAgentStatePlugin`, `zynk:opencode`, `ZYNK_*` env, `// ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=9`). The fork's pre-existing double-fallback rebrand artifact in this asset (`process.env.ZYNK_PANE_ID ?? process.env.ZYNK_PANE_ID`, and the parenthesized `(process.env.ZYNK_ENV ?? process.env.ZYNK_ENV) !== "1"` gate the fork added because `??` binds looser than `!==`) is **carried through unchanged** — it is load-bearing for the sibling pi asset assertions at `src/integration/assets/pi/zynk-agent-state.test.ts:83-88`, and unlike the new mastracode asset this is an existing file, not a fresh one. The rename map was proved complete before any edit: upstream `v0.7.1`'s asset passed through it diffs against the fork's file in exactly the three gate lines above and nowhere else. **EXCLUDED — the windows hunk that sits between these commits.** Upstream's asset at `f23e3779` already carries `a51a1591`'s `const socketEndpoint = process.platform === "win32" ? ...` named-pipe branch and its `net.createConnection(socketEndpoint, ...)` call site. `a51a1591` is M4-07's commit and its windows branches are a standing exclude for this linux-only fork, so the target file was reconstructed as upstream at `60c04005` with `f23e3779`'s asset patch applied on top (clean, one -3 line offset) rather than copied from `f23e3779` — the branch never entered the tree and `socketPath` is still passed straight to `net.createConnection`. **New Bun suite:** `src/integration/assets/opencode/zynk-agent-state.test.ts`, 193 lines (142 from `60c04005` + 51 from `f23e3779`), four tests. It mocks `node:net` rather than binding a real socket like the fork's shared `assets/zynk-agent-state.test.ts`, which is what lets it hold a response open and prove ordering: `serializes lifecycle reports` (a second report opens no second connection until the first is acknowledged, and `seq` increments by exactly one), `suppresses redundant same-session updates`, `reports retry status as working`, `reports child prompts without replacing the root session` (five reports, every `agent_session_id` `undefined`). Wired as the third line of the fork's `test-ts` recipe in `justfile` (the fork's name for upstream's `integration-assets-test`), so it runs under `ci` and `check`. **A corrected port-sheet claim.** The M4 sheet §6 and the M4-05 brief both require the OPENCODE row of `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions` to move 7 -> 9 in this commit. No such edit exists to make: the row adopted in M4-02 is `("opencode", OPENCODE_PLUGIN_ASSET, OPENCODE_INTEGRATION_VERSION)` — it reads the const rather than a literal, so bumping the const is the whole change and the test enforces the marker against it automatically. `src/integration/tests.rs` is therefore untouched by this commit, and no hardcoded opencode version literal exists anywhere in `src/` or `tests/`. **Red proofs, so no assertion is vacuous:** dropping the `requestChain` wrapper fails `serializes lifecycle reports`; dropping the `sessionID !== reportedRootSessionID` guard fails `suppresses redundant same-session updates`; removing the `["retry", "working"]` row fails `reports retry status as working`; reverting `stateFromSessionStatus` to string-only fails three of the four; deleting the `CHILD_EVENT_STATES` lookup fails `reports child prompts without replacing the root session`; and holding the const at 8 with the marker at 9 fails `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions` (`opencode asset version must match its integration version constant`, left `Some(9)` right `Some(8)`). All six reverted and green, the asset byte-identical to its pre-mutation state. **Runtime proof beyond the unit tests:** against an isolated `HOME`, `zynk integration install opencode` wrote the plugin byte-identically to the embedded asset and `zynk integration status` reported `opencode: current (v9)`; driving that installed file against a real Unix socket with `ZYNK_ENV=1` produced exactly six requests in order — `{type:"retry"}` and the legacy bare string `"busy"` both `working` with `agent_session_id: root`, a same-id `session.updated` suppressed entirely, a child `permission.asked` `blocked` and a child `question.replied` `working` **both with no `agent_session_id`**, `{type:"idle"}` `idle` with `agent_session_id: root`, and a different-id `session.updated` as `pane.report_agent_session` for `other` — with `seq` strictly increasing across all six, and the child `session.created` itself producing no request. **Identity invariants:** the asset reports under the `zynk:opencode` source label, which `full_lifecycle_hook_authority` (`src/detect/mod.rs:319`) already pairs with `opencode`; nothing here reads a detected agent, and the child-prompt path deliberately omits `agent_session_id` precisely so the stored session triple and the owner-coherence check keep the root session. All three upstream commits predate `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22), so they arrive under AGPL-3.0-or-later and **no** `NOTICE` §4(b) *Modified files* entry is added. **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=~/workspace/.zynk-dev-target` + isolated `HOME`/`ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_*`, short `TMPDIR` for the SUN_LEN budget noted in the row above):** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo build --locked` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` all clean; `cargo nextest run --locked --bin zynk integration::` 116/116 (unchanged — this commit adds no Rust test); the full `--bin zynk` suite 2644/2644 passed, 1 skipped; `just test-ts` 17/17 across three files (3 shared + 10 pi + 4 new opencode, up from 13). |
| *(this commit)* | `adbaae68` *(non-test hunks)* `9e4fdd61` *(both pre-relicense — no `NOTICE` entry)* | **A pi session replacement re-anchors the pane's status on the new session instead of holding the one it replaced, and omp retries a lifecycle report the socket accepted but never answered.** `adbaae68`: the pi extension threads the session-start reason onto the wire — `reportSession(sessionStartSource?)` adds `session_start_source` to `pane.report_agent_session`, and `session_start` becomes `async (event, ctx)` so it `await`s that report before `publishState(true)`; state published ahead of the acknowledgement would still be attributed to the replaced session. On the Rust side `session_start_source_allows_session_replacement` gains `("zynk:pi", "pi", Some("new" | "resume" | "fork"))` at upstream's position (after the opencode arm, before omp's), and `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` FORGETS the stale entry for the session it is about to anchor whenever the start source is one this owner may replace on. `9e4fdd61`: omp mirrors pi's retry — `sendRequestNow` splits into `sendRequestAttempt(request, timeoutMs) -> Promise<boolean>` plus an `async` wrapper that retries once at 1500 ms when the 500 ms attempt goes unanswered, so a dropped first response no longer loses the report to the serialized queue. **Bumps:** `PI_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 6 -> 7 and `OMP_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 4 -> 5, each with its asset marker in this commit (upstream moves pi 4 -> 5 and omp 4 -> 5; the fork's pi sits +2 from its own state-only rewrite). **Already present, taken as no delta:** upstream's new `forget_stale_full_lifecycle_hook_session` helper — this fork generalized the map to `stale_hook_sessions` (both hook shapes) and already carries the identical helper as `forget_stale_hook_session`, reached from `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting`; only the CALL SITE inside `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` was missing, and that is the genuine delta. `adbaae68`'s two `src/integration/tests.rs` additions (`bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions`, `outdated_integrations_detect_previous_pi_version`) landed in M4-02 and are not re-taken here. **Adaptation:** upstream gates the forget on `session_replacement_allowed || foreground_takeover_allowed`; this fork has no takeover path (`foreground_agent_confirms_different_owner_takeover` and `suppress_current_full_lifecycle_hook_authority` do not exist — `current_session_owner_conflicts` simply refuses), so the gate is `session_replacement_allowed` alone. **Identity invariants held (review of every new `state.rs` path):** the new replacement arm is a pure function of `(source, agent_label, session_start_source)` — all three come off the hook report, none from `detected_agent`; `zynk:pi`/`pi` is in `crate::detect::full_lifecycle_hook_authority`, so the arm cannot widen any session-identity-only owner. The new forget call reads only `source`/`agent_label`/`session_ref` from the report and mutates `stale_hook_sessions`; it never touches `hook_authority`, `hook_identity` or `detected_agent`, and it sits after the identity-only retirement gate, after the `replacing_identity_only_session && !process_present` check and after both owner-conflict gates — upstream's exact position. It is provably INERT for a session-identity-only owner: that branch already ends with `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting(.., Some(&session_ref))`, whose last act is the same `forget_stale_hook_session(source, agent_label, accepted_ref)`, so forget -> remember_stale(suppressed) -> forget leaves the same final state as remember_stale(suppressed) -> forget. No identity-only branch is touched. The four adopted tests assert on `terminal.hook_authority` only; their `set_detected_state(Some(Agent::Pi), AgentState::Idle)` exists solely to clear `known_agent_label_conflicts_with_detected_agent`, exactly as upstream, and never sources identity. **Red proof (both hunks, reverted then restored):** dropping the forget call fails `pi_resume_reactivates_a_previously_stale_session` at `assert!(working.is_some())` — a resumed session stays retired and every later state report for it is refused; dropping the pi arm fails `pi_session_replacement_reports_reanchor_full_lifecycle_authority` for all three reasons. Each was restored and `src/terminal/state.rs` re-verified byte-identical. **Asset proof:** normalizing upstream `adbaae68:assets/pi/…` and `9e4fdd61:assets/omp/…` through the standing rename set reproduces the fork assets exactly, leaving only the four known fork divergences — the duplicated `process.env.ZYNK_* ?? process.env.ZYNK_*` reads behind `zynkEnv`, the version marker offset, omp's fork-local placement of `parseDurationEnv` after `reportSession`, and the upstream product name in the `shouldReleaseOnSessionShutdown` release-policy comment. The fork's `ZYNK_OMP_IDLE_DEBOUNCE_MS` / `ZYNK_OMP_RETRY_GRACE_MS` names already match what the new Bun test drives. **Bun harness:** `src/integration/assets/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` takes both commits in full — the `Handler` type, `createExtensionHarness()`, `configureIntegrationEnvironment()`, `startRecordingServer(name)` and `startDroppedFirstResponseServer(name)` factorings, `ZYNK_OMP_IDLE_DEBOUNCE_MS` in the env save/restore set, the `connectionCount()` accessor, the `requestState()` helper, and three new tests (`Pi reports the session replacement source`, `Pi waits for a replacement session report before publishing state`, `Oh My Pi retries working before a queued idle state`). After the standing renames the file is byte-identical to upstream at `9e4fdd61` apart from the fork's own three-line header comment. **Fork-side test upkeep:** `pi_and_omp_extensions_restore_working_state_on_reload` anchored on the literal `pi.on("session_start", (_event, ctx)` for BOTH assets; pi's signature is now `async (event, ctx)`, so the anchor is shortened to the registration itself and the signature is asserted in the new pi test instead. Two fork-authored Rust guards cover under nextest what upstream only tests under Bun: `pi_extension_reports_the_session_start_source` (the reason reaches `session_start_source`, and the report is awaited before state is published) and `omp_extension_retries_an_unanswered_state_report` (the omp mirror of the existing pi guard). `9e4fdd61`'s `outdated_integrations_detect_previous_omp_version` is adopted with the `ZYNK_INTEGRATION_ID=omp` fixture prefix — the upstream prefix would be rejected as legacy residue by `src/integration/registry.rs`. **Version pins updated in the same commit:** `pi_asset_is_state_only_no_receiver` 6 -> 7, the Bun marker pin `// ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=6` -> `=7` with its test name, and `tests/cli_wrapper.rs` `pi: current (v6)` -> `(v7)`. **Excluded:** nothing — both commits are integration/state only, with no `.ps1`, no `cfg!(windows)` and no `docs/next` content. **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` + isolated `ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_*`/`HOME`):** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo build --locked` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` all clean; `cargo nextest run --locked --bin zynk integration::` 119/119 (up from 116); `cargo nextest run --locked --bin zynk 'terminal::state'` 132/132 (up from 128); the full suite 2961/2961 passed with 2 skipped; `just test-ts` 20/20 across three files (6 shared, up from 3, plus 10 pi and 4 opencode). |
| *(this commit)* | `a51a1591` `28b628d7` *(both pre-relicense — no `NOTICE` entry)* | **The pi/omp extension installers share one directory-provisioning helper that creates a missing `extensions/` dir when the agent dir exists, and kimi's `AskUserQuestion` tool now reports `blocked` for as long as the question is open; `PI_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 7 -> 8, `OMP` 5 -> 6, `KILO` 2 -> 3, `KIMI` 4 -> 5.** Both upstream commits are SPLIT — `a51a1591` is a Windows commit with a real portable residue, `28b628d7` ships a `.ps1` twin — and only the portable halves are taken. **`a51a1591` portable half.** `src/integration/targets.rs` gains `ensure_extension_dir(dir, agent)`: it returns early when the dir exists, creates it when its PARENT exists, and otherwise errors `"{agent} extension directory not found at {}. install {agent} first"`. `install_pi` and `install_omp` both call it, which collapses omp's duplicated create-then-recheck ladder (-23 lines) and gives pi the create behaviour omp already had — a real behaviour change for pi: with `~/.pi/agent` present but no `extensions/`, `install_pi` used to refuse and now provisions the directory. The two fork tests that pin the failure path (`install_pi_errors_when_extension_dir_missing`, `install_omp_errors_when_extension_dir_missing`) assert only the `"<agent> extension directory not found"` prefix, so the shortened message keeps them green, and both fixtures leave the parent absent so they still take the error arm. The pi and omp assets move from the named import `import { createConnection } from "node:net"` to the namespace form `import net from "node:net"` with `net.createConnection(socketPath!)` at the call site — behaviour-identical on Linux, and it aligns those two assets with the shape kilo/opencode already use. **`28b628d7` portable half.** `KIMI_HOOK_EVENTS` grows a middle `Option<&str>` tool-matcher column and goes `[(&str, &str); 9]` -> `[(&str, Option<&str>, &str); 12]`; the single unmatched `("PreToolUse", "working")` row splits into `("PreToolUse", Some(KIMI_OTHER_TOOL_MATCHER), "working")` plus `("PreToolUse", Some(KIMI_ASK_USER_QUESTION_MATCHER), "blocked")`, and `PostToolUse` / `PostToolUseFailure` on the same matcher return the pane to `working`. The two new consts are `KIMI_ASK_USER_QUESTION_MATCHER = "^AskUserQuestion$"` and `KIMI_OTHER_TOOL_MATCHER = "^(?!AskUserQuestion$).*$"`. `config_edit.rs::kimi_hook_table` takes the matcher and emits an optional `matcher = <toml basic string>` line between `event` and `command`; `build_kimi_config_with_hooks` destructures the third column. Without this, a kimi question left the pane reading `working` until the user answered, because only `PermissionRequest` mapped to `blocked`. The kimi asset itself is unchanged apart from its marker — the hook script already dispatches on the `action` argument the config hands it. **EXCLUDED (linux-only fork).** From `a51a1591`: the `socketEndpoint` named-pipe mapping (a `process.platform === "win32"` ternary that rewrites the socket path into a `\\.\pipe\` endpoint and otherwise passes `socketPath` through) and its call sites in all four JS/TS assets (the opencode instance was already held out in M4-05, and this commit leaves that asset untouched); the `registry.rs` widening of the `#[cfg(windows)]` `integration_target_supported` allow-list, which has no target here because M1 deleted that function outright — `grep -rn integration_target_supported src/ tests/` is empty, so the six-line hunk is a no-op rather than an adaptation; the three `#[cfg(windows)]` rewrites in `tests.rs` (`windows_supports_only_cli_hook_integrations` -> `_portable_integrations`, `windows_does_not_offer_unsupported_integrations_even_when_commands_exist` -> `windows_availability_excludes_unsupported_integrations`, and the `install_target` swap from `Pi` to `Hermes`), none of which exist in the fork; the two Windows named-pipe Bun test loops over `socketPlugins` and `integrations` together with the harness that serves them (`originalPlatform` / `originalCreateConnection` capture and `afterEach` restore, `captureConnectionEndpoint()`, and the `net, { createConnection, type Server }` import change that exists only to make `net.createConnection` mockable); and `docs/next/**` (4 files). From `28b628d7`: `assets/kimi/herdr-agent-state.ps1`. `windows_command_quote` is not reintroduced — M1 removed it and nothing in either commit needs it here. **Tests.** `install_pi_creates_extensions_dir_when_agent_dir_exists` is adopted verbatim from `a51a1591:src/integration/tests.rs:489-509` (it creates `~/.pi/agent`, installs, and asserts the path is `agent/extensions/<install name>` and is a file). `assert_kimi_hook` gains the matcher column and compares `hook.get("matcher")` against it, so a hook that lands with the wrong matcher no longer satisfies a same-event assertion; `install_kimi_writes_hook_and_updates_config` destructures the third column, and its `hooks.len() == KIMI_HOOK_EVENTS.len() + 1` and `install_kimi_is_idempotent_for_config_block`'s `== KIMI_HOOK_EVENTS.len()` follow the array to 13 and 12 with no literal to edit. `kimi_question_hooks_report_blocked_until_the_question_finishes` is adopted from the same commit. One portable Bun test comes across from `a51a1591`'s shared harness — upstream's `OpenCode stays disabled without the Herdr socket environment` — placed in the fork's `assets/opencode/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` rather than the shared `assets/zynk-agent-state.test.ts`, because M4-05 split opencode's Bun coverage into its own file and the shared harness is scoped to pi and omp by its header comment. **Version pins updated in the same commit:** `pi_asset_is_state_only_no_receiver` `Some(7)` -> `Some(8)` with its comment, the Bun marker pin `// ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=7` -> `=8` and its test name in `assets/pi/zynk-agent-state.test.ts`, and `tests/cli_wrapper.rs` `pi: current (v7)` -> `(v8)`. `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions` needs no edit — its rows read the consts, so the four bumps are enforced against the four asset markers automatically. **Red proofs (each patch reverted and the file restored byte-identical):** dropping the `matcher = ` emission from `kimi_hook_table` fails `install_kimi_writes_hook_and_updates_config` at `missing kimi hook for PreToolUse (Some("^(?!AskUserQuestion$).*$")) -> working`; dropping the create-parent branch from `ensure_extension_dir` fails `install_pi_creates_extensions_dir_when_agent_dir_exists` with the new error string; dropping the `ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH` gate from the opencode asset fails `stays disabled without the zynk socket environment`. **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` + isolated `ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_*`/`HOME`):** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo build --locked` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` all clean; `cargo nextest run --locked --bin zynk integration::` 121/121 (up from 119); `cargo nextest run --locked -E 'test(integration_commands_run_locally_when_server_is_missing)'` 1/1; `just test-ts` 21/21 across three files (6 shared, 10 pi, 5 opencode — up from 4). |
| *(this commit)* | `44f22116` `d3b4d5b0` `08ca7985` `73d17682` *(all pre-relicense — no `NOTICE` entry)* | **Four hook assets stop staging their payload through a temp file and read it straight from stdin, and pi stops hand-rolling an idle debounce in favour of Pi's own `agent_settled` event; no version moves — `PI` stays 8, `KIMI` 5, `QODERCLI` 2, `CURSOR` 1.** **`44f22116` (pi).** `assets/pi/zynk-agent-state.ts` loses 114 lines: the `ZYNK_PI_IDLE_DEBOUNCE_MS` / `ZYNK_PI_RETRY_GRACE_MS` duration parsing and `parseDurationEnv`, the retryable-provider-error regex with `lastAssistantMessage` / `retryableErrorMessage`, the `idleTimer` / `retryTimer` pair with `clearTimer`, `clearPendingTimers`, `clearFailureState`, `scheduleIdle` and `holdForRetry`, and the `retryHoldActive` / `failureBlocked` / `failureMessage` arms of `desiredState()`. `pi.on("agent_end", …)` is replaced by `pi.on("agent_settled", (_event, ctx) => …)`, which returns unless the runtime confirms `ctx?.isIdle?.() === true` and otherwise clears `agentActive` and publishes immediately — so a settlement raised while the agent is still busy is dropped instead of debounced, and explicit `zynk:blocked` precedence survives a settle because `desiredState()` still checks `blockedCount` first. The patch applied verbatim to the fork after the standing env-var and event-label renames; the resulting file is byte-identical to upstream's post-state modulo three pre-existing fork deviations that live outside every hunk — the marker at 8 (fork is +2 from its own state-only rewrite), the `process.env.ZYNK_ENV ?? process.env.ZYNK_ENV` read shape pinned by `assets/pi/zynk-agent-state.test.ts`, and `net.createConnection(socketPath!)` with no `socketEndpoint` named-pipe ternary (excluded in M4-07, linux-only fork). **omp is deliberately NOT swept**: it keeps `agent_end`, `scheduleIdle`, `holdForRetry` and `ZYNK_OMP_IDLE_DEBOUNCE_MS`, because upstream moves only pi onto the settled event. **`d3b4d5b0` (qodercli), `08ca7985` (kimi), `73d17682` (cursor).** All three shed the same shape: `set -eu`, the `mktemp` + `trap rm` + `cat >"$hook_input_file"` staging, the `ZYNK_ACTION` / `ZYNK_HOOK_INPUT_FILE` env handoff, the `python3 - <<'PY'` heredoc, `import random`, and the manual socket ladder with its nested try/recv/except/close. The body becomes a single-quoted `python3 -c` script that reads `json.load(sys.stdin)` (kimi takes its action from `sys.argv[1]`, passed as the trailing `"$action"`), builds the request in one `json.dumps`, and — for kimi and cursor — sends it through a `with socket.socket(...) as client` block; the id is now the deterministic `f"zynk:<agent>:{seq}"` instead of `time`+`random.randrange`. The four-line "installed by zynk / managed by … / add custom hooks beside …" header collapses to one `# managed by zynk; reinstalling the integration replaces this file.` line above the `ZYNK_INTEGRATION_ID` / `ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION` markers, and qodercli also loses its docs-URL comment block; both markers survive on every asset, so `all_native_status_assets_pass_identity_gate` and `hook_is_native` still pass. **qodercli is the one real behaviour change:** it no longer speaks the socket at all but shells out to the CLI — `command -v zynk >/dev/null 2>&1 \|\| exit 0` plus `subprocess.run(["zynk", "pane", "report-agent-session", os.environ["ZYNK_PANE_ID"], "--source", "zynk:qodercli", "--agent", "qodercli", "--agent-session-id", session_id, "--seq", str(time.time_ns())], stdin=DEVNULL, …, timeout=1, check=False)`. That argv was verified against the fork's own dispatcher (`src/cli/pane.rs:41`): the real binary accepts it and proceeds to the socket connect, while an unknown flag exits 2 with `unknown option:`. It also introduces a PATH dependency — a qodercli pane whose `PATH` lacks `zynk` silently reports nothing — which `1079fff4` (M4-15) resolves by resolving the running binary through `ZYNK_BIN_PATH`. **Fork context preserved.** The three `[ "${ZYNK_ENV:-${ZYNK_ENV:-}}" = "1" ]`-style guards are unchanged context lines in all three upstream diffs, so the fork's doubled (post-rebrand, functionally identical) form is kept rather than quietly normalised to upstream's single form; the python-side `os.environ.get(X) or os.environ.get(X)` twins disappear with the code that held them. **Tests.** `assets/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` takes `44f22116` verbatim after the module-path and `zynk:blocked` renames: `createExtensionHarness()` gains an `eventHandlers` map and a recording `events.on(event, handler)`, and the file gains `completionHandlers`, `piContext`, `requestStates` and `waitFor` plus the two upstream tests `Pi reports idle only after the agent settles` (handler set is exactly `["agent_settled"]`, `agent_end` absent, a stale settlement produces no request) and `Pi settlement preserves explicit blocked-state precedence`. The fork-authored `assets/pi/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` is adapted, not replaced: the `ZYNK_PI_IDLE_DEBOUNCE_MS` set/delete pair goes (the asset no longer reads it), the handler-set assertion becomes `agent_settled` true / `agent_end` false, a `settleAgent(fake, idle)` helper feeds the handler the `isIdle` predicate it gates on, the lifecycle test is renamed to `agent_start->working; agent_settled->idle`, and the now-impossible `retryable provider error at agent_end holds working (not idle)` becomes `a settlement while the agent is still busy does not publish idle` — same shape, same anchor, guarding the mechanism that replaced the retry hold. In `src/integration/tests.rs`, `bundled_integration_assets_report_session_refs` takes upstream's qodercli block (`ZYNK_PANE_ID`, `session_id`, `report-agent-session`, `--agent-session-id`, and negatives on `report-agent"` and `release-agent`) — which is what retires the fork's `QODERCLI_HOOK_ASSET.contains("ZYNK_HOOK_INPUT_FILE")` assertion; the `CODEX_HOOK_ASSET` twin stays, because no commit here rewrites codex — and upstream's kimi block (`source": "zynk:kimi`, `method = "pane.report_agent_session"`, `method = "pane.report_agent"`, `params["state"] = action`). Upstream's one-line pi assertion has no fork counterpart to edit (the fork's copy of that test never carried the pi handler rows), so it lands as three new lines: `PI_EXTENSION_ASSET.contains("pi.on(\"agent_settled\"")`, the matching negative on `pi.on(\"agent_end\"`, and `OMP_EXTENSION_ASSET.contains("pi.on(\"agent_end\"")` so a future sweep across both assets fails loudly. All ten cursor assertions were re-verified against the rewritten asset and all ten still hold unchanged (`ZYNK_INTEGRATION_ID=cursor`, `conversation_id`, `conversationId`, `sessionId`, `agent_session_id`, `pane.report_agent_session`, `hook_event_name`, `sessionStart`, and the negatives on `"state":` and `pane.release_agent`), so `73d17682` needs no test edit. **EXCLUDED (linux-only fork).** The `.ps1` twins of all three shell assets; no commit here carries a `cfg!(windows)` branch. **Behaviour smoke-tested end to end** (isolated scratch dir, a throwaway `AF_UNIX` server and a stub `zynk` on `PATH`): kimi `session` emits `pane.report_agent_session`, kimi `working` emits `pane.report_agent` carrying both `state` and `agent_session_id`, kimi `blocked` on a non-JSON payload still reports the state with no session id, cursor reports on `hook_event_name: sessionStart` with only `conversationId` present and stays silent on any other event, and qodercli invokes the CLI with the exact argv above and skips it entirely for an empty payload. Each asset was also checked with `sh -n` and its embedded python body with `ast.parse` (no single quote can appear inside the single-quoted `python3 -c` string). **Red proofs (each mutation reverted and the file restored byte-identical, verified with `cmp`):** reverting `assets/pi/zynk-agent-state.ts` to `dae0fd7` fails `assertion failed: PI_EXTENSION_ASSET.contains("pi.on(\"agent_settled\"")` and both new Bun tests (`6 pass 2 fail`); reverting the kimi asset fails `assertion failed: KIMI_HOOK_ASSET.contains("source\": \"zynk:kimi")`; reverting the qodercli asset fails `assertion failed: QODERCLI_HOOK_ASSET.contains("report-agent-session")`; dropping `"conversationId"` from the cursor asset's `first_text(...)` call fails `assertion failed: CURSOR_HOOK_ASSET.contains("conversationId")`. **Verification (isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` + isolated `ZYNK_HOME`/`ZYNK_SQLITE_HOME`/`ZYNK_SOCKET_PATH`/`XDG_*`):** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo build --locked` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` all clean; `cargo nextest run --locked --bin zynk integration::` 121/121 (unchanged — this commit changes assertions inside existing tests, it adds no Rust test fn); `just test-ts` 23/23 across three files (8 shared — up from 6 — 10 pi, 5 opencode). |
| *(this commit)* | `7e821ba7` + `5f1957c2` *(ASSET half; both POST-relicense — `NOTICE` §4(b) entries below)* | **omp's extension installer stops borrowing pi's config directory, and the bundled hermes plugin stops reporting lifecycle state so it reports only the session identity the fork already treats it as authoritative for.** **`7e821ba7` (omp config dir).** `omp_extension_dir()` (`src/integration/env.rs`) resolved through `config_dir_from_env_or_home(PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR_ENV_VAR, [".omp", "agent"])`, so setting `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` -- pi's own relocation variable -- pointed omp at pi's extensions directory and the two agents overwrote each other's extension. It now resolves in explicit priority order: `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` (tilde-expanded, used verbatim as the agent dir) -> `$PI_CONFIG_DIR/agent/extensions` under `$HOME` -> `~/.omp/agent/extensions`. The new const `OMP_CONFIG_DIR_ENV_VAR = "PI_CONFIG_DIR"` is deliberately NOT renamed to a `ZYNK_*` name and carries a comment saying why: it is the omp agent's OWN variable, read by omp to find its config, not part of the `ZYNK_*` env surface (ADR 0010 §7 governs zynk's own names, not third-party ones), so the extension has to land where omp will look. `install_omp()` (`src/integration/targets.rs`) additionally hard-errors before creating or writing anything when `omp_extension_dir() == pi_extension_dir()`, which is exactly the shape `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` produces for both agents; the guard runs ahead of `ensure_extension_dir` and `remove_legacy_pi_extension_from_omp_dir`, so a shared directory can no longer lose its pi extension to omp's legacy-cleanup pass. **Tests** (`src/integration/tests.rs`): `clear_integration_path_env` clears the new variable; `install_omp_uses_pi_coding_agent_dir_env` is renamed to `install_omp_uses_pi_config_dir_env` and rewritten onto `HOME` + `PI_CONFIG_DIR=custom-omp` (its old body now names the refused shared-directory case, which is the point of the fix); and the new `install_omp_refuses_shared_pi_extension_directory` sets both variables, asserts the error text and the resolved path, and asserts the pi extension survived while no omp extension was written. `integration::` goes 121 -> 122. **`5f1957c2` ASSET half (deferred here from M3-11).** M3-11 (`2be143c`) took the detect half -- hermes left `full_lifecycle_hook_authority` for `session_identity_only_integration`, so its hook reports no longer set lifecycle state and the screen manifest carries it instead -- but left the shipped plugin still emitting nine lifecycle hooks, whose `state` the fork now discards. `src/integration/assets/hermes/__init__.py` is brought to upstream's post-commit shape: the docstring becomes "report resumable session identity"; `_INTERACTIVE_PLATFORMS = {cli, tui, desktop, acp}` is added; `_session_id`, `_report`, `_working`, `_blocked` and `_idle` are deleted; and `_report_session(start_source, **kwargs)` (platform-gated, non-empty-`str` session id, emitting `pane.report_agent_session` with `agent_session_id` + `session_start_source`) plus `_session_started` (`startup`), `_session_reset` (`new`) and `_session_observed` (`resume`, `platform == "cli"` only) replace them. `register()` drops from nine hooks to exactly `{on_session_start, on_session_reset, pre_llm_call}`. Net effect: a subagent or background turn can no longer repoint the pane's identity, and a resumed CLI session recovers its id on the first turn instead of never. `_SOURCE = "zynk:hermes"`, `_AGENT`, `_base_params` and `_send` are untouched, and the marker stays `# ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=3` against `HERMES_INTEGRATION_VERSION` -- **no bump in this commit** (upstream's `4` here comes from a `_session_started` arm the fork's asset never had; `50ddc06f` takes the fork to `4` in M4-13, which must land after this row because it rewrites `_send` into `_send_session`). **The four obsolete assertions** in `bundled_integration_assets_report_session_refs` (`src/integration/tests.rs`) are deleted: they pinned `session_id = _session_id(kwargs)` and `pane.report_agent",`, both of which the asset no longer contains. Upstream deleted eight; the fork only ever had four, because the fork's asset never carried upstream's `session_start_source: startup` / `on_session_end` lines. The other six `HERMES_PLUGIN_INIT_ASSET` uses stay: the registry row, the install round-trip, the native-identity gate `all_native_status_assets_pass_identity_gate`, and the four `uninstall_hermes_*` fixtures. **New `scripts/test_hermes_integration_asset.py`** replaces them, loading the asset by path under the module id `zynk_hermes_integration` (upstream's `herdr_` prefix rebranded), stubbing `_send` and driving `register()` against a fake context: `test_reports_only_root_session_identity` pins the hook set to exactly those three and asserts that five hook invocations produce exactly two `pane.report_agent_session` calls (the subagent-platform and background-`pre_llm_call` ones are dropped), and `test_first_turn_recovers_resumed_session_identity` pins the CLI resume path. It is wired into BOTH `python3 -m unittest` lists in the `justfile` (`test` and `check`), taking the maintenance suite to ten modules, and `CLAUDE.md`'s enumeration of that list is updated to match. **`tests/zynk_receipt.rs`** keeps its five identity-only receipt tests unchanged and green (25/25, run twice): they build their reports as raw socket JSON rather than by executing the asset, and `pane.report_agent` carrying `state` + `agent_session_id` remains a live wire shape every other integration sends and a pre-`5f1957c2` installed hermes plugin keeps sending, so the identity-only routing must still accept it. Only the `report_identity_only_agent` doc comment changes, because it claimed that shape was "the call the asset makes from every hook it registers", which this commit makes false. **EXCLUDED:** `docs/next/CHANGELOG.md` and `docs/next/website/src/content/docs/integrations.mdx` from `7e821ba7` (the `docs/next/` tree is untracked in this fork), and `website/agent-detection/hermes.toml` plus the already-landed detect half from `5f1957c2`. Both upstream commits are post-`cd5ea1be`, so `NOTICE` §4(b) gains `justfile`, `scripts/test_hermes_integration_asset.py`, `src/integration/assets/hermes/__init__.py`, `src/integration/env.rs`, `src/integration/targets.rs` and `src/integration/tests.rs`. `CLAUDE.md` and `tests/zynk_receipt.rs` are not listed: both changes are fork-authored (a doc list entry and a comment) and carry no upstream code. |

| *(this commit)* | `1491b7dd` `a07871a5` `5eab32da` *(all **post-relicense** — `NOTICE` updated)* | **A codex sub-session nested inside another codex session can no longer steal the pane's identity, omp accepts a Windows-shaped session path, and pi activates only on a real TUI session.** `1491b7dd`: the codex hook asset gains two payload-keyed guards — it exits unless the `SessionStart` payload carries a non-blank `transcript_path` (an ephemeral side session persists none), and it exits when the inherited `CODEX_THREAD_ID` env var names a session other than the one in the payload. Identity therefore stays hook-payload-authoritative: both guards read the hook input and the process env, never detection. `src/pane.rs` closes the other half — `apply_pane_launch_env` now `env_remove`s `CODEX_THREAD_ID` (named by the new `CODEX_THREAD_ID_ENV_VAR` const beside `PaneLaunchEnv`) so a pane spawned from inside a codex session does not hand the outer thread id to the codex it launches. `a07871a5`: upstream's Windows fix lives entirely inside the **TypeScript** omp asset, so it ships on Linux too — `updateSessionRef` stops testing `file.startsWith("/")` and calls the new exported `isAbsoluteSessionPath()` (`path.posix.isAbsolute || path.win32.isAbsolute`, `import path from "node:path"`). Dropping it would leave the omp asset a revision behind and break the marker/const parity chain. `5eab32da`: pi's `session_start` gate moves from `ctx?.hasUI !== true` to `ctx?.mode !== "tui"` — RPC mode also reports `hasUI: true`, so mode is the reliable gate for "there is a PTY zynk can display". **omp keeps `ctx?.hasUI !== true`** (`omp_root_activation_requires_ui_context`, `omp_root_session_guard_is_instance_scoped`); the guard was deliberately not swept across both assets. Bumps, const + `# ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=` marker in this commit: CODEX `6 -> 7`, OMP `6 -> 7`, PI `8 -> 9` (fork pi runs +1 on upstream 8 from its own state-only rewrite). Hardcoded pins moved with them: `codex_v2_integration_status_is_outdated` `expected_version` `6 -> 7`, `pi_asset_is_state_only_no_receiver` `Some(8) -> Some(9)`, the Bun marker pin in the fork-authored `assets/pi/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` (`=8 -> =9`, test renamed), and `tests/cli_wrapper.rs` `pi: current (v8) -> (v9)`. Tests: `bundled_integration_assets_report_session_refs` gains `CODEX_HOOK_ASSET.contains("CODEX_THREAD_ID")` plus a pi-scoped `ctx?.mode !== "tui"` pair — the fork never carried upstream's `PI_EXTENSION_ASSET.contains("ctx?.hasUI !== true")` line, so `5eab32da`'s one-line swap is an **addition** here (positive on the new gate, negative on the old one), which keeps the omp-vs-pi regression trap explicit. The shared Bun harness `assets/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` gains `mode: "tui"` on the five pi-driving contexts and the two new upstream tests *OMP accepts POSIX and Windows session paths* and *Pi ignores RPC sessions even when UI APIs are available*; the fork's `rootSessionContext()` in `assets/pi/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` gains the same `mode: "tui"`. **Upstream's `tests/cli/hooks.rs` and `tests/cli/sessions.rs` do not exist in the fork yet** (the `tests/cli/` tree arrives with M8), so both upstream test hunks were placed in the fork's existing `tests/cli_wrapper.rs`: `run_shell_hook` is now a thin wrapper over a new `run_shell_hook_with_env(asset, args, input, extra_env)` that scrubs `CODEX_THREAD_ID` before applying the caller's env, and `codex_hook_reports_session_id_from_stdin` becomes `codex_hook_reports_persisted_root_session_and_ignores_ephemeral_or_nested_sessions` covering all four upstream cases (persisted root reports, matching inherited id still reports, null transcript is ignored, mismatched inherited id is ignored). **EXCLUDED:** the `assets/codex/*.ps1` hunk of `1491b7dd` (no `.ps1` twins in this Linux-only fork, ADR 0013) — no other Windows-path hunk exists in these three commits, since `a07871a5`'s Windows support is the portable TypeScript change itself. |
| *(this commit)* | `d742e515` *(**post-relicense** — `NOTICE` updated)* | **Installing or removing the claude hook stops reformatting the user's `~/.claude/settings.json`.** The old `install_claude`/`uninstall_claude` did a full `serde_json::Value` round-trip and wrote the result with `to_string_pretty`, which destroyed the user's indentation, key order (serde's `Map` is a `BTreeMap` — no `preserve_order` feature — so every object came back alphabetised), inline/compact layout, CRLF line endings, number and escape literals (`1e+02` → `100.0`, `a` → `a`) and the trailing newline, and churned the file on every install even when nothing changed. New fork-adopted `src/integration/claude_settings.rs` (804 lines, upstream verbatim except the one test fixture path `herdr-agent-state.sh` → `zynk-agent-state.sh`) replaces that with a two-track edit: **track A** builds the intended result as a `serde_json::Value` using the pre-existing `config_edit` helpers and returns the input byte-for-byte when it already equals the parsed original (so a repeat `zynk integration install claude` writes nothing at all), and **track B** applies the same edit surgically to the source text through `jsonc-parser`'s trivia-owning mutable CST (`CstNode::remove()` / `CstObject::append`), so every byte the edit does not touch is re-emitted unchanged. Containers whose direct children hold no newline bail out of the CST — whose `append` would inject newline+indent formatting and shred a one-line file — into a hand-rolled byte splice (`append_hooks_property_compact` / `append_session_property_compact` / `append_session_entry_compact`) that reuses the file's own inferred key/value separator and inter-item delimiter. Every emitted string then passes `verify_updated`, which re-parses it with `serde_json` and demands equality with the desired `Value`, so a splice bug surfaces as a clean `io::Error` instead of a corrupted user file. The ten install-side and ten uninstall-side `remove_hook_commands` calls collapse into one shared nine-entry `HOOK_REMOVALS` table (`SessionStart` carries `["idle", "session"]`) — set-equal to the old calls, order-irrelevant. `src/integration/targets.rs` keeps only the read/delegate/compare-and-write shell; `src/integration/mod.rs` gains `mod claude_settings;`. New dependency `jsonc-parser = { version = "0.33.1", default-features = false, features = ["cst", "serde_json"] }` (`Cargo.toml`, verbatim upstream line); its only dependency is `serde_json`, which is already a direct fork dep, so `Cargo.lock` grows by exactly the ten lines upstream's own lock grew by (the package stanza plus the `zynk` dep-list entry, same `0.33.1` checksum) and the single `libsqlite3-sys` node ADR 0006 depends on is untouched. **Behaviour changes worth knowing:** the canonical `SessionStart` entry now keeps its position instead of being removed and re-appended at the tail; a duplicate key anywhere in the document is now a hard error (`contains duplicate key "…"`) where `serde_json` silently kept the last and rewrote a deduped file, and that check runs only when an edit is actually needed; and a fresh install on a machine with no `settings.json` seeds `"{}"`, takes the compact path and writes a minified one-line file with no trailing newline where the fork used to write 2-space pretty. **Comments are still rejected, not preserved** — despite the crate name, `parse_value` is plain `serde_json::from_str` and `strict_parse_options()` turns all seven `jsonc-parser` leniencies off (`allow_comments: false` included), so a JSONC `settings.json` fails install exactly as it did before; the new fork test `install_claude_rejects_settings_with_comments_and_leaves_the_file_untouched` pins that and pins that the user's file is left byte-for-byte alone when it happens. Tests: the eight upstream `claude_settings::tests` are adopted verbatim and pass unmodified, and the fork adds `install_then_uninstall_claude_restores_original_settings_bytes` — a real `install_claude()` + `uninstall_claude()` round trip under an isolated `HOME` over a 4-space-indented, non-alphabetically-ordered fixture, asserting the untouched prefix and suffix survive byte-for-byte, that `zeta` still precedes `hooks` still precedes `alpha`, and that the file returns to its **exact original bytes** after the uninstall. The five pre-existing claude settings tests pass unchanged. **EXCLUDED:** the `remove_legacy_bash_hook_file(&hook_path)?` call that appears in upstream's post-image at `targets.rs:135`/`:554` — it is diff *context*, not part of this change, and the function does not exist in this fork (it is upstream's Windows `.sh` → `.ps1` cutover helper, dropped in M1). No version bump: `CLAUDE_INTEGRATION_VERSION` stays `7` — the hook asset is untouched, only the installer's write path changed. `Cargo.lock` is deliberately **not** added to the `NOTICE` §4(b) list: its added stanza is cargo-generated resolution metadata, not upstream code. |
| *(this commit)* | `50ddc06f` *(portable half; post-relicense — `NOTICE` §4(b))* | **Hermes reports session identity by shelling out to the zynk CLI instead of speaking the socket protocol itself, and every pane learns the path of the running binary.** `src/integration/assets/hermes/__init__.py`: `_base_params`/`_send` (raw `AF_UNIX` socket, hand-built JSON-RPC envelope, `json`/`random`/`socket` imports) are replaced by `_pane_id`/`_send_session(session_id, start_source)`, which runs `$ZYNK_BIN_PATH pane report-agent-session <pane> --source zynk:hermes --agent hermes --seq <ns> --agent-session-id <id> --session-start-source <src>` through `subprocess.run(check=False, timeout=1)` with both streams to `DEVNULL`, still swallowing every exception so a hook can never break the agent. `_report_session` and the three registered hooks (`on_session_start`, `on_session_reset`, `pre_llm_call`) are unchanged, so hermes stays session-identity-only: it reports identity, never lifecycle state, and its receipts still anchor on `hook_identity`. **`HERMES_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 3 -> 4** with the asset marker `# ZYNK_INTEGRATION_VERSION=4` in the same commit (upstream is 4 -> 5; the fork is one revision behind because `e608a751`, which took hermes 3 -> 4 upstream, is deferred past M8 — const and marker still agree, which is what `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions` enforces). **`src/integration/env.rs`:** `apply_pane_base_env` now also exports `ZYNK_BIN_PATH` from `std::env::current_exe()`, so a hook asset invokes the running build rather than whatever `zynk` a `PATH` lookup finds (previously only plugin panes got it, via `src/app/api/plugins/{panes,runtime}.rs` and `src/app/input/navigate.rs`); new `HERMES_HOME_ENV_VAR = "HERMES_HOME"` and `hermes_dir()` honours it through `expand_tilde_path` before falling back to `~/.hermes` — hermes' own variable, deliberately not renamed to a `ZYNK_*` name, exactly like `OMP_CONFIG_DIR_ENV_VAR`/`GROK_HOME_ENV_VAR`. **`src/integration/targets.rs`:** `install_cursor`/`uninstall_cursor`/`install_mastracode`/`uninstall_mastracode` drop their local `shell_single_quote` + `format!("bash {…} {action}")` and call the shared `hook_command(&hook_path, Some(action))`, which emits the byte-identical string on Linux — a de-duplication, not a behaviour change. **`src/detect/mod.rs`:** `agent_name_from_known_package_path` gains the 4-component window `["node_modules","mastracode","dist","cli"]`, so a globally npm-installed mastracode running as `node …/dist/cli.js` is identified from its package path; covered by the new `identify_agent_in_job_detects_node_wrapped_mastracode_package_cli` (Linux npm prefix, not upstream's Windows `%APPDATA%` path, whose fixture also embedded the forbidden upstream name). **Tests:** `scripts/test_hermes_integration_asset.py` now drives `_send_session`, adds `test_send_session_uses_cli_with_the_active_pane` (argv prefix `[$ZYNK_BIN_PATH, "pane", "report-agent-session", "w1:p2"]`, `check=False`) and the fork-added `test_send_session_stays_silent_outside_a_zynk_pane`; `apply_pane_base_env_exports_the_running_binary_path` and `hermes_dir_honors_the_hermes_home_override` are new; `clear_integration_path_env` now clears `HERMES_HOME` so a stray value cannot redirect an install test; `install_cursor_writes_hook_and_updates_hooks_json` and `install_mastracode_writes_hook_and_updates_hooks_json` assert exact equality against `hook_command` instead of the old prefix/suffix substring checks. **EXCLUDED (linux-only fork):** the four new `.ps1` assets (cursor, devin, grok, mastracode) and the `cfg!(windows)` `*_HOOK_INSTALL_NAME`/`*_HOOK_ASSET` selectors that reference them; the `cursor-agent.cmd` selection in `src/agent_resume.rs` and `interactive_agent_executable`; the `#[cfg(windows)]` `LOCALAPPDATA`/`USERPROFILE` branch of `hermes_dir`; the `mastracode_hook_command` and `grok_hook_command` wrappers plus their PowerShell `-EncodedCommand` bodies and the migration-only duplicate `remove_flat_command_hook` calls (on Linux each collapses to the plain `hook_command` already emitted); `integration_target_supported` and `hermes_install_layout_available`'s Windows probe, neither of which exists in this fork; every `#[cfg(windows)]` test; and `docs/next/**`. **Sheet deviation:** §2.30 said to keep the asset's `if os.name == "nt": creationflags = CREATE_NO_WINDOW` branch and its guarded test assertion; both are dropped, because M4-07 (`dae0fd7`) already set the precedent of stripping Windows branches out of the *assets* as well as the Rust — no fork asset carries a platform branch, and the guarded assertion would pin behaviour the asset deliberately no longer has. |
| *(this commit)* | `ccccda54` *(**post-relicense** — `NOTICE` updated)* | **An opencode pane now tracks the root conversation selected in ITS OWN TUI, and stops adopting the activity of clients attached to the same server; `OPENCODE_INTEGRATION_VERSION` 9 -> 10 on BOTH opencode assets.** One opencode server is shared by every client attached to it, so `session.created` and every `chat.message`/`session.status` the bundled server plugin sees are server-GLOBAL: the old asset read a root `session.created` as this pane's own new session and reported it as `session_start_source: "new"`, which let an attached client repoint the pane's identity, and a report naming another root session still moved the pane's state. **Asset half:** `assets/opencode/zynk-agent-state.js` drops the start source from `reportSession` and, on `session.created`, only records `reportedRootSessionID` locally; a NEW second asset `assets/opencode/zynk-tui-session.js` (108 lines) runs in opencode's `tui` slot, polls `api.route.current` every 100 ms and reports the selected ROOT session (`session.parentID` absent) as `pane.report_agent_session` with `session_start_source: "select"` and NO `seq`, retrying at 0/100/400/1000 ms while the same route stays selected so a selection made before zynk detects the process is not lost. **Rust half:** `session_start_source_allows_session_replacement` swaps opencode's arm `Some("new")` -> `Some("select")`, `normalize_session_start_source` accepts `"select"`, and a new `is_unsequenced_opencode_selection` exempts the TUI shape from the shared sequence fence — both plugins report under `zynk:opencode` but only the server plugin numbers its reports, so `accept_hook_report(source, None)` would refuse every selection once the server had reported once. **Second installed artifact.** `integration_specs()` keys ONE path per target, so opencode's completeness is checked from the registered plugin path: `install_opencode` also writes `zynk-tui-session.js` beside the config dir and registers `./zynk-tui-session.js` in opencode's own `tui.jsonc`; new `src/integration/opencode_config.rs` (298 lines) does that edit through the `jsonc-parser` CST that M4-12 already vendored, so the user's comments, formatting, trailing commas and existing plugin entries survive, an existing entry is never duplicated, and a `["spec", {..}]` option tuple counts as configured. `registry::integration_status_at` gains an opencode arm mirroring the existing grok one: a current server plugin beside a missing/stale/unregistered TUI plugin is `Outdated`, and `uninstall_opencode` removes both files plus the managed config entry, collecting errors so a broken `tui.jsonc` still gets the plugins deleted. Because that arm can now mark an install at the expected version `Outdated`, `zynk integration status` would have printed the nonsense `outdated (v10 < v10)`; `src/cli/integration.rs` gains upstream's guarded arm printing `needs repair (v10)` instead (it covers grok's identical case too). **IDENTITY REVIEW — every new `src/terminal/state.rs` path.** Three hunks, all in functions this fork owns; `hook_authority` is the only authority read and no path consults `effective_agent_label()`/`detected_agent` for IDENTITY. (1) The `("zynk:opencode","opencode",Some("select"))` replacement arm is a pure function of `(source, agent_label, session_start_source)`, all three off the hook report; the pair is already in `crate::detect::full_lifecycle_hook_authority`, so it cannot widen a session-identity-only owner, and it NARROWS what may repoint the pane (`new` no longer qualifies). (2) `is_unsequenced_opencode_selection` is a pure function of the four values the report itself carries and gates only `accept_hook_report`; it leaves the identity-only retirement gate, `known_agent_label_conflicts_with_detected_agent`, both owner-conflict gates and the `replacing_identity_only_session && !process_present` clamp untouched, and it never records a sequence (there is none to record), so the server plugin's own numbering is unchanged — asserted by `opencode_tui_selection_is_exempt_from_the_shared_sequence_fence`. (3) `opencode_state_report_is_cross_talk` is upstream's `opencode_cross_talk`, placed in `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status_at` BEFORE `hook_report_survives_retirement` and `accept_hook_report` so an ignored cross-talk report consumes no sequence, exactly as upstream's early `FullLifecycleHookReportRoute::Ignore`. It resolves the anchor through the existing `owned_session_ref` (`hook_authority` first, then the persisted session — which is what a TUI selection leaves behind), and reads `detected_agent` ONLY as upstream's `process_present` evidence that the agent's own process is in the foreground, the identical use `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` already makes of it; identity itself still comes off the hook. It is inert for every other owner (`(source, agent_label) != ("zynk:opencode","opencode")` returns early) and for identity-only owners, which never reach this function. No `hook_identity` branch, retirement/evidence guard or suppression-boundary path is touched. **EXCLUDED, with reasons.** `docs/next/CHANGELOG.md` (standing). The new asset's `process.platform === "win32"` named-pipe branch and its `socketEndpoint` indirection — linux-only fork, and the M4-05/M4-07 precedent already strips platform branches out of the *assets*; `net.createConnection(socketPath, ...)` matches the sibling opencode asset exactly. Upstream's `session_start_source_is_recognized` (+`"select"`) and the whole `full_lifecycle_source && unsequenced_selection` suppression stash (`SuppressedFullLifecycleHookReport::{replacement_session_ref, pending_replacement_report}`, and the `selection_can_reconcile` clearing of it) have NO fork counterpart and are not needed: upstream REFUSES a full-lifecycle session-start report while `!process_present || generation_gated || !session_anchored` and therefore has to park the selection until detection catches up, whereas this fork's `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` has no process gate on that path at all — the selection anchors immediately and detection then finds it already in place. Same end state, proved by `opencode_tui_selection_anchors_before_the_process_is_detected`, and the divergence is recorded there in a comment. This fork's suppression map is `SuppressedHookReport` (shared with `hook_identity`) plus `stale_hook_sessions`, with no replacement/pending fields, so the stash could not have been taken verbatim in any case. **Divergent upstream assertions not adopted:** upstream's `opencode_tui_selection_reanchors_full_lifecycle_authority` asserts `hook_report_sequences["herdr:opencode"] == 20` after the reanchor, because its routing returns `Ignore` before the fence records the attached client's `seq: 21`; this fork records the sequence in `accept_hook_report` before `live_full_lifecycle_hook_authority_conflicts_with_session` rejects, a pre-existing ordering difference this commit does not introduce, so the fork's adaptation asserts the behaviour that matters (the late old and late attached reports are both refused and the state stays `Working`) and drops the sequence assertion. **Tests.** `src/integration/tests.rs`: `install_opencode_writes_plugin_to_plugins_dir` grows the two new artifacts and the `tui.jsonc` entry; `uninstall_opencode_removes_plugin_when_present` is replaced by `uninstall_opencode_removes_plugins_and_managed_tui_config_entry`; new `opencode_status_requires_the_tui_plugin_and_config_entry`, `install_opencode_invalid_tui_config_does_not_write_plugins` and `uninstall_opencode_removes_plugins_when_tui_config_is_invalid`; the all-agent parity table gains an `opencode_tui` row so BOTH markers are pinned to the one const. `src/integration/opencode_config.rs` carries upstream's four CST tests (temp prefix `zynk-opencode-config-`, test-local spec literal `./zynk-tui-state.js` kept deliberately distinct from `OPENCODE_TUI_PLUGIN_SPEC`). `src/terminal/state.rs`: `opencode_new_session_ref_replaces_existing_session_ref` becomes `opencode_tui_selection_replaces_existing_session_ref`, plus upstream's `opencode_server_{new,resume}_does_not_replace_existing_session_ref` and fork-shaped `opencode_tui_selection_{is_exempt_from_the_shared_sequence_fence,anchors_before_the_process_is_detected,reanchors_full_lifecycle_authority}` and `opencode_state_report_for_another_root_session_is_ignored`. Bun: the new 181-line `assets/opencode/zynk-tui-session.test.ts` (5 tests) is wired as the fourth line of `test-ts`, and `assets/opencode/zynk-agent-state.test.ts` takes upstream's two new tests. `hook_authority_preserves_current_session_ref_when_incoming_ref_differs` still passes unchanged: it drives `zynk:opencode` with NO detected agent, so the new guard's `process_present` requirement leaves it on the old clamp path. **Red proofs, each reverted and restored, file re-verified byte-identical:** reverting the `select` arm to `new` fails 4 state tests; dropping the `!unsequenced_selection &&` fence bypass fails 3; dropping the `opencode_state_report_is_cross_talk` call fails 2; holding the const at 9 fails `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions` (`opencode asset version …`, left `Some(10)` right `Some(9)`) and holding the TUI marker at 9 fails the new row (`opencode_tui asset version …`, left `Some(9)` right `Some(10)`); dropping the registry completeness arm fails `opencode_status_requires_the_tui_plugin_and_config_entry` (left `Current`, right `Outdated`); restoring the `session.created` report fails the new Bun test `does not classify server-global root creation as a local selection`; dropping `session_start_source: "select"` from the TUI asset fails `reports a root session when only the local route changes`. **Runtime proof (isolated `HOME`, debug binary):** against a hand-written `tui.jsonc` carrying a user comment and their own `./their-plugin.js`, `zynk integration install opencode` printed all three messages, wrote both assets byte-identically, appended only `./zynk-tui-session.js` and left the comment intact; | *(this commit)* | `1079fff4` *(portable half; **post-relicense** — `NOTICE` updated)* | **An agent hook that shells out to the CLI now runs the build that spawned its pane instead of whatever `zynk` a `PATH` lookup finds, and seven integrations move a version.** `src/integration/assets/qodercli/zynk-agent-state.sh` is the one functional change on Linux: the `command -v zynk >/dev/null 2>&1 \|\| exit 0` precondition is deleted and the argv head becomes `os.environ.get("ZYNK_BIN_PATH") or "zynk"`. Those two edits are one fix — the guard made a pane whose `PATH` lacked `zynk` skip the session report in silence (the regression `d3b4d5b0` introduced in M4-08 when qodercli stopped speaking the socket), and dropping it is only safe because the argv now resolves the running binary first; when `ZYNK_BIN_PATH` is absent the call still falls back to a `PATH` lookup, and with neither present `subprocess.run` raises inside the existing `except Exception: pass`, so the hook stays silent and exits 0 exactly as the guard used to. **`ZYNK_BIN_PATH` was already reaching agent panes — nothing was re-wired.** M4-13 (`4bcafe4`) added the export to `apply_pane_base_env` (`src/integration/env.rs:52-54`) from `std::env::current_exe()`; tracing the spawn path confirms every PTY pane inherits it, because `PaneRuntime::spawn` (a thin delegate to `spawn_with_initial_history`), `spawn_with_initial_history`, `spawn_shell_command` and `spawn_argv_command` (`src/pane.rs:1395 :1426 :1464 :1498`) each call `apply_pane_launch_env` (`:112`), which calls `apply_pane_base_env` — and agent panes reach those through `TerminalRuntime::{spawn,spawn_argv_command}` and `Tab::new_with_runtime` (`src/workspace/tab.rs:127-155`, `:371`). `from_handoff_fd` needs no export: it adopts an already-spawned PTY whose env was set at first spawn. The pre-existing `apply_pane_base_env_exports_the_running_binary_path` covers the helper in isolation, so this commit adds `pane_launch_env_exports_the_running_binary_path` (`src/pane.rs`) to pin the composition through `apply_pane_launch_env` — the layer an agent pane actually goes through. **Version moves (const + asset marker in this commit, both directions guarded by `bundled_integration_asset_versions_match_expected_versions`):** CLAUDE 7 -> 8, CODEX 7 -> 8, KIMI 5 -> 6, COPILOT 2 -> 3, DROID 2 -> 3, QODERCLI 2 -> 3, ANTIGRAVITY_CLI 1 -> 2.
