| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only security fix on top of the `2696cb7` row above; `NOTICE` unchanged)* | **A pid is not an identity: the pane-tree principal is now `(pid, start time)` at BOTH ends, so a reused pid cannot inherit the pane it once rooted.** Finding `ARCH-E8-ADR14-PID-REUSE-001` (architect `msg_ddbdb2e2daf82aa8`, arbiter `msg_4e926ca4f9fe467a`), against the ADR 0014 binding this branch added in `2696cb7`. **The hole.** `caller_is_inside_pane` (`src/app/api/caller.rs`) resolved the socket peer's pid from `SO_PEERCRED` and walked `/proc/<pid>/stat`'s PPid chain until it reached the pane's stored PTY child pid; neither `caller.rs` nor `src/platform/linux.rs` read a process START TIME (the string `starttime` appeared nowhere in the tree), so a pid was treated as an identity. The kernel reuses pids. The pane's root process P can be reaped while the server still holds P as that pane's child; an unrelated same-UID process then receives pid P, forks R, and R's ordinary ancestry reaches P — so the predicate placed R INSIDE the pane, which is the pane's whole standing: reporting its identity and receipting its messages. **What was and was not shown.** The reviewer reproduced acceptance AT THE PREDICATE and stated plainly that it did not force host pid reuse and did not produce an end-to-end `received` event; a receipt additionally needs a stale identity and a delayed pane-died signal. It is recorded here as a real hole in the principal, not as a proven end-to-end exploit — the predicate IS the principal, so the hole is in the principal. Two windows existed: the pane root's pid (above) and the peer's own pid between `connect()` and the server's `/proc` read. **The fix.** `/proc/<pid>/stat` field 22 is now read: `process_parent_and_start_time` (`src/platform/linux.rs:25`) returns the PPid and the start time from ONE read, so the pid a walk stands on and the start time it compares cannot straddle the moment that pid changed hands, and `process_start_time` (`:44`) is the standalone accessor; both reuse the existing last-`)` parsing, because `comm` may contain spaces and parens. `parent_pid` is gone — it had exactly one caller and a bare parent pid is no longer a usable answer. `ProcessPrincipal { pid, start_time }` and `TreePlacement` (`src/platform/mod.rs:47`, `:58`) replace the boolean walk: `place_process_in_tree` (`:95`) verifies the pane root's start time, then the caller's, and only then walks the same bounded PPid chain as before — the walk body, its PID-1 stop, its self-parent stop and `MAX_ANCESTRY_HOPS` are unchanged, so nothing that was refused before is now accepted. **The caller's principal** is captured at accept: `stream_peer_credentials` (`src/ipc.rs:37`) reads the peer's start time immediately after `SO_PEERCRED` into the new `PeerCredentials::start_time` (`src/platform/mod.rs:36`), so the connection is bound to a process rather than to a number the kernel may hand to someone else before the request is checked. **The pane's principal** is published on the PTY path without a lock: `PaneRuntime` gains `child_start_time: Arc<AtomicU64>` (`src/pane.rs:919`) stored with `Release` BEFORE the pid (`:1740`) and read with `Acquire` AFTER it (`child_start_time`, `:2525`), so a reader that has seen a pid is guaranteed to see the start time belonging to it; `0` means never captured, which refuses. **Fail-closed set**, all one F4 `caller_outside_pane` with the reason in the message (the socket `ErrorBody` carries only `code` and `message`), as new `CallerRejection` variants (`src/app/api/caller.rs:32`) decided by the pure, injectable `place_caller_in_pane_tree` (`:113`): the pane root has no captured start time; the pane root is gone from `/proc` (reaped — nothing can be inside a tree whose root no longer exists); the pane root's pid now holds another process; the caller's start time was unreadable at accept; the caller's pid no longer holds the process that connected; any failed `/proc` read; the hop bound. **Live handoff.** The handed-over pane keeps its child process, so it keeps that process's start time: `HandoffRuntimeState` carries `child_start_time` (`src/handoff_runtime.rs:11`, `#[serde(default)]`), and an importer that receives `0` from a server predating the field re-reads it from the still-live child (`src/pane.rs:1590`) rather than let the upgrade a live handoff exists to perform leave every pane unidentifiable. **`SO_PEERPIDFD` is NOT adopted**: it needs Linux >= 6.5 and this fork's users include Ubuntu 22.04 on 5.15, so it would fail closed on a supported platform; the ADR amendment records it as the future strengthening, with that kernel floor as the reason, and names the residual window (each hop of the walk is read at a different instant, with both endpoints pinned). **RED first.** On base `1a76040` the reviewer's numbers — root 4242 reaped and reused, its new child 4243 connecting — were ACCEPTED by the pid-only predicate (`a reused pane-root pid must not be inheritable`), and `process_start_time` did not compile because the platform layer could not state when a process started. **Tests.** `a_reused_pane_root_pid_is_not_the_pane_it_once_rooted` (the reviewer's shape, refused, plus the matching-start-time half still accepted), `a_reaped_pane_root_fails_closed`, `a_caller_pid_that_changed_hands_is_refused`, `a_caller_that_has_exited_is_refused`, `the_decision_names_every_fail_closed_shape` (all nine outcomes through the injected process table), `every_refusal_names_the_pane_and_the_decision`; the six pre-existing walk tests are carried over onto the principal API; real-`/proc` accessor tests in `src/platform/linux.rs`; handoff-state serde tests pinning both the carried value and the absent-field default. The real-socket ADR 0014 controls stay green unchanged, and two of them are now load-bearing for the start time: `an_identity_report_from_outside_the_pane_is_refused` and `live_handoff_keeps_the_pane_tree_binding` assert the refusal says the CALLER is outside rather than that the pane lost its principal, so a binding that silently degraded to unidentifiable would refuse everyone and could no longer read as a pass. **Verification** (isolated target + isolated `ZYNK_*`/`XDG_*`): `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean; full `cargo nextest run --locked --bin zynk` 2693 tests (2692 passed, 1 pre-existing ignored) against 2682 on the base, the +11 being this fix's own; `--test zynk_receipt --test live_handoff --test zynk_native_cli` 82/82; `just check` and `just gate` clean. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only custody fix on top of the `1a76040` row above; `src/build_sha.rs` is fork-authored and `src/main.rs` is already listed, so `NOTICE` is unchanged)* | **A remote binary that is merely REUSED must satisfy the same custody as one that is installed, and a dirty or malformed attestation is now refused at both ends.** Gate-3 B1 warden R15 finding 1 and the custody half of finding 2 (`msg_23ae1d2c0f40332c`; evidence `msg_e60980952feed121`, correction `msg_52d3885020209a5a`). **The hole.** `prepare_remote_zynk` (`src/remote/unix.rs:326-350`) had two early returns that accepted an EXISTING remote binary whenever `remote_binary_matches` was true — a predicate that compared only the self-reported version and protocol — and returned `installed_or_replaced: false` without ever calling `local_install_custody` or `verify_remote_custody`. Custody therefore ran on the install path alone (`:369-372`), so a foreign binary already sitting on the remote host was run by this client on its own say-so. The warden drove the exact `e8f6e7e` release binary with a fake ssh returning Linux/x86_64 and a remote path `/opt/foreign/zynk` reporting version `3.1.0`, protocol 14 and an all-ones 40-hex build SHA: the reuse branch accepted it and the command trace showed NO `sha256sum` and no installer before the bridge started. That SHA is WELL-FORMED hex, so shape validation alone would not have caught it — only comparing it against the local canonical SHA does. Separately, nothing anywhere refused a `-dirty` or malformed attestation (`grep -n dirty src/remote/unix.rs src/build_info.rs` was empty), so a dirty local build could seed a remote and a dirty remote binary was accepted — exactly what ADR 0013 Decision 3 custody exists to prevent. **The rule.** The reviewed local binary is read BEFORE either decision, because reusing a binary is as much a decision to RUN it as installing one is: `custody_source_path` names the reference (`ZYNK_REMOTE_BINARY` when set, else the running executable — the reference even for a package-manager-managed install that cannot itself seed a remote, so reuse stays available to it) and `local_install_custody` refuses it outright unless it attests a well-formed, non-dirty commit. `remote_binary_is_the_reviewed_one` then gates both early returns on `sha256` + source SHA + version + protocol, logging both hashes and both SHAs with `tracing::info!`; a refusal is NOT fatal — it falls through to the existing confirmation-and-install path, which is unchanged. **One comparator, one round trip.** `remote_custody_probe` asks for `sha256sum`, `--version` and `status client --json` in a single ssh round trip, and `check_remote_custody` — now returning a typed `CustodyRefusal` rather than an `io::Error`, so a reuse refusal cannot be propagated as a fatal error by accident — is the ONLY place that decides whether a remote binary is the reviewed one. It keeps its ordering (bytes first, so a substituted binary fails on what it IS rather than on what it says), then requires the attestation to be well-formed and clean before comparing it, then version, then protocol. `verify_remote_custody` runs that same comparator after the copy, REPLACING the version-only re-check at the old `:373-379`, which was both weaker than custody and a second probe a swap could slip between; `remote_binary_matches` is deleted with it. A fail-closed guard refuses to copy an install source that is not the file custody was taken of. **Build input.** `ZYNK_BUILD_SHA` was accepted verbatim by `build.rs::export_build_sha`, so any string could be attested. New `src/build_sha.rs::attested_sha_problem` (40 lowercase hex, no `-dirty`) is compiled by BOTH `build.rs` (`#[path]` module, plus its own `rerun-if-changed`, since the explicit watch list replaces cargo's default) and the binary, so the build input and the custody boundary cannot disagree; a non-empty malformed value now panics the build naming ADR 0013. An EMPTY value keeps its meaning — no attestation, refused later by the remote path, not a build failure — and the git-derived `<sha>-dirty` still compiles, because a dirty build must build; it just cannot authorise a remote host. **Nothing weakens:** `installed_or_replaced` still reports whether a copy happened, both confirmation prompts are untouched, and the Linux x86_64 restriction from `628dcc4` stands. **RED on `c2ba298`:** the warden's all-ones probe was REUSED by `remote_binary_matches`; `local_install_custody` returned `InstallCustody { build_sha: "aaaa…aaaa-dirty" }` and `{ build_sha: "not-a-sha" }`; `check_remote_custody` accepted a matching pair of dirty attestations and a `0.0.1-foreign` version. **Tests** (+12): reuse refused for a foreign source SHA, for different bytes and for a foreign protocol, reused for the matching control, and not reused when the probe fails; dirty and malformed refused locally and remotely; the post-copy comparator refusing a foreign version, a foreign protocol and a missing status line; and the build-input validator over empty, short, long, uppercase, non-hex and `-dirty` values. The four pre-existing remote-custody tests carry onto the typed refusal and onto full 40-hex attestations. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `f210016` row above; `NOTICE` unchanged)* | **The unanswered-exit fence is keyed by the FULL owner — hook source plus agent label — not by the label alone, so one source's pending exit can no longer gate another source that reports the same agent.** Gate-3 B1 warden R15 (`msg_e60980952feed121`), refining fix #20 (`f210016`). **Why the label was not enough.** #20 scoped the terminal-level `unanswered_hook_exit` to the `agent_label` it was recorded for, which closed the cross-AGENT case. But `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status_at` does not require the reporting source to be in the `full_lifecycle_hook_authority` allowlist, so two owners can hold the same agent label under different sources — `zynk:hermes` and some other source both reporting `hermes` — and a label-only key let the first owner's unanswered exit be inherited as the second owner's `unconfirmed_since`. That is exactly the bug #20 fixed, one level finer: the successor was installed already fenced and `confirmed_hook_owner()` answered `None`, so it could anchor no receipt. **The type.** `unanswered_hook_exit` is now `Option<(HookOwner, Instant)>` (`src/terminal/state.rs:458`) with `HookOwner { source: Option<String>, agent_label: String }` (`:310`) — a named struct rather than a `(String, String, Instant)` triple, because the pair is stored, snapshotted and matched as ONE thing and the legacy no-source case needs a rule of its own, which `HookOwner::matches` (`:327`) states: a fence with no recorded source matches its label under ANY source, exactly the reach the server that wrote it had. **The predicates** `unanswered_exit_to_inherit` (`:1052`), `hold_hook_owner_unconfirmed` (`:1078`) and `hook_owner_has_unanswered_exit_older_than` (`:1123`) take `(source, agent_label)` as two borrowed parameters rather than a constructed `HookOwner`, because every call site already holds both fields and a report should not allocate to ask a question; the owned struct is for storage. Both exit limbs already resolved their owner, so they now resolve `(source, agent_label)` and pass both through — the authority limb from `authority.source`/`authority.agent_label`, the identity limb from the identity's own pair — and the two live representations are filtered on both fields as well. `confirm_pending_hook_owner` still clears the fence on a running observation of the agent it names, because the DETECTOR observes a process, not a source. **The snapshot** `UnansweredExitSnapshot` (`:287`) gains `source: Option<String>` with `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]`: a snapshot from a server that keyed by label alone deserializes with `None` and restores a fence that gates its label under any source — narrowing it to a source that server never recorded would invent evidence, and dropping it would open a window that server did not have — and it re-exports without inventing one. Every #20 guarantee stands: same-owner inheritance, oldest-fence-wins within one owner, and the fence outliving its owner's retirement. **RED on `19cf9c4`:** with a `zynk:pi`/`pi` fence in place, an `other:pi`/`pi` owner's `confirmed_hook_owner()` was `None` where it must be `Some(("other:pi", "pi"))`. **Tests** (+4): `a_fence_recorded_for_one_source_never_gates_another_source_with_the_same_label` (the RED case, plus the control that the fence is not discarded), `a_delayed_pi_exit_does_not_gate_a_confirmed_codex_owner` (the reviewer's own probe shape end to end), `a_handoff_snapshot_round_trips_the_fence_source_with_its_label`, and `a_fence_from_a_snapshot_without_a_source_still_gates_its_label` (JSON without the field, restored, gating two different sources of its own label and no other label, then re-exported). Four existing fence assertions now compare the full owner. **Also in this commit:** ADR 0014's first `Amendment 2026-09-09` section gains one appended bullet making the third of the reviewer's three requirements explicit — only a row recorded AFTER migration 0004 has run, by a build that performs the pane-tree origin check, may carry `pane_tree`; a row that predates it can only ever be `integration`. Decision 2 and every existing bullet are untouched, and the section stays append-only. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only, TEST-ONLY fix; no production behaviour change; `NOTICE` unchanged)* | **A git test fixture can no longer write into an outer repository: every fixture git command runs with the inherited git environment scrubbed, containment is asserted before any identity write, and the write names its config file explicitly.** Gate-3 B1 arbiter test-custody finding (`msg_8b73579479220603`). **The defect.** Fixture helpers created a temp repo and then set an identity with `git -C <path> config user.email zynk@example.invalid` / `user.name "Zynk Test"`. `git config` WALKS UP to the nearest parent repository when `<path>` is not itself one, so any fixture whose `git init` did not take — or whose root happens to sit inside a checkout — wrote those values into the REAL repository's `.git/config`. It happened twice in this program's shared checkout, each time right after a `just check` run made inside it, and every later commit there without an explicit identity was then authored `Zynk Test <zynk@example.invalid>`. **The trigger, reproduced.** With `GIT_DIR=<outer>/.git` INHERITED in the environment, `git init` inside the child exits 0 while leaving `child/.git` ABSENT — git initialises the directory `GIT_DIR` names — after which `git -C child config user.email …` also exits 0 and writes into the OUTER repository. Every status code is success, so nothing in the fixture notices. Reproducing the exact BASE command sequence in an isolated throwaway repo: `git init exit=0`, `fixture/.git present? NO`, both `config` writes `exit=0`, and the outer `.git/config` gained `[user] email = zynk@example.invalid`. A sanitised caller environment hides this, so the fix cannot rely on the caller having one. **Three layers, all kept.** `scrub_git_env` `env_remove`s `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`, `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` and `GIT_DISCOVERY_ACROSS_FILESYSTEM` and pins `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`/`GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` to `/dev/null`, so the shape cannot arise and a fixture also stops reading the operator's global config (it was inheriting `commit.gpgsign = true`, so fixture commits were being signed with the operator's key). `set_repo_identity` asserts `<path>/.git` EXISTS before writing anything — a failed or skipped init aborts the test instead of escaping — and then writes with `git config --file <gitdir>/config`, which cannot walk up at all; when `.git` is a FILE (a linked worktree or a submodule) the real gitdir is resolved with `rev-parse --absolute-git-dir` first. **Hardened in place, not refactored** (deliberately: a shared module would be production-adjacent churn in a test-only commit), so five near-identical helper copies now carry the same treatment — `src/workspace/git/test_support.rs:60`/`:82` (shared by `discovery.rs` and `status.rs`), `src/worktree.rs:450`/`:472`, `src/app/worktrees.rs:807`/`:829`, `src/app/api/worktrees.rs:787`/`:809` and `tests/cli_wrapper.rs:46`/`:68` — covering all seven identity call sites, including the two `--ref-format=reftable` fixtures whose bare `Command::new("git")` init is now scrubbed too. `src/cli/plugin.rs`'s `run_git` is production code that writes no identity and is out of scope here. **Test.** `a_fixture_git_command_cannot_reach_an_outer_repository` (`src/workspace/git/test_support.rs:129`) builds a throwaway outer repository, sets `GIT_DIR` at it for the process (removed again by a `Drop` guard so a failing assertion cannot leak it), and then checks all three layers: `git init` really initialises the child rather than the directory `GIT_DIR` names; `set_repo_identity` on a directory whose init was skipped PANICS with the containment message rather than succeeding; the contained write lands in the child's own config; and the outer repository's config is byte-identical throughout. With `set_repo_identity` temporarily reverted to the BASE write form the test fails exactly as described — "a config write into an uninitialised fixture must abort the test" — so it is not vacuous. |

## B1 handoff corrections (2026-09-09)

### Fixture containment and disk-backed tests

- Follow-up to `1f70d7e` / `ARB-E8-FIXTURE-CONTAINMENT-001`: the four non-identity-writing Rust fixtures now scrub inherited Git routing/configuration and assert their own `.git` before any write. The empty-commit fixture cannot mutate a parent repository. Reuse is test-only via `workspace::scrub_git_env`; production Git behavior is unchanged.
- The five identity helpers resolve linked worktrees with `rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir`, then write the common config explicitly. The regression checks both the effective identity and the absence of an ignored per-worktree config.
- Six Python fixture helpers in five maintenance modules share `scripts/git_test_support.py`: clean Git environment, verified init and explicit config path. Validator child processes use the same environment. The ignored build-attestation checkout test also isolates clone, config and build subprocesses.
- RED: the actual Python release-audit helper moved an outer HEAD and left the fixture uninitialized; the Rust empty-commit probe moved the outer HEAD; the linked-worktree probe read the old common identity. GREEN: all three regressions, 163 targeted Rust tests and 56 affected Python tests.
- Backport the test-only root support from port commit `40b6028` (all sixteen harnesses, unchanged suffixes and fallback). `ZYNK_TEST_ROOT` permits a short real disk-backed directory; `TMPDIR` still controls unit/maintenance temp files. No runtime installation or production path changes.
- Verification on the combined working tree: `just check` passed 3054 Rust tests (4 skipped), 9 TypeScript tests and 94 maintenance cases, with isolated build/runtime directories and a disk-backed test root. Implementation is pending co-author verification; remaining B1 identity and vendoring findings are not closed by this entry.

### Fixture query environment

- Follow-up to `d1c3373`: the license tracked-file query and release-audit test consumers use the fixture Git environment too, so an inherited `GIT_DIR` cannot make a read-only assertion inspect the wrong repository. Production runners are unchanged. Both maintenance modules pass all 28 cases under a hostile `GIT_DIR`; ordinary `just check` passes 3054 Rust tests (4 skipped), 9 TypeScript tests and 94 maintenance cases.
- The broader hostile-environment probe is **NOT GREEN**: production worktree runners still create refs and worktree registrations in the explicitly named sentinel when ambient Git routing overrides their repository argument. This is recorded separately as `PROD-GIT-ROUTING-001`, including the analogous message `git_meta` provenance risk. Its scheduling requires a separate scope decision; the test-only fixture correction does not claim to fix it or treat it as an accepted production behavior.

### Captured exits, receipts and handoff

- `ARCH-E8-PENDING-EXIT-RECEIPT-001` and `ARCH-E8-HANDOFF-LOST-EXIT-001`: the detector records each process exit in its runtime before awaiting bounded queue space. Receipt resolution refuses while an exit is pending; application acknowledges only the matching owner label and capture timestamp after the state transition. A single awaited producer bounds the pending list to the channel capacity plus its blocked send. Snapshot capture projects pending exits through a copy of the existing pure terminal state before exporting retirement, retaining capture-time ordering without consuming events or mutating the live state on rollback. No protocol, DB, peer-principal, or suppression policy change.
- `ARCH-E8-PROVISIONAL-FULL-LIFECYCLE-001`: the remaining defect was in both detector producers, not the state consumer (the inherited consumer already confirms before ignoring screen state). An actual same-agent process probe now publishes confirmation while a hook owns lifecycle; cached presence, misses and release-suppressed probes cannot generate it. The hook-reported status still wins.
- RED: the production publisher blocked behind a full queue while the App receipt gate still accepted its owner, and actual snapshot capture restored a confirmed identity despite that pending exit. A separate producer regression failed because the unchanged-agent probe emitted nothing. GREEN: these regressions, matching-only acknowledgement and restart controls; 945 targeted state/app/persist/pane tests, 85 receipt/native-CLI/live-handoff tests, and `just check` (3058 Rust passed, 4 skipped; 9 TS; 94 maintenance). The capture test covers both retirement and the provisional newer-report shape. Implementation remains pending co-author verification.

### Source archive validation and replacement

- `INSPECTOR-E8-001` / `INSPECTOR-E8-002`: validate the complete archive namespace before extraction, regardless of `tarfile` filter availability. Reject parent path components, duplicate members and any member beneath an archive link. Allow symlink/hardlink aliases only to a regular member in the same source root; directory aliases and link chains are deliberately unsupported (the currently tracked vendor tree contains no links). Safe file aliases remain covered on filtered and unfiltered paths.
- Extract and copy to a staging directory beside the destination before moving the old tree. Rename the prepared tree into place, restoring the previous tree on an install failure; if rollback itself fails, retain the backup and report its location rather than cleaning it up. Resolve source metadata before replacement too. No vendored files or dependency pins changed.

- `INSPECTOR-CB-001` / `ARB-CB-FIXTURE-GIT-CONFIG-001`: the first reftable initializer now uses the fixture scrub and checks its own `.git`. ### Initializer sidecar race found by the required check

- `CB-DB-INIT-SIDECAR-001`: `named_sessions_use_separate_servers_and_workspace_state` failed during the build-attestation check: beta saw alpha's 512-byte initialization journal beside a zero-byte main DB and aborted with `db_orphan_sidecar`. This is a pre-existing opener race, not a build-attestation regression or a timing-only test flake. Route that length-based condition through the existing hot-journal init-lock wait and recheck, before opening any SQLite connection. Other errors, including sidecar links, remain immediate refusals; a lock error retains the original sidecar diagnosis as context.
- RED with a held init lock and a 512-byte journal: the second opener returned instead of waiting. GREEN: it waits without touching bytes, initializes when the first writer removes its temporary journal, and still refuses unchanged orphan data when the journal survives lock release. Existing orphan, symlink, foreign-DB and lock controls remain. No migration, checksum or foreign-database policy changed. The startup failure and failed full-check log remain attributed to the dirty build-attestation tree that exposed it.

### Failed Git queries cannot attest clean source

- `WARDEN-CB-BUILD-ATTESTATION-FAILOPEN-001`: distinguish a successful empty Git response from a failed query in `build.rs`. A status failure now emits an empty source attestation and a warning, never the clean HEAD; malformed or missing HEAD also cannot attest. The existing local/remote custody checks refuse missing attestations. Explicit build-SHA validation and source watches are unchanged.
- RED by compiling and running the actual build script with a Git wrapper whose status command exits 42: changed source incorrectly retained a clean SHA. GREEN controls cover clean, dirty, failed status, failed HEAD, and recovery to clean. This regression uses docs mode to exercise the actual script without Zig; the existing ignored warm binary clean/edit/revert test remains the Cargo invalidation control. Targeted build/custody tests: 65 passed. Full check including the separately recorded initializer fix: 3,062 Rust passed, 4 skipped; 9 TS and 103 Python passed. These are dirty-tree verification counts, not an approval of either commit.

### Imported identity is provisional until the new detector confirms it

- `ARCH-CB549-POSTSNAPSHOT-EXIT-001`: exits after snapshot capture cannot be present in that snapshot. The handoff restore now puts the transferred hook identity behind a separate owner-scoped import-confirmation boundary; only matching running evidence captured strictly after import clears it. A hook report cannot confirm itself. Existing oldest-unanswered-exit and retirement rules are unchanged, full lifecycle authority is still not restored, and cold restore ignores the handoff payload as before. No wire/schema/protocol change; another handoff creates its own confirmation boundary.
- RED: the real delayed-importer test kills a foreground Hermes child after capture and obtains `received` from its surviving pane shell, with the real SO_PEERCRED check and no peer-trust bypass. After the fix it gets `receiver_identity_unverified` and the message stays submitted. Positive control: a still-live agent retains the same session and becomes receipt-capable from the new detector, without another hook report; the stored proof is `pane_tree`. Restore/state tests pin old/equal observations, exits, different agents, hook self-confirmation and repeated imports. Focused terminal/app/persist/receipt/CLI/handoff suites: 929 passed. The first positive-test assertion incorrectly looked for `pane_tree` in the CLI response; the corrected test queries its actual home, the stored delivery event, and passes on both sides. Both actual RED failures and that harness correction are retained in the verification record.

Correct the test, not that production clamp: without a detected process replacement is refused; an observation at the import boundary establishes presence and permits the new session, but cannot grant receipt authority; strictly newer evidence then confirms it. This changes test setup/assertions only.

### Remote custody bound to the executed file

- `WARDEN-CB-REMOTE-CUSTODY-TOCTOU-001`: the descriptor is opened inside the REMOTE shell, not the local SSH client. Reuse and post-copy probes hash that descriptor before running any metadata command; all prepared commands and each bridge connection recheck the expected digest and execute the held inode. Live handoff passes the holder's `/proc/<pid>/fd/3` path while that shell remains alive. A bridge bootstraps its daemon from `/proc/self/exe`, not its mutable or deleted install pathname. Local source hashing and version inspection likewise share an open file. Existing source-SHA/version/protocol comparison stays mandatory.
- RED: actual generated shell commands ran substituted bytes after hashing on reuse and post-copy; a later bridge reopened a changed pathname, and local custody reopened a substituted source. GREEN: these regressions, pre-execution hash mismatch, failed hash query (no binary runs), quoted paths/session names, and handoff from a child with fd 3 closed. A real ELF bootstrap test compares the daemon's device/inode through its isolated socket peer PID to the held image, with no peer-trust seam. Reverting only the daemon spawn to `current_exe()` makes it fail with a missing deleted pathname; restoring the fix passes. The initial test used a pathname-filtered PID scanner that could not see the copied/deleted image; that harness failure and a subsequent borrow-check error are retained, not counted as product failures or passing runs. Targeted remote/autodetect/bootstrap suite: 82 passed.
- Linux x86_64 scope is unchanged. Executable procfs access is an explicit runtime prerequisite with a fail-closed diagnostic. This closes atomic pathname replacement, not hostile in-place inode writes, a compromised SSH account/kernel/tool, or legacy pre-install compatibility discovery. Those trust boundaries are documented in `DEVELOPMENT.md`. No live remote host, operator server or installation was used for the probes. Implemented/pending review, not approval.

The [Kitty keyboard protocol, Key codes](https://sw.kovidgoyal.net/kitty/keyboard-protocol/#key-codes) permits `key:shifted:base` and `key::base`; both are positive controls, not malformed inputs. The base-layout slot is validated without adding a wire field or changing layout-based binding policy. Nonnumeric, non-scalar, signed and excess components are rejected; release events still discard associated text as required by the existing key model.

  the core's immutable borrow remains sufficient. No bindings/vendor rewrite.
  OSC12 and its query/reset meaning are documented in
  [XTerm Operating System Commands](https://invisible-island.net/xterm/ctlseqs/ctlseqs.html#h3-Operating-System-Commands).
- Controls cover explicit/host/child fallback, cursor reset, every split of
  BEL/ST queries, ordered OSC10/11/12 replies, no queued-input duplicate, and
  the actual RGB/no-value accessor ABI. Work is per received color query,
  not added to pane-scaled rendering or detection. Existing modified-file
  notices/NOTICE entries cover all three source files.
- Protocol19, all M5 carry-forwards and the held M6-06 identity design remain
  unchanged. **IMPLEMENTED / PENDING VERIFICATION** by Gate2/Gate3; no merge,
  installation or release is implied.

### M6-05: Basic-Key Compatibility Under Kitty (2026-09-11)

- **Source:** `f4d216b63d010987775dc6eaf92afa72f86f2816`. Take the pane-side
  encoder's REPORT_ALL_KEYS bit and compatibility decisions, replace the old
  unmodified-Enter release expectation, and retain the Ctrl-Backspace positive
  control. Exclude the Windows VTI mapper/record controls, not their Linux
  counterparts. No Windows input mapper, host flag streaming or wire change.
- Without report-all, unmodified Enter/Tab/Backspace press/repeat use their
  legacy bytes and release stays silent. With report-all, basic key events
  are encoded, with repeat/release distinguished when event reporting is set.
  Modified basic keys keep their existing CSI-u controls. This follows the
  [Kitty progressive enhancement contract](https://sw.kovidgoyal.net/kitty/keyboard-protocol/#progressive-enhancement).
- Three behavioral reds cover the fallback encoder and real pane wrapper.
  The pane repeat failure also closes with the upstream fallback delta:
  `encoded_key_preserves_event_kind` sends that case to the fallback; no new
  runtime dispatch policy was needed. Pane report-all controls parse the
  valid vendor framing, while fallback controls assert exact bytes. Explicit
  committed-text precedence and M5's release-text guards remain unchanged.
- This is not complete printable-key report-all or host negotiation support:
  the scoped `e7fc85bf`/`cfa112e7` ordering prerequisites remain M6-17, with host
  work still M8. Protocol19 and D-M5-6 to M9 are unchanged. Both source files
  already carry modified-file notices and NOTICE entries.
- **IMPLEMENTED / PENDING VERIFICATION** by Gate2/Gate3, no deployment implied.

### M6-08: Legacy Alt, Shift and Control Composition (2026-09-11)

- **Sources:** `e98c49658caf054f2cfdc0f52831f29d59ed6fba`,
  `39ba6b7a0242768b7d3684e8163d13a0609fcaa3`,

  subsumes `39ba6b7a` by parsing the inner single key before adding Alt, keeping
  both uppercase Shift and control-byte semantics. The upstream changelog
  sentence is accounted for here, not copied as a second release changelog.

  survive. Remove only Alt from the owned key, preserving its private source
  without widening KeySource visibility or synthesizing a replacement key.
  Decode 0x1f canonically as Ctrl-underscore; M6-02's Ctrl-slash alias remains.
- Five independent behavioral red controls cover shifted punctuation, uppercase
  Alt letters, Alt control composition, the 0x1f key identity and real Kitty
  pane bytes from raw Alt-Ctrl input. Positives include all 32 prefixed control
  bytes, Tab/Enter/Escape special cases, lower/non-ASCII characters, non-US
  shifted pairs, plain Alt punctuation, release silence and explicit associated
  text. The existing IME source distinction and strict alternate grammar remain
  intact; no generated-text dispatch or binding policy changed.
- No host negotiation, protocol19 change, new platform path or dependency.
  All three source files already carry modified-file notices and NOTICE entries.
- **IMPLEMENTED / PENDING VERIFICATION** by Gate2/Gate3, no deployment implied.

### M6-13: Bidirectional PTY Progress (2026-09-11)

- **Source:** `ef85fa0c7ebb48bb59fe7593af5a67f3e02ff3d4`, adapted to the
  Linux-only fd/actor modules without reintroducing platform cfg gates.
  Remove the nested 50ms write-ready wait; dispatch readable output before
  writable input. During handoff drain, poll both directions and the wake fd
  until the existing deadline; refuse read EOF instead of timing out as if
  the peer were only blocked. Partial-write offset and queue order stay intact.
- `poll_pty_and_wake` keeps one deadline across EINTR, clears revents before
  retries and preserves zero/infinite timeout behavior. The no-longer-used
  write-only poll helper is removed. This follows the interruption, timeout
  and readiness contract in [Linux poll(2)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/poll.2.html).
- Permanent behavioral reds: the running actor must drain 512KiB output while
  input is blocked; a handoff peer refuses input until its output callback
  fires; read EOF with pending input is BrokenPipe, not TimedOut; thread-local
  signal interruptions must not restart a 100ms poll budget. The latter took
  about 1.1s before the fix. Timing thresholds are bounded fixture controls,
  not production-latency claims. Signals target only the test pthread; the
  sender joins before the original mask and handler are restored.
- Positive controls retain exact partial-write suffix/FIFO bytes, zero-timeout
  readiness and infinite-wait wake behavior. Peer workers are joined before
  outcome assertions; existing quiesce/rollback and response-order controls
  remain. TestChannel fixtures are not replaced by PTYs. No process detector,
  identity, receipt, protocol 19, dependency or M6-06 policy change.
- fd.rs receives its first modified-file notice and NOTICE entry. Actor
  attribution already existed. **IMPLEMENTED / PENDING VERIFICATION** by
  Gate2/Gate3; no deployment implied.

### M6-14: Pane Size Replies and Empty Clipboard (2026-09-11)

- **Sources:** `169a4fd90c20a00aec77d684ecec98b9f0f996cc` and
  `471041690af928d9a4d4cda8e4963cc7dcb3d6a8`. Register the existing vendored
  size callback, initialize row/column geometry, and update the reported size
  only after a successful terminal resize. Preserve raw unknown pixel sizes
  for query refusal rather than reporting the emulator's minimum-one fallback.
  Seed imported runtime geometry before replay/reader startup.
- The ABI already exists in vendor include/ghostty/vt/terminal.h and the Rust
  bindings; neither changes. Query/reply geometry follows the
  [xterm XTWINOPS reference](https://invisible-island.net/xterm/ctlseqs/ctlseqs.html)
  for CSI 14/16/18 t. Controls pin every split of a mixed size/status query
  stream, changed geometry, rejected zero-row/column resize preserving the
  last valid size, and unknown pixel geometry remaining silent.
- Empty clipboard requests return success without creating a queued write;
  unsupported content, size bounds and nonempty writes retain the previous
  policy. BEL/ST and callback controls pin the empty case. A socket-backed
  imported-runtime control asserts first-query geometry without another
  resize and exactly one nonempty ClipboardWrite event; it does not assert a
  live remote handoff, process identity, or caller authority.
- Six behavioral reds precede implementation. The additional runtime fixture
  first failed compilation because its Duration import was missing; that
  exit101 is retained separately from the corrected missing-reply red.
  Existing in-band reports, host appearance and response-order behavior stay
  intact. No protocol 19, vendor, dependency, detection or M6-06 policy change.
  All four source files already carry modified-file notices/NOTICE entries.
- The first whole check found the fork's old clear-acceptance expectation in
  pane/osc.rs (exit100, later tests cancelled). Adapt that test to the same
  intentionally changed empty-write policy, covering both selectors and
  terminators at every split plus a later nonempty write. This additional
  file changes tests only; no OSC parser or callback policy is changed again.
- Vendor verification preserves its harness failures: ReleaseFast failed a
  Debug-safety compile assertion; non-SIMD Debug compiler processes segfaulted.
  The unchanged suite passed in Debug with default SIMD (4873 pass/50 skip),
  and both original non-SIMD module commands passed with the documented LLVM
  backend (4869 pass/54 skip). No vendor assertion or test filter was changed;
  the compiler's internal defect is not claimed fixed. Retained author evidence
  also discloses the debugger failure and removal of a help-created local cache.
- **IMPLEMENTED / PENDING VERIFICATION** by Gate2/Gate3; no deployment implied.
